.

In order to define the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators on the global sections of vector bundles, we also need maps

\displaystyle\pi^{*}

\displaystyle:H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0-}_{1,n_{1}+1,n_{2},n_{3}+1,m},p^{*}_{2}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\longrightarrow H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0-}_{1,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{1}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\pi^{*}

\displaystyle:H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0-}_{1,n_{1},n_{2}+1,n_{3}+1,m},p^{*}_{2}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\longrightarrow H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0-}_{1,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{1}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right).

Suppose that
\underline{\varepsilon}_{A}
(resp.
\underline{\varepsilon}_{A/C}
) is a basis of
\underline{\omega}_{A}
(resp.
\underline{\omega}_{A/C}
) compatible with
\alpha_{p}
(resp.
\alpha^{\prime}_{p}
). We have
\underline{\varepsilon}_{A}=(\pi^{*}\underline{\omega}_{A/C})g
with

g=\begin{cases}h\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&1\\
&&p^{-1}\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)h^{\prime},&\bullet=+,\,j=2,\\
h\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&1\\
&&1\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)h^{\prime},&\bullet=+,\,j=3,\\
h\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}\\
&p^{-1}\\
&&p^{-1}\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)h^{\prime},&\bullet=-,\,j=1,\end{cases}

for some
h,h^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\ast&p^{n}\ast&p^{n}\ast\\
\ast&\ast&p\ast\\
\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&&&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. In all the cases,
g^{-1}
belongs to the semi-group

\Delta_{+}=\left\{\left(h\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}\\
&a_{2}\\
&&a_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right)h^{\prime},a_{4}\right)\,:\,h,h^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\ast&p\ast&p\ast\\
\ast&\ast&p\ast\\
\ast&\ast&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\GL_{3}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),\,a^{-1}_{1}a_{2},a^{-1}_{2}a_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right\}.

We make
\Delta_{+}
act on
W_{\underline{t}}
by

\left(h\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}\\
&a_{2}\\
&&a_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right)h^{\prime},a_{4}\right)\cdot q(\underline{x},y)=q\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-v_{p}(a_{1})}\\
&p^{-v_{p}(a_{2})}\\
&&p^{-v_{p}(a_{3})}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\underline{x}h\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}\\
&a_{2}\\
&&a_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right)h^{\prime},a_{4}p^{-v_{p}(a_{4})y}\right),

and define
\pi^{*}
as

\pi^{*}\vec{f}(A,\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p},C,\underline{\varepsilon}_{A})=\vec{f}(A,\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p},C,\underline{\varepsilon}_{A},(\pi^{*}\underline{\varepsilon}_{A/C})g)=g^{-1}\cdot\vec{f}(A,\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p},C,\underline{\varepsilon}_{A/C}).

The
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators are defined as

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Up}{{{}}U^{+}_{p,2}}

\displaystyle:H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0}_{n_{1}+1,n_{2},n_{3}+1,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{*}_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0+}_{2,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{2}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi^{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0+}_{2,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{1}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\xrightarrow{p^{-2}\mathrm{Tr}\,p_{1}}H^{0}(\mathscr{T}^{0}_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},\omega_{\underline{t}}),

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Up}{{{}}U^{+}_{p,3}}

\displaystyle:H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0}_{n_{1},n_{2}+1,n_{3}+1,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{*}_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0+}_{3,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{2}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi^{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0+}_{3,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{1}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\xrightarrow{p^{-3}\mathrm{Tr}\,p_{1}}H^{0}(\mathscr{T}^{0}_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},\omega_{\underline{t}}),

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Up}{{{}}U^{-}_{p,1}}

\displaystyle:H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0}_{n_{1},n_{2}+1,n_{3}+1,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{*}_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0-}_{1,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{2}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi^{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{0-}_{1,n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},p^{*}_{1}\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)\xrightarrow{p^{-2}\mathrm{Tr}\,p_{1}}H^{0}(\mathscr{T}^{0}_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3},m},\omega_{\underline{t}}).

Similarly as
V_{n,m}
, one can check that these
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators preserve the spaces
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)
and
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
. It is also easy to see that
U^{+}_{p,2}U^{+}_{p,3}U^{-}_{p,1}
maps
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)
(resp.
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
) into
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n-1,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\right)
(resp.
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n-1,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n-1,m}}\right)
),
i.e.
the operator
U^{+}_{p,2}U^{+}_{p,3}U^{-}_{p,1}
increases the level.

2.8.3.
Adelic

If we identify
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\GL_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
via (
2.4.1
), the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators defined above acting on classical automorphic forms on
G
of weight
(t^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{2}.t^{+}_{3};t^{-}_{1})
corresponds to the following adelic operators (up to the action of the center of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
):

(2.8.7)

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Up}{{{}}U^{+}_{p,2}}:

\displaystyle p^{\left<(t^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{2}.t^{+}_{3};-t^{-}_{1})+2\rho_{\mathrm{c}},(1,1,0;0)\right>}\int_{N(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}R\left(u\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p\\
&p\\
&&1\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),1\right)\,du;

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Up}{{{}}U^{+}_{p,3}}:

\displaystyle p^{\left<(t^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{2}.t^{+}_{3};-t^{-}_{1})+2\rho_{\mathrm{c}},(1,1,1;0)\right>}\int_{N(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}R\left(u\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p\\
&p\\
&&p\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),1\right)\,du;

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Up}{{{}}U^{-}_{p,1}}:

\displaystyle p^{\left<(t^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{2}.t^{+}_{3};-t^{-}_{1})+2\rho_{\mathrm{c}},(0,0,0;-1)\right>}\int_{N(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}R\left(u\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&1\\
&&1\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right),1\right)\,du;

where
N(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&1&\ast&\ast\\
&&1&\ast\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\right\}
,
R(-)
denotes the right translation, and
2\rho_{\mathrm{c}}=(2,0,-2;0)
is the sum of the compact positive roots of
G
.

2.9.
Statement of main theorem

Let
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\circ}
be the connected component of
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
containing
\mathbf{1}_{2}
,
i.e.
,
T_{\mathrm{so}}(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and put
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Lambdaso}{{{}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\circ}\rrbracket
.

Theorem 2.9.1
.

The following hold:

(1)

Let
U_{p}=U^{+}_{p,2}U^{+}_{p,3}U^{-}_{p,1}
. Then for each
f\in\mathpzc{V}
,
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
converges, and we can define the semi-ordinary projector as
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{eso}{{{}}e_{\mathrm{so}}}=\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}
. The
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-modules

\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}^{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right),\quad\mathpzc{V}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V},\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)

are both free of finite rank over
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
.

(2)

The spaces of
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-families of tame level
K^{p}_{f}
are defined as

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cM}{{{}}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}}=\Hom_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\left(\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so}},\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}\right),\quad\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so}}=\Hom_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\left(\mathpzc{V}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{so}},\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}\right).

For a given weight
(\tau^{+},\tau^{-})\in\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),F^{\times}\right)
, let
\mathcal{P}_{\tau^{+},\tau^{-}}
be the ideal in
\mathcal{O}_{F}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
generated by
\{(x,y)-\tau^{+}(x)\tau^{-}(y)\colon(x,y)\in T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\}
. Then for
0\geq t^{+}\geq-t^{-}+4
, we have

(2.9.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\mathcal{O}_{F}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket/\mathcal{P}_{\tau^{+},\tau^{-}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\right)[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}],

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\mathcal{O}_{F}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket/\mathcal{P}_{\tau^{+},\tau^{-}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}V_{n,m}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\right)[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}].

The semi-ordinary projector preserves the spaces of classical forms, and by combining (
2.9.1
) with (
2.7.2
), we have the embeddings

\displaystyle e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{1}_{p,n},\epsilon^{+},\epsilon^{-};F\right)

\displaystyle\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\left(\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\mathcal{O}_{F}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket/\mathcal{P}_{\tau^{+},\tau^{-}}\right)[1/p],

\displaystyle e_{\mathrm{so}}M_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{1}_{p,n},\epsilon^{+},\epsilon^{-};F\right)

\displaystyle\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\left(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\mathcal{O}_{F}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket/\mathcal{P}_{\tau^{+},\tau^{-}}\right)[1/p],

where
t^{\pm}=\tau_{\mathrm{alg}}^{\pm}
and
\epsilon^{\pm}=\tau_{\rm f}^{\pm}
.

(3)

Given
0\geq t^{+}
, there exists
A\geq-t^{+}+4

such that the above embedding for the cuspidal forms is surjective if
t^{-}\geq A
.

(4)

There is the following so-called
fundamental exact sequence
(in the study of Klingen Eisenstein congruences),

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so}}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{g\in(G^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\ltimes P^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathbb{Q},f}))\backslash G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathbb{Q},f})/K^{p}_{f}}\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{M00}{{{}}M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket\rightarrow 0,

where
K^{\prime}_{f,g}\subset G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathbb{Q},f})
is defined in (
4.2.1
), and
M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denotes the space of classical automorphic forms on
G^{\prime}
of weight
(0,0)
and level
K^{\prime}_{f,g}
.

Remark 2.9.2
.

In general, one can consider semi-ordinary families whose members have weight
\underline{t}=(t^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{2},t^{+}_{3};t^{-}_{1})
with
t^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{2}
fixed and
t^{+}_{3},t^{-}_{1}
varying in the family. We only consider the case
t^{+}_{1}=t^{+}_{2}=0
because it suffices for proving Theorem
8.2.1
, and we want to avoid having the main idea obscured by the extra complications of working with vector bundles. For the general case, instead of considering the global sections of the structure sheaf over the Igusa tower, one considers the global sections of a vector bundle
\omega_{\underline{t}}
. For defining
\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
(cf. §
4.3
) such that the quotient
\mathpzc{V}/\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
has a nice structure, besides the requirement of vanishing outside the strata labeled by cusp labels in
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, one also requires that the elements are global sections of
\omega^{\flat}_{\underline{t}}\subset\omega_{\underline{t}}
with
\omega^{\flat}_{\underline{t}}
a subsheaf defined as in
[
Hsi14a
, Section 4.1]
.

3.
The proof of Theorem
2.9.1
for the cuspidal part

In this section, we prove Theorem
2.9.1
for cuspidal families. The results for cuspidal families will be used to deduce the results for non-cuspidal families in §
4

Proposition 3.0.1

(Base change property)
.

Let
\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}[1/E]
the open subscheme of
\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}
where
E
, our fixed lift of Hasse invariant viewed as a section over
\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}
, is nonvanishing. For any classical dominant weight
\underline{t}
, the natural map

H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}[1/E],\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}}\right)\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Over
\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}
, we have the exact sequence of sheaves

0\rightarrow\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{m}}}{{\rightarrow}}\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}}\rightarrow\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\rightarrow 0.

By
[
Lan18
, Thm. 8.2.1]
,
R^{1}_{\pi_{n},*}(\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}})=0
(where
\pi_{n}
is the projection
\pi_{n}:\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}^{\min}_{n}
),
so we have the exact sequence of sheaves

0\rightarrow\pi_{n,*}(\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{m}}}{{\rightarrow}}\pi_{n,*}(\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}})\rightarrow\pi_{n,*}(\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}})\rightarrow 0

over
\mathscr{T}^{\min}_{n}
. Since
\mathscr{T}^{\min}_{n}[1/E]
is affine by definition, taking global sections gives the result.
∎

Proposition 3.0.2
.

Let
U_{p}=U^{+}_{p,2}U^{+}_{p,3}U^{-}_{p,1}
. Let
f\in\mathpzc{V}^{0}
and
\vec{f}\in H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
. Then both limits
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
and
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}\vec{f}
converge.

Proof.

Consider
\tilde{\mathscr{T}}_{n}
, the ordinary locus of level

\tilde{K}^{n}_{p,1}=\left\{g\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\mid g^{+}\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&1&\ast&\ast\\
&&1&\ast\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n}\right\}.

Let
\tilde{\mathscr{T}}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}
be the partial toroidal compactification of
\tilde{\mathscr{T}}_{n}
, and put
\tilde{V}_{n,m}^{0}=H^{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\tilde{\mathscr{T}}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}})
. The definition of the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators in §
2.8
can be naturally extended to
\tilde{V}^{0}_{n,m}
, so it suffices to show that
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
converges for every
f\in\tilde{V}^{0}_{n,m}
. As explained in
[
Hid02
]
,
\varprojlim\limits_{m}\varinjlim\limits_{n}\tilde{V}^{0}_{n,m}
is
p
-torsion free and there is an embedding

\bigoplus_{\underline{t}\text{ dominant}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{tor}},\underline{\omega}_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\right)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\hookrightarrow\left(\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}\tilde{V}^{0}_{n,m}\right)[1/p].

The
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{tor}},\underline{\omega}_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\right)
are free of finite rank and stable under the action of
U_{p}
, so the limit
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}
exists on them. Since

(3.0.1)

\left(\bigoplus_{\underline{t}\text{ dominant}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{tor}},\underline{\omega}_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{tor}}}\right)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\right)\cap\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}\tilde{V}^{0}_{n,m}

is dense in
\varprojlim\limits_{m}\varinjlim\limits_{n}\tilde{V}^{0}_{n,m}
by
[
Hid02
]
, the convergence of
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
for every
f\in\tilde{V}^{0}_{n,m}
follows.

On the other hand, let
\vec{f}\in H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
. By Proposition
3.0.1
,
\vec{f}
lifts to

\vec{\mathfrak{f}}\in H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}[1/E],\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}}\right).

For
l\gg 0
, we have
\vec{\mathfrak{f}}E^{l}\in H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}}\right)
, which by the Koecher principle can be viewed as an element in the finite dimensional space
M^{0}_{\underline{t}+lt_{E}(p-1)}(K^{p}_{f}K^{0}_{p,n};\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Thus the limit
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}(\vec{\mathfrak{f}}E^{l})
converges. Since
E\equiv 1\mod p
, we have
\vec{\mathfrak{f}}E^{l}\equiv\vec{f}\mod p^{m}
, and the convergence of
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}\vec{f}
follows.
∎

Thanks to the convergence of
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}
on
\mathpzc{V}^{0}
and
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
, we can define the semi-ordinary projector on them as
e_{\mathrm{so}}=\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}
.

Proposition 3.0.3
.

For
n\geq m
and a dominant weight
(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})
, the map

e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)\rightarrow e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}[t^{+},t^{-}]

induced by (
2.7.1
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Given
\vec{f}
, we have

(3.0.2)

(U^{n}_{p}\vec{f})(A,\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p},\underline{\varepsilon}_{\alpha_{p}})=p^{-(2+3+3)n}\sum_{C}\left({\vphantom{\!u}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!u^{-1}_{C}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}\\
&p^{n}\\
&&p^{2n}\end{smallmatrix}\right),p^{2n}\right)\cdot\vec{f}(A/C,\lambda^{\prime},i^{\prime},(\alpha^{p})^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}_{p},\underline{\varepsilon}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}).

Here
C
runs over the subgroups of
A[p^{4n}]
which can be spanned as

\displaystyle(e^{+}_{1},e^{+}_{2},e^{+}_{3},e^{+}_{4})\cdot p^{n}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&u_{1}&\ast\\
&1&u_{2}&\ast\\
&&1&\ast\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-n}&&&\\
&p^{-n}&&\\
&&p^{-2n}&\\
&&&p^{-4n}\end{smallmatrix}\right),

\displaystyle(e^{-}_{1},e^{-}_{2},e^{-}_{3},e^{-}_{4})\cdot p^{n}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&1\\
&-u_{2}&1&-u_{1}\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-2n}&&\\
&p^{-5n}\\
&&p^{-4n}\\
&&&p^{-5n}\end{smallmatrix}\right),

with
e^{\pm}_{1},e^{\pm}_{2},e^{\pm}_{3},e^{\pm}_{4}
a basis of
A[p^{n}]
compatible with
\alpha_{p}
. For such a
C
,
u_{C}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&u_{1}\\
&1&u_{2}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. By our definition of the
\Delta_{+}
-action on
W_{\underline{t}}
, we know that if
w\in W_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}
is a vector of non-highest weight, then

\displaystyle\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}\\
&p^{n}\\
&&p^{2n}\end{smallmatrix}\right),p^{2n}\right)\cdot w\equiv 0\mod p^{n}.

Therefore, from (
3.0.2
) we see that
U^{n}_{p}\vec{f}
is actually determined by the projection of its values to the highest weight space.

On one hand, this shows that the map in the statement is injective. On the other hand, given
f\in e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}[t^{+},t^{-}]
, we can define
\vec{f}
by the rule

(3.0.3)

\vec{f}(A,\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p},\underline{\varepsilon}_{\alpha_{p}})=p^{-(2+3+3)n}\sum_{C}\left({\vphantom{\!u}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!u^{-1}_{C},1\right)\cdot w_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}f(A/C,\lambda^{\prime},i^{\prime},(\alpha^{p})^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}_{p}),

where
C
runs over the same range as above. One can check that
\vec{f}
satisfies

\vec{f}(A,\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p}\circ g,\underline{\varepsilon}_{\alpha_{p}\circ g})=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\ast&p\ast&\\
\ast&\ast\\
u_{1}&u_{2}&a_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right),a_{2}\right)^{-1}\cdot\vec{f}(A,\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p},\underline{\varepsilon}_{\alpha_{p}})

for
g^{+}\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\ast&\ast&u_{1}&\ast\\
p\ast&\ast&u_{2}&\ast\\
&&a_{1}&\ast\\
&&&a^{-1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n}
, so (
3.0.3
) defines an element in
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
, and it is easy to check its semi-ordinarity from the semi-ordinarity of
f
. The composition of (
3.0.3
) and the map in the statement is
U^{n}_{p}
. Hence, the map is a bijection.
∎

Proposition 3.0.4
.

e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)=e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m}}\right)
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the fact that
U_{p}
maps the space
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
into
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n-1,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
. (See §
2.8.2
.)
∎

Proposition 3.0.5
.

For a fixed tame level
K^{p}_{f}
and a fixed integer
B\geq 0
, the dimension of
e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{\underline{t}}(K^{p}_{f}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is uniformly bounded for all
t^{+}_{1}\geq t^{+}_{2}\geq t^{+}_{3}\geq-t^{-}_{1}+4
with
t^{+}_{1}-t^{+}_{2}\leq B
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\Pi
is an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
G(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by a semi-ordinary form
\varphi\in e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{\underline{t}}\left(K^{p}_{f}K^{0}_{p,n};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)
whose (generalized) eigenvalue of
U^{+}_{p,2}
(resp.
U^{+}_{p,3}
,
U^{-}_{p,1}
) is
\lambda^{+}_{2}
(resp.
\lambda^{+}_{3}
,
\lambda^{-}_{1}
). The semi-ordinarity condition implies that
\lambda^{+}_{2},\lambda^{+}_{3},\lambda^{-}_{1}
are all
p
-adic units. We view
\Pi_{p}
as an irreducible representation of
\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
via (
2.4.1
). Let
Q
be the parabolic subgroup
\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\ast&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
\ast&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&&\ast&\ast\\
&&&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right\}\subset\GL_{4}
with Levi factorization
Q=M_{Q}N_{Q}
. Let

\Pi_{p,N_{Q}}=\Pi_{p}/\{\Pi_{p}(u)v-v\,:\,v\in\Pi_{p},\,u\in N_{Q}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\},

the Jacquet module of
\Pi_{p}
with respect to the parabolic
Q
acted on by
M_{Q}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cong\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\GL_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\GL_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. It follows from Jacquet’s Lemma (see
[
Cas95
, Theorem 4.1.2, Proposition 4.1.4]
) that
\Pi^{M_{Q}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K^{0}_{p,n}}_{p,N_{Q}}
equals the image under the natural projection of

\displaystyle\bigcap_{r\geq 1}\left(U_{p,\mathrm{loc}}\right)^{r}\Pi^{K^{0}_{p,n}}_{p},

\displaystyle U_{p,\mathrm{loc}}=\int_{N_{Q}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\Pi_{p}\left(u\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{2}\\
&p^{2}\\
&&p^{\phantom{2}}\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\,du.

The semi-ordinary form
\varphi\in\Pi
is fixed by
K^{0}_{p,n}
and belongs to a generalized eigenspace of
U_{p,\mathrm{loc}}
with a nonzero eigenvalue. Thus, we deduce that
\Pi^{M_{Q}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K^{0}_{p,n}}_{p,N_{Q}}\neq 0
and
\Pi_{p}
is isomorphic to a subquotient of

\Ind^{\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{Q(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\sigma\boxtimes\chi\boxtimes\chi^{\prime},

where
\sigma
is an irreducible smooth admissible representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and
\chi,\chi^{\prime}
are smooth characters of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}
. We have
M_{Q}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K^{0}_{p,1}\cong K^{\prime}_{p}\times(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with
K^{\prime}_{p}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
p\ast&\ast\end{pmatrix}\right\}\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. If
\sigma
is supercuspidal,
\Pi^{M_{Q}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K^{0}_{p,n}}_{p,N_{Q}}\neq 0
implies that
\sigma^{K^{\prime}_{p}}\neq 0
(
[
Cas95
, Theorem 6.3.5]
). However, a supercuspidal representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
does not contain any nonzero vector fixed by
K^{\prime}_{p}
. Therefore, we see that
\sigma
is not supercuspidal, and
\Pi_{p}
is isomorphic to a subquotient of a principal series.
Denote by
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4}
the evaluations at
p
of the corresponding characters of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}
. Then, up to reordering, we have

(3.0.4)

\displaystyle\lambda^{+}_{2}

\displaystyle=p^{t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{1}\cdot p^{t^{+}_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{2},

(3.0.5)

\displaystyle\lambda^{+}_{3}

\displaystyle=p^{t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{1}\cdot p^{t^{+}_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{2}\cdot p^{t^{+}_{3}-\frac{3}{2}}\alpha_{3},

(3.0.6)

\displaystyle\lambda^{-}_{1}

\displaystyle=p^{t^{-}_{1}-\frac{3}{2}}\alpha^{-1}_{4}.

Now, in addition to
U^{+}_{p,2},U^{+}_{p,3},U^{-}_{p,1}
, we also consider the action of the operator

(3.0.7)

U^{+}_{p,1}=p^{\left<(t^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{2},t^{+}_{3};-t^{-}_{1})+2\rho_{\mathrm{c}},(1,0,0;0)\right>}\int_{N(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}R\left(u\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p\\
&1\\
&&1\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),1\right)\,du

on
M^{0}_{\underline{t}}\left(K^{p}_{f}K^{0}_{p,n};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)
. The operator
U^{+}_{p,1}
has a geometric interpretation analogous to (
2.8.5
), and the above normalization makes all its eigenvalues
p
-integral. If
\alpha^{-1}_{1}\alpha_{2}\neq p^{\pm 1}
, then
p^{t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{1}
and
p^{t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{2}
are both eigenvalues for the action of
U^{+}_{p,1}
on the holomorphic forms in
\Pi
of level
K^{p}_{f}K^{0}_{p,n}
, so

(3.0.8)

\displaystyle v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{1})+t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

\displaystyle v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{2})+t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\geq 0.

By using that (
3.0.4
) is a
p
-adic unit and our condition
0\leq t^{+}_{1}-t^{+}_{2}\leq B
,

(3.0.9)

\displaystyle(v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{1})+t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2})+(v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{2})+t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2})

\displaystyle=t^{+}_{1}-t^{+}_{2}+1\leq B+1.

Combining (
3.0.8
) and (
3.0.9
), we get

(3.0.10)

0\leq v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{j}+t^{+}_{1}+\frac{1}{2})\leq B+1,\quad j=1,2.

If
\alpha^{-1}_{1}\alpha_{2}=p^{\pm 1}
, then (
3.0.4
) being a
p
-adic unit implies that

\frac{t^{+}_{1}-t^{+}_{2}}{2}\leq v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{j})\leq\frac{t^{+}_{1}-t^{+}_{2}}{2}+1,\quad j=1,2.

Combining this with our condition
0\leq t^{+}_{1}-t^{+}_{2}\leq B
, we get

(3.0.11)

0\leq v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{j})\leq\frac{B}{2}+1,\quad j=1,2.

Therefore, all the semi-ordinary forms in
e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{\underline{t}}(K^{p}_{f}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
t^{+}_{1}\geq t^{+}_{2}\geq t^{+}_{3}\geq-t^{-}_{1}+4
,
t^{+}_{1}-t^{+}_{2}\leq B
, have slope
\leq B
for the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operator
U^{+}_{p,1}U^{+}_{p,2}U^{+}_{p,3}U^{-}_{p,1}
.

Recall that the theory of Coleman families for unitray groups (developed in
[
Bra16
]
as a generalization of
[
AIP15
]
)
shows that for every point in the weight space
\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}((\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p})^{4},\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{p})
, there exists a neighborhood
\mathcal{U}
of that point and a projective
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{U}}
-module of finite rank interpolating all the cuspidal overconvergent forms of weights in
\mathcal{U}
and
U^{+}_{p,1}U^{+}_{p,2}U^{+}_{p,3}U^{-}_{p,1}
-slope
\leq B
and a fixed tame level. Since all the algebraic weights
\underline{t}
are contained in a compact subset of
\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}((\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p})^{4},\mathbb{C}_{p})
, when
\underline{t}
varies among all the algebraic weights, there is a uniform bound on the dimension of the space of cuspidal overconvergent forms of weight
\underline{t}
and slopes
\leq B
and tame level
K^{p}_{f}
. The Proposition follows.
∎

Proposition 3.0.6
.

(1)

\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\,e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1}}\right)<\infty
.

(2)

(Classicity) There is a canonical embedding

e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{1}_{p,0};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\left(\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}[t^{+},t^{-}]\right)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},

and given
0\geq t^{+}
, there exists
A(t^{+})\geq-t^{+}+4
such that if
t^{-}\geq A(t^{+})
then the embedding is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(1) Suppose that
\overline{f}_{1},\dots,\overline{f}_{d}\in e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1}}\right)
are linearly independent. By Proposition
3.0.1
, they lift to
f_{1},\dots,f_{d}\in H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}[1/E],\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}}\right)
. Recall that
E
has scalar weight
t_{E}(p-1)
and let
\underline{t}+lk_{E}(p-1)=(t^{+}_{1}+lt_{E}(p-1),t^{+}_{2}+lt_{E}(p-1),t^{+}_{3}+lt_{E}(p-1);t^{-}_{1}+lt_{E}(p-1))
. For
l\gg 0
, we have

f_{1}E^{l},\dots,f_{d}E^{l}\in H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n},\omega_{\underline{t}+lt_{E}(p-1)}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n}}\right).

Because
E\equiv 1\mod p
, we have
\overline{e_{\mathrm{so}}(f_{j}E^{l})}=\overline{f}_{j}
, and therefore
e_{\mathrm{so}}(f_{1}E^{l}),\dots,e_{\mathrm{so}}(f_{d}E^{l})

are linearly independent. Thus
d
can be at most the bound in Proposition
3.0.5
.

(2) We have
M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)=H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}}\right)\otimes_{\iota_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. (As mentioned above, the base change of
\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}
to
\mathcal{K}
is an open subscheme of
S^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K^{p}_{f}K^{0}_{p,1}}
; here we use the Koecher principle to get the equality of global sections.) The map (
2.7.1
) induces

H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}}\right)\rightarrow\left(\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}[t^{+},t^{-}]\right),

and this gives the canonical embedding in the statement.

Put
V^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}[t^{+},t^{-}]=\varprojlim\limits_{m}\varinjlim\limits_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}[t^{+},t^{-}]
. Note that from Propositions
3.0.3
and
3.0.4
, for
n\geq m
, we have

e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}[t^{+},t^{-}]\simeq e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m}}\right).

It follows that
V_{\mathrm{so}}^{0}[t^{+},t^{-}]
is
p
-torsion free, and together with Proposition
3.0.1
we get

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}V^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}[t^{+},t^{-}]/pV^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}[t^{+},t^{-}]

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}[1/E],\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}}\right)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}.

Denote this dimension by
d
. The above shows that
V^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}[t^{+},t^{-}]
is a free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module of rank
d
. Thus it suffices to show that there exists
A(t^{+})\geq-t^{+}
such that
\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)\geq d
for
t^{-}\geq A(t^{+})
.
Pick an unramified Hecke character
\chi
of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
of
\infty
-type
(-t_{E}(p-1),0)
. Twisting
E
by the character obtained by composing
\chi
with
\det:G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
, we get a holomorphic form
E^{\prime}
of weight
(0,0,0;2t_{E}(p-1))
with the same vanishing locus as
E
. Multiplying by
(E^{\prime})^{l}
and applying
e_{\mathrm{so}}
gives an injection

e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-}+2t_{E}(p-1))}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right),

so

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)\leq\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-}+2t_{E}(p-1))}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right).

By the definition of
d
, we thus see that for each
0\leq j\leq 2t_{E}(p-1)-1
there exists
l_{j}(t^{+})\geq 0
such that

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}e_{\mathrm{so}}M^{0}_{(0,0,t^{+};-t^{+}+4+j+2t_{E}(p-1)l_{j}(t^{+}))}\left(K_{f}^{p}K^{0}_{p,1};\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)\geq d.

Therefore,
A(t^{+})=-t^{+}+4+2t_{E}(p-1)(\max_{j}\{l_{j}(t^{+})\}+1)
has the required property.
∎

Theorem 3.0.7

(Vertical Control Theorem)
.

\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}
is a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module of finite rank.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{m}
be a maximal ideal of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
. (We know that
\mathfrak{m}\cap\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}=(p,T^{+},T^{-})
, where we identify
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T^{+},T^{-}\rrbracket
by identifying
(1+p,1)\in T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp.
(1,1+p)\in T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with
T^{+}
(resp.
T^{-}
).) We show that
\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}
, the localization of
\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}
at
\mathfrak{m}
, is a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module of finite rank.

First, we consider the quotient
\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}/(p,T^{+},T^{-})
, which by definition, equals
(e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}[\mathfrak{m}])^{*}
. Take
0\geq t^{+}\geq-t^{-}+4
with
\mathcal{P}_{t^{+},t^{-}}\subset\mathfrak{m}
. Then

\displaystyle e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}[\mathfrak{m}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phantom{\text{Prop~\ref{prop:VH}}}}}{{=}}\text{$p$-torsion of }\varinjlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}[t^{+},t^{-}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(Prop~\ref{prop:VH})}}}{{=}}\text{$p$-torsion of }\varinjlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(Prop~\ref{prop:n1})}}}{{=}}\text{$p$-torsion of }\varinjlim_{m}e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phantom{\text{Prop~\ref{prop:VH}}}}}{{=}}e_{\mathrm{so}}\left[\text{$p$-torsion of }\varinjlim_{m}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m}}\right)\right].

Also, Proposition
3.0.1
implies

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{m}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m}}\right)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{m}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}[1/E],\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}}\right)\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\text{$p$-torsion of }\varinjlim_{m}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,m}}\right)

\displaystyle=H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}[1/E],\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1}}\right)\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle=H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1}}\right),

where Proposition
3.0.1
is used again for the second equality. Thus,

e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}[\mathfrak{m}]=e_{\mathrm{so}}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1},\omega_{(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{1,1}}\right).

By Proposition
3.0.6
, we know this is finite dimensional over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Hence,
\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}/(p,T^{+},T^{-})=(e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}[\mathfrak{m}])^{*}
is finite dimensional over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Let

d=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}/(p,T^{+},T^{-}).

By Nakayama’s Lemma, there exist
F_{1},\dots,F_{d}\in\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}
such that

\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}=\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}F_{1}+\cdots+\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}F_{d}.

Next, we show that
F_{1},\dots,F_{d}
are
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-linearly independent. Suppose that
a_{1},\dots,a_{d}\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
are such that
a_{1}F_{1}+\cdots+a_{d}F_{d}=0
. Given any
0\geq t^{+}\geq-t^{-}+4
with
\mathcal{P}_{t^{+},t^{-}}\subset\mathfrak{m}
, we put

\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{t^{+},t^{-}}=\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}\cap\mathcal{P}_{t^{+},t^{-}}.

In view of Proposition
3.0.1
, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module
\varinjlim\limits_{m}\varinjlim\limits_{n}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m},\omega_{\underline{t}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{0,\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)
is
p
-divisible, and by Proposition
3.0.3
so is
e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}^{0}[t^{+},t^{-}]
.
Therefore,

\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}/\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{t^{+},t^{-}}=\left(e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}^{0}[t^{+},t^{-}]\right)^{*}

is
p
-torsion free. On the other hand,

\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\left(\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}/\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{t^{+},t^{-}}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}/(p,T^{+},T^{-})=d.

It follows that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module
\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}/\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{t^{+},t^{-}}
is free of rank
d
, and therefore
a_{1},\dots,a_{d}\in\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{t^{+},t^{-}}
. Then from
\bigcap\limits_{0\geq t^{+}\geq-t^{-}+4}\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{t^{+},t^{-}}=0
we conclude that
a_{1}=\cdots=a_{d}=0
, and hence
\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so},\mathfrak{m}}
is a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module of rank
d
.
∎

This completes the proof of part (1) of Theorem
2.9.1
for cuspidal forms. For part (2), because
\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}
is free over
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
, letting
V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}]=e_{\mathrm{so}}\varinjlim\limits_{n}V^{0}_{n,m}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{F}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}]
we have

(3.0.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\mathcal{O}_{F}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket/\mathcal{P}_{\tau^{+},\tau^{-}}

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\mathpzc{V}^{0,\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathcal{P}_{\tau^{+},\tau^{-}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left((e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}^{0}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}])^{*},\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\left(\varinjlim_{m}V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}]\right)^{*},\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right).

Since the left hand side is a free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module (because
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so}}
is a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module), we see that

\varinjlim\limits_{m}V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}])
is
p
-divisible, and
V^{0}_{m+1,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}]\rightarrow V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}]
is surjective, so

\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\varinjlim_{m}V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}],\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\cong\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\varprojlim_{m}V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}],\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)

and hence

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:MV}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\varprojlim_{m}V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}],\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right),\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{m}V^{0}_{m,\mathrm{so}}[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}]

\displaystyle=\left(\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{so}}V^{0}_{n,m}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\right)[\tau^{+},\tau^{-}],

concluding the proof of part (2) of Theorem
2.9.1
for cuspidal forms. Part (3) follows from Proposition
3.0.6
.

4.
The proof of Theorem
2.9.1
for the non-cuspidal part

We apply the approach in
[
LR20
]
to prove the vertical control theorem for semi-ordinary forms on
\GU(3,1)
by analyzing the quotient
\mathpzc{V}/\mathpzc{V}^{0}
and using the vertical control theorem for cuspidal semi-ordinary forms on
\GU(3,1)
. When studying
\mathpzc{V}/\mathpzc{V}^{0}
, we introduce an auxiliary space
\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
. One difference from
loc.cit
is that
q
-expansions are used there to reduce proving some properties for the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-action on
\mathpzc{V},\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
to matrix computations, but
q
-expansions are not available in the case of
\GU(3,1)
. The analogue of those properties in our case are proved in §
4.5
by working with semi-abelian schemes over the boundary of the partial toroidal compactification.

4.1.
Cusp labels

Following
[
Lan13
]
, a
cusp label
is a
K_{f}
-orbit of triples
(Z,Φ,δ)
, with:

•

{\tt Z}:0\subset{\tt Z}_{-2}\subset{\tt Z}_{-1}={\tt Z}_{-2}^{\bot}\subset L\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
a
fully symplectic admissible
filtration.

•

\Phi=({\tt X},{\tt Y},\phi,\varphi_{-2},\varphi_{0})
a
torus argument
, where

\phi:{\tt Y}\rightarrow{\tt X}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
-linear embedding of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
-modules with finite cokernel, and where, writing
\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z}_{-i}={\tt Z}_{-i}/{\tt Z}_{-i-1}
,
\varphi_{-2}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z}_{-2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Hom\left({\tt X}\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}},\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)\right)
and
\varphi_{0}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}{\tt Y}\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
are isomorphisms such that
\left<v,w\right>=\varphi_{0}(v)\Big(\phi\big(\varphi_{-2}(w)\big)\Big)
.

•

\delta:\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z}=\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z}_{-2}\oplus\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z}_{-1}\oplus\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}L\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
-equivariant splitting.

Following
[
Lan18
, Def. 3.2.3.1]
, an
ordinary cusp label
of tame level
K_{f}^{p}
is a
K^{p}_{f}P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-orbit of triples
({\tt Z},\Phi,\delta)
as above compatible with
{\tt D}
, in the sense that

\displaystyle{\tt Z}_{-2}\otimes_{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subset{\tt D}\subset{\tt Z}_{-1}\otimes_{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

There is a unique cusp label for which
{\tt Z}_{-2}=0
, and in our case, all other cusp labels have
{\tt Z}_{-2}
with rank
1
. As explained in
[
HLTT16
, B.2, B.3, B.11]
, the latter are parametrized by a certain double coset space. To recall this, we have the filtration

(4.1.1)

X^{\vee}\subset X^{\vee}\oplus L_{0}\subset L=X^{\vee}\oplus L_{0}\oplus Y.

Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{P}{{{}}P}\subset G
be the parabolic subgroup preserving this filtration, and
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{P}{{{}}P^{\prime}}\subset P
be the kernel of the natural projection
P\rightarrow\GL(Y)
. Define the triple
(Z

(
1
)

,Φ

(
1
)

,δ

1
)

)
as

•

{\tt Z}^{(1)}
:
{\tt Z}_{-2}=X^{\vee}\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
,
{\tt Z}_{-1}=(X^{\vee}\oplus L_{0})\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
.

•

\Phi^{(1)}=({\tt X}^{(1)},{\tt Y}^{(1)},\phi^{(1)},\varphi^{(1)}_{-2},\varphi^{(1)}_{0})
with
{\tt X}^{(1)}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\left(X^{\vee},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}(1)\right)
,
{\tt Y}^{(1)}=Y
,
\phi^{(1)}:Y\rightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\left(\mathfrak{d}^{-1}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\cdot{\tt x}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}(1)\right)
induced by our fixed basis of
{\tt x}_{1}
and
{\tt y}_{1}
of

and

2\pi i\cdot\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\circ\left<\,,\,\right>_{L}
,
\varphi^{(1)}_{-2}=2\pi\sqrt{-1}\cdot\mathrm{id}
and
\varphi^{(1)}_{0}=\mathrm{id}
.

•

.

Denote by

the filtration obtained as (

) tensored with
\mathbb{Q}
. For every

(

)

g
)

by

•

{\tt Z}^{(g)}_{j}=\left(g^{-1}({\tt V}_{j}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})\right)\cap\left(L\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}\right)
.

•

\Phi^{(g)}=({\tt X}^{(g)},{\tt Y}^{(g)},\phi^{(g)},\varphi^{(g)}_{-2},\varphi^{(g)}_{0})

\mathrm{Gr}^{{\tt Z}^{(g)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{Gr}(g)}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Gr}^{\tt Z^{(1)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta^{(1)}}}{{\rightarrow}}L\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g^{-1}}}{{\rightarrow}}L\otimes\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Then the map that sends

(See

.

The formal completion along boundary strata
