4.3.
The subset
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
of
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
and the subspace
\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
of
\mathpzc{V}

By a generic point, denoted by
\eta
, of the formal completion
\mathfrak{X}_{g,n}
, we mean a generic point of
\Spec(\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U}))
with
U
an affine open subscheme of
\mathfrak{X}_{g,n}
. Let
(\mathcal{G},\lambda,i,\alpha^{p},\alpha_{p})
be the pullback to
\Spec(\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U}))
of the family of semi-abelian varieties over
\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}
, which is a degenerating family obtained by Mumford’s construction. Let

0\longrightarrow\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cT}{{{}}\mathcal{T}}\longrightarrow\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Gray}{{{}}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}\rightarrow 0

be the Raynaud extension of
\mathcal{G}
. Then, for any positive integer
N
,
\mathcal{T}[N]
(resp.
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[N]
) is canonically isomorphic to a subgroup
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cGmu}{{{}}(\mathcal{G}[N])^{\mu}}\subset\mathcal{G}[N]
(resp.
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cGmu}{{{}}(\mathcal{G}[N])^{\mathrm{f}}}\subset\mathcal{G}[N]
), and
(\mathcal{G}[N])^{\mu}\subset(\mathcal{G}[N])^{\mathrm{f}}
. The quotient
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cGmu}{{{}}(\mathcal{G}[N])^{\mathrm{ab}}}=(\mathcal{G}[N])^{\mathrm{f}}/(\mathcal{G}[N])^{\mu}
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathcal{A}[N]
. (See also
[
Lan18
, §3.4.2]
.)

Let

¯

η

be a geometric point over
\eta
. The ordinary level structure
\alpha_{p,\overline{\eta}}
of
\mathcal{G}_{\overline{\eta}}
gives rise to a filtration

{\tt F}^{+}_{\bar{\eta}}\colon 0\subset{\tt F}^{+}_{2,\bar{\eta}}\subset{\tt F}^{+}_{3,\overline{\eta}}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}]^{\mathrm{mult}+}

(refer to text around (
2.5.2
)). We define a subset
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}\subset C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
in terms of the relative positions of
{\tt F}^{+}_{2,\overline{\eta}}
and
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p])^{\mu}
.

Definition 4.3.1
.

Define the subset
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
of
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
consisting of
g
such that

{\tt F}^{+}_{2,\bar{\eta}}\cap(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p])^{\mu}=0

for a geometric point
\bar{\eta}
over a generic point
\eta
of
\mathfrak{X}_{g,n}
.

One can check that the preceding definition does not depend on the choice of
\bar{\eta}
.

Proposition 4.3.2
.

We have

(4.3.1)

C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}=\mathrm{Im}\left(G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathbb{Q},f})P^{\flat}_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\longrightarrow\GL(X\otimes\mathbb{Q})\ltimes P^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})/K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n}\right),

which is in a natural bijection with

(4.3.2)

C(K^{p}_{f})\times\left(U_{\mathcal{K},K^{p}_{f}}\ltimes P^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\backslash P^{\flat}_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/K^{1}_{p,n}\cap P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\right),

where

P

♭

D

(ℚ

p

)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\,\middle|\,g^{+}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{11}&a_{12}&a_{13}&a_{14}\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&a_{23}&a_{24}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&a_{33}&a_{34}\\
&&&a_{44}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ with }\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{21}&a_{22}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{23}\\
a_{33}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{p}\right\}

and

\displaystyle P^{\flat}_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle=\left\{g_{p}\in P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\,\middle|\,g^{+}_{p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{11}&a_{12}&a_{13}&a_{14}\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&a_{23}&a_{24}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&a_{33}&a_{34}\\
&&&a_{44}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ with }\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{21}&a_{22}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\right\}

\displaystyle=P^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&&1\\
1\\
&1\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(K^{0}_{p,n}\cap P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\right).

Proof.

Given
g_{p}\in P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with

\displaystyle(g^{-1}_{p})^{+}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}c_{11}&c_{12}&c_{13}&\ast\\
c_{21}&c_{22}&c_{23}&\ast\\
c_{31}&c_{32}&c_{33}&\ast\\
&&&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right),

\displaystyle(g^{-1}_{p})^{-}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d_{11}&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right),

the corresponding
{\tt F}_{2,\overline{\eta}}
is spanned by the image of
x^{+}_{1},w^{+}_{1}
under
\alpha_{p,\bar{\eta}}
, and
\mathcal{T}_{\bar{\eta}}[p]
is spanned by the image under
\alpha_{p,\overline{\eta}}
of

(4.3.3)

({\tt x}^{+}_{1},{\tt w}^{+}_{1},{\tt w}^{+}_{2};{\tt x}^{-}_{1})\begin{pmatrix}c_{11}&c_{12}&c_{13}\\
c_{21}&c_{22}&c_{23}\\
c_{31}&c_{32}&c_{33}\\
&&&d_{11}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&0\\
0&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},

Therefore, the condition
{\tt F}^{+}_{2,\overline{\eta}}\cap(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p])^{\mu}=0
is equivalent to the condition that
c_{11}{\tt x}^{+}_{1}+c_{21}{\tt w}^{+}_{1}+c_{31}{\tt w}^{+}_{2}
does not belong to
\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{{\tt x}^{+}_{1},{\tt w}^{+}_{1}\}
, which is equivalent to
c_{31}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
. The condition
c_{31}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
for
(g^{-1}_{p})^{+}
is equivalent to the condition
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{21}&a_{22}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for
g^{+}_{p}
. This shows that
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
equals (
4.3.2
). It is also easy to see that there is a natural bijection between (
4.3.1
) and (
4.3.2
).
∎

Definition 4.3.3
.

Let

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Vflatnm}{{{}}V^{\flat}_{n,m}}=\left\{f\in V_{n,m}\;\colon\;f|_{\mathscr{Z}_{g,n}}=0\quad\text{for all $g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}-C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}$}\right\},

i.e. the space of forms in
V_{n,m}
vanishing along all the boundary strata of
\mathscr{T}_{n}^{\mathrm{tor}}
indexed by ordinary cusp labels outside
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, and

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Vflat}{{{}}\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}}=\varinjlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}V^{\flat}_{n,m}.

4.4.
The exact sequence for
V^{\flat}_{n,m}

When
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, the level
K^{\prime}_{f,g,n}
is independent of
n
, and we denote it by
K^{\prime}_{f,g}
with

(4.4.1)

\displaystyle K^{\prime p}_{f,g}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Im}\left(P(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathbb{Q},f})\cap gK^{p}_{f}g^{-1}\rightarrow G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathbb{Q},f})\right),

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{K^{\prime}pg}{{{}}K^{\prime}_{p,g}}

\displaystyle=\left\{h\in G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}):h^{+}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
&\ast\end{pmatrix}\mod p\right\}.

Let
M

(
0
,
0
)

(K

′

f
,
g

;ℤ/p

m

ℤ)
be the space of classical automorphic forms on
G^{\prime}
valued in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}
of weight
(0,0)
and level
K^{\prime}_{f,g}
(which are functions valued in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}
on the finite set
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})/K^{\prime}_{f,g}
), and
M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\varinjlim\limits_{m}M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z})
.

Proposition 4.4.1
.

We have the exact sequences

(4.4.2)

0\longrightarrow V^{0}_{n,m}\longrightarrow V^{\flat}_{n,m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\oplus\Phi_{g}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bigoplus_{g\in C(K^{p}_{f})}M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/U_{\mathcal{K},K^{p}_{f}}\rrbracket\longrightarrow 0

and

(4.4.3)

0\longrightarrow\mathpzc{V}^{0}\longrightarrow\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\oplus\Phi_{g}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bigoplus_{g\in C(K^{p}_{f})}M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/U_{\mathcal{K},K^{p}_{f}}\rrbracket\longrightarrow 0,

where
Φ

g

is obtained by restriction to the stratum
\mathscr{Z}_{g,n}
(whose global sections are the same as the global sections of
\mathscr{Y}_{g,n}
, which can be identified with the
0
-dimensional Shimura variety
\mathscr{S}_{G^{\prime},K^{\prime}_{f,g}}
), and is sometimes called a Siegel operator.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{n}:\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}^{\min}_{n}
be the natural map. By
[
Lan18
, Lem. 8.2.2.10]
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\pi_{n,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}/\pi_{n,*}\mathcal{I}_{\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}}\right)^{\wedge}_{\mathscr{Y}_{g,n}}\simeq h_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{g,n}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{S}_{G^{\prime},K^{\prime}_{f,g}}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}.

(Here
h
is the map in the diagram (
4.2.2
).) Taking global sections over the affine scheme
\mathscr{T}^{\min}_{n,m}
, we get

(4.4.4)

V_{n,m}/V^{0}_{n,m}=\bigoplus_{g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}}M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g,n};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}).

By definition, an element in
V_{n,m}
belongs to
V^{\flat}_{n,m}
if and only if it vanishes along the strata associated to the cusp labels outside
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, so

(4.4.5)

V^{\flat}_{n,m}/V^{0}_{n,m}=\bigoplus_{g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}}M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}).

The action of
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on
V_{n,m}
preserves
V^{0}_{n,m}
, so it descends to
V_{n,m}/V^{0}_{n,m}
. This action permutes the direct summands of the right-hand side of (
4.4.4
). More precisely, the corresponding action of
(a_{1},a_{2})\in T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on the index set

C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}=C(K^{p}_{f})\times\left(U_{\mathcal{K},K^{p}_{f}}\ltimes P^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\backslash P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/K^{1}_{p}\cap P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\right)

sends
g=g^{p}g_{p}
to
g^{p}g_{p}t_{a_{1},a_{2}}
where
t_{a_{1},a_{2}}\in G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with
t^{+}_{a_{1},a_{2}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&1\\
&&a_{1}\\
&&&a^{-1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. It is easy to see that
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action on
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
preserves
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, so the
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on
V_{n,m}
preserves
V^{\flat}_{n,m}
and induces an action on
V^{\flat}_{n,m}/V^{0}_{n,m}
.

For given
g\in C(K^{p}_{f})
, set

(4.4.6)

\displaystyle C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord},g}

\displaystyle=\{\text{elements in $C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}$ whose projection to $C(K^{p}_{f})$ is $g$}\},

\displaystyle C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord},g}

\displaystyle=\{\text{elements in $C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}$ whose projection to $C(K^{p}_{f})$ is $g$}\}.

The
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module structures of
V_{n,m}/V^{0}_{n,m}
(resp.
V^{\flat}_{n,m}/V^{0}_{n,m}
) are determined by the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-actions on
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord},g}
(resp.
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord},g}
). The
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action on
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord},g}
has too many orbits, and the number of orbits grows with
n
, so
V_{n,m}/V^{0}_{n,m}
is not very nice as a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module. In contrast, the
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-action on
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord},g}
is transitive, factoring through
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})/U_{\mathcal{K},K^{p}_{f}}\rrbracket
, and the action of this quotient is free. Therefore, by fixing an element in
P^{\flat}_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, which we will always choose to be
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&&1\\
1\\
&1\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, we get the isomorphism

(4.4.7)

\text{RHS of (\ref{eq:Vquot})}\cong M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/U_{\mathcal{K},K^{p}_{f}}\rrbracket,

from which the exact sequence (
4.4.2
) follows. The exact sequence (
4.4.3
) follows from (
4.4.2
) by taking the direct limit.
∎

4.5.
Three propositions on the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-action on
\mathpzc{V}
and
\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
.

We introduce some setting for the proof of the following propositions. Given
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
, recall that
\eta
denotes a generic point of
\mathfrak{X}_{g,n,m}
, and
\bar{\eta}
denotes a geometric point over
\eta
. Let
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}},i_{\bar{\eta}},\lambda_{\bar{\eta}},\alpha^{p}_{\bar{\eta}},\alpha_{p,\bar{\eta}})
be the pullback to
\bar{\eta}
of the family of semi-abelian schemes over
\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n,m}
. Recall that certain Lagrange subgroups
C\subset\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{2}]
are used in the construction of
\mathscr{C}^{1\bullet}ar_{j,n,m}
for defining
U^{\bullet}_{p,j}
in §
2.8
. (Here
(\bullet,j)=(+,2),\,(+,3)
or
(-,1)
.) Let

(4.5.1)

(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\bar{\eta}}=\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}/C,i^{\prime}_{\bar{\eta}},\lambda^{\prime}_{\bar{\eta}},(\alpha^{p}_{\bar{\eta}})^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}_{p,\bar{\eta}})

be the tuple defined as in the construction of
\mathscr{C}^{\bullet}_{j,n,m}
. If
(e^{\pm}_{1},e^{\pm}_{2},e^{\pm}_{3},e^{\pm}_{4})
is a basis of
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}]
compatible with
\alpha_{p,\bar{\eta}}
, then
C
is spanned by
(e^{\pm}_{1},e^{\pm}_{2},e^{\pm}_{3},e^{\pm}_{4})\cdot p^{n}\gamma^{\pm}_{C,p}
with
\gamma_{C,p}
given as in equations (
2.8.1
), (
2.8.2
), and (
2.8.3
). From Mumford’s construction of the degenerating family over
\mathfrak{X}_{g,n,m}
, we see that the tuple (
4.5.1
),
corresponds to a geometric point
\bar{\eta}^{\prime}
over a generic point
\eta^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{X}_{g^{\prime},n,m}
, where
g^{\prime}=g\cdot\gamma_{C,p}
.

Proposition 4.5.1
.

The space
\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
is preserved by the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators.

Proof.

We shall show that
g^{\prime}=g\cdot\gamma_{C,p}\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
only if
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. By the definition of the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators, this will imply that if
f\in\mathpzc{V}
vanishes along the strata outside
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, then so does
U^{\bullet}_{p,j}(f)
. We use the description (
4.3.1
) for
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. Consider the case
(\bullet,j)=(+,2)
.
Write
g^{+}_{p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{11}&a_{12}&a_{13}&a_{14}\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&a_{23}&a_{24}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&a_{33}&a_{34}\\
&&&a_{44}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and and
g^{\prime+}_{p}=g^{+}_{p}\cdot\gamma^{+}_{C,p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a^{\prime}_{11}&a^{\prime}_{12}&a^{\prime}_{13}&a^{\prime}_{14}\\
a^{\prime}_{21}&a^{\prime}_{22}&a^{\prime}_{23}&a^{\prime}_{24}\\
a^{\prime}_{31}&a^{\prime}_{32}&a^{\prime}_{33}&a^{\prime}_{34}\\
&&&a^{\prime}_{44}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
with
\gamma^{+}_{C,p}
as in (
2.8.1
). We have

(4.5.2)

\begin{pmatrix}a^{\prime}_{11}&a^{\prime}_{12}&a^{\prime}_{13}&a^{\prime}_{14}\\
a^{\prime}_{21}&a^{\prime}_{22}&a^{\prime}_{23}&a^{\prime}_{24}\\
a^{\prime}_{31}&a^{\prime}_{32}&a^{\prime}_{33}&a^{\prime}_{34}\\
&&&a^{\prime}_{44}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{11}&a_{12}&\frac{a_{13}+u_{1}a_{11}+u_{2}a_{12}}{p}&\ast\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&\frac{a_{23}+u_{1}a_{21}+u_{2}a_{22}}{p}&\ast\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&\frac{a_{33}+u_{1}a_{31}+u_{2}a_{32}}{p}&\ast\\
&&&\frac{a_{44}}{p}\end{pmatrix}

and

(4.5.3)

\begin{pmatrix}a^{\prime}_{21}&a^{\prime}_{22}\\
a^{\prime}_{31}&a^{\prime}_{32}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}a^{\prime}_{23}\\
a^{\prime}_{33}\end{pmatrix}=p^{-1}\left[\begin{pmatrix}a_{21}&a_{22}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}a_{23}\\
a_{33}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
u_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right].

Since
u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the right hand side can be integral only if
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{21}&a_{22}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{23}\\
a_{33}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{p}
. Therefore,
g^{\prime}=g\cdot\gamma_{C,p}\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
only if
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. The argument for the other two cases is similar. ∎

Proposition 4.5.2
.

There exists a positive integer
N
such that the exact sequences in Proposition
4.4.1
are
(U^{\bullet}_{p,j})^{N}
-equivariant for
(\bullet,j)=(+,2),(+,3),(-,1)
in the sense that for all
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
and
f\in\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
, we have

\Phi_{g}\left((U^{\bullet}_{p,j})^{N}f\right)=\Phi_{g}(f).

Proof.

Take
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
and we use the setting described at the beginning of this subsection. In order to look at the image of
U^{\bullet}_{p,j}f
under the Siegel operator
\Phi_{g}
, we consider the abelian quotients
\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}
of the Raynaud extensions

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\bar{\eta}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\bar{\eta}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}\longrightarrow 0,

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow 0.

Let

(4.5.4)

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Cab}{{{}}C^{\mathrm{f}}}

\displaystyle=\big(C+(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mu}\big)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mathrm{f}},

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Cab}{{{}}C^{\mathrm{ab}}}

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{f}}/(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mu}.

(See the beginning of §
4.3
for the definition of
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mu}
and
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mathrm{f}}
.) Then we have

\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}/C^{\mathrm{ab}}.

We use the description (
4.1.3
) and (
4.3.2
) for
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
and
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. Write
g=g^{p}g_{p}
with
g_{p}\in P_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and

(4.5.5)

\displaystyle(g^{-1}_{p})^{+}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}c_{11}&c_{12}&c_{13}&\ast\\
c_{21}&c_{22}&c_{23}&\ast\\
c_{31}&c_{32}&c_{33}&\ast\\
&&&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right),

\displaystyle(g^{-1}_{p})^{-}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d_{11}&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right).

The condition
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
implies that
c^{-1}_{31}c_{11},c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Thus we see that
(\mathcal{G}_{\overline{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mu}
is spanned by

(4.5.6)

c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}e^{+}_{1}+c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}e^{+}_{2}+e^{+}_{3},\quad e^{-}_{1}

(
cf.
(
4.3.3
)), and
(\mathcal{G}_{\overline{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mathrm{f}}
is spanned by

(4.5.7)

e^{+}_{1},\quad e^{+}_{2},\quad e^{+}_{3},\quad e^{-}_{1},\quad(e^{-}_{2},e^{-}_{3},e^{-}_{4})\begin{pmatrix}-\iota_{p}(\bar{\zeta}_{0})\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}&c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}.

We first consider the operator
U^{+}_{p,3}
. By a direct computation, we see that for
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, the
p
-part of
g^{\prime}\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
is

(4.5.8)

g^{\prime+}_{p}=g^{+}_{p}\cdot\gamma^{+}_{C,p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\ast\\
&1&&\ast\\
&&1&\ast\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)g^{+}_{p},

where
\ast\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. This shows that the cusp label
g^{\prime}
is independent of
C
and belongs to
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. For given
C
spanned by
(e^{\pm}_{1},e^{\pm}_{2},e^{\pm}_{3},e^{\pm}_{4})\cdot p^{n-2}\gamma^{\pm}_{C,p}
with
\gamma_{C,p}^{\pm}
as in (
2.8.2
),
from the definition (
4.5.4
) and the basis (
4.5.7
) of
(\mathcal{G}_{\overline{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mathrm{f}}
, we see that
C^{\mathrm{f}}
is spanned by

c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}e^{+}_{1}+c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}e^{+}_{2}+e^{+}_{3},\quad e^{-}_{1},\quad(e^{-}_{2},e^{-}_{3},e^{-}_{4})\cdot p^{n-2}\begin{pmatrix}-\iota_{p}(\bar{\zeta}_{0})\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}&c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},

its quotient
C^{\mathrm{ab}}
is independent of the choice of
C
, and is spanned by
(\epsilon^{-}_{1},\epsilon^{-}_{2})\cdot p^{n-2}
, with
(\epsilon^{-}_{1},\epsilon^{-}_{2})
the image of
(e^{-}_{2},e^{-}_{3},e^{-}_{4})\begin{pmatrix}-\iota_{p}(\bar{\zeta}_{0})\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}&c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
under the quotient map
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\bar{\eta}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}
which is a basis of
\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}]^{-}
. (Here we identify
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mathrm{f}}
with
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}]
by the canonical isomorphism between them.) Thus we get

(4.5.9)

\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}/\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{2}]^{-}.

Denote by
\left<p\right>^{-}
the operator on
M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z})
induced by taking the quotient by
\mathcal{A}[p^{2}]^{-}
. Combining (
4.5.8
) and (
4.5.9
), we see that the trace cancels the normalizer in (
2.8.5
) due to the independence of the choice of
C
, and

\Phi_{g}(U^{+}_{p,3}f)=(\left<p\right>^{-})^{2}\Phi_{g^{\prime}}(f).

With
K^{p}_{f}
fixed, there exists
N_{1}\geq 1
, such that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\ast\\
&1&&\ast\\
&&1&\ast\\
&&&p^{-N_{1}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{p}g
and
g
represent the same element in the double coset
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, and
(\left<p\right>^{-})^{2N_{1}}=1
on
M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z})
. For such
N_{1}
, we deduce that

\Phi_{g}\left((U^{+}_{p,3})^{N_{1}}f\right)=\Phi_{g}(f).

for all
f\in\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
and
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. A similar calculation shows that that there exists
N_{2}\geq 1
such that

\Phi_{g}\left((U^{-}_{p,1})^{N_{2}}f\right)=\Phi_{g}(f).

for all
f\in\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
and
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
.

It remains to consider
U^{+}_{p,2}
.
Write
g^{+}_{p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{11}&a_{12}&a_{13}&\ast\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&a_{32}&\ast\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&a_{33}&\ast\\
&&&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
g^{\prime+}_{p}=g^{+}_{p}\cdot\gamma^{+}_{C,p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a^{\prime}_{11}&a^{\prime}_{12}&a^{\prime}_{13}&a^{\prime}_{14}\\
a^{\prime}_{21}&a^{\prime}_{22}&a^{\prime}_{23}&a^{\prime}_{24}\\
a^{\prime}_{31}&a^{\prime}_{32}&a^{\prime}_{33}&a^{\prime}_{34}\\
&&&a^{\prime}_{44}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
with
\gamma_{C,p}^{+}
as in (
2.8.1
). Then we have (
4.5.2
) and (
4.5.3
), and

g^{\prime}\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
if and only if

(4.5.10)

\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
u_{2}\end{pmatrix}\equiv-\begin{pmatrix}a_{21}&a_{22}\\
a_{31}&a_{32}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}a_{23}\\
a_{33}\end{pmatrix}\mod p.

Now suppose
g^{\prime}\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
,
i.e.
the entries
u_{1},u_{2}
in
\gamma^{\pm}_{C,p}
(see (
2.8.1
)) satisfy the congruence (
4.5.10
). Then we have

g^{\prime+}_{p}\in\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&1&&\ast\\
&&1&\ast\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)g^{+}_{p}K^{1}_{p,n},

and as an element in the double coset
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, and
g^{\prime}
represents the same element as
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&&&\\
&1&&\\
&&1&\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)g
in
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. The congruence (
4.5.10
) implies that the
c_{ij}
’s in (
4.5.5
) satisfies

(4.5.11)

\displaystyle u_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv-c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}\mod p,

\displaystyle u_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv-c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}\mod p.

From the definition (
4.5.4
) and the relation (
4.5.11
), by using the basis (
4.5.7
) of
(\mathcal{G}_{\overline{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mathrm{f}}
and the basis of
C
in (
2.8.1
), we see that
C^{\mathrm{f}}
is spanned by

c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}e^{+}_{1}+c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}e^{+}_{2}+e^{+}_{3},\quad e^{-}_{1},\quad(e^{-}_{2},e^{-}_{3},e^{-}_{4})\cdot p^{n-2}\begin{pmatrix}-\iota_{p}(\bar{\zeta}_{0})^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
c^{-1}_{31}c_{11}&c^{-1}_{31}c_{21}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}.

Like in the case of
U^{+}_{j,3}
, we can deduce that
C^{\mathrm{ab}}=\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}]^{-}
, and

\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}/\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{2}]^{-}.

The independence of
C^{\mathrm{ab}}
on the
\ast
’s in
\gamma^{+}_{C,p}
cancels the normalizer in (
2.8.5
), and we get

\Phi_{g}(U^{+}_{p,2}f)=(\left<p\right>^{-})^{2}\Phi_{g^{\prime}}(f).

For fixed
K^{p}_{f}
, take
N_{3}
such that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&&&\\
&1&&\\
&&1&\\
&&&p^{-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{N_{3}}_{p}g

and
g
represent the same element in the double coset
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
and
(\left<p\right>^{-})^{2N_{3}}=1
on
M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z})
. Then

\Phi_{g}\left((U^{+}_{p,3})^{N_{3}}f\right)=\Phi_{g}(f).

for all
f\in\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}
and
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. By taking
N=N_{1}N_{2}N_{3}
, we conclude the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.5.3
.

(U^{+}_{p,2})^{m}f\in V^{\flat}_{n,m}
for all
f\in V_{n,m}
.

Proof.

We shall show that for all
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}-C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
and
f\in V_{n,m}
,

(4.5.12)

\Phi_{g}\left(U^{+}_{p,2}f\right)\in pM_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z});

it is easy to see that the proposition follows from this. For a given
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}-C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
, we use the setting described at the beginning of this subsection. As in the proof of Proposition
4.5.2
, we consider
\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}
, the abelian quotient of the Raynaud extension associated to
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}/C
. We have

\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}/C^{\mathrm{ab}},

where
\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}}
is the abelian quotient of the Raynaud extension associated to
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}
, and
C^{\mathrm{ab}}
is defined in (
4.5.4
). Since the
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operator considered here is
U^{+}_{p,2}
, the Lagrangian subgroup
C\subset\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{2}]
is spanned by
(e^{\pm}_{1},e^{\pm}_{2},e^{\pm}_{3},e^{\pm}_{4})\cdot p^{n}\gamma^{\pm}_{C,p}
with
\gamma_{C,p}^{\pm}
as in (
2.8.1
). We use the description (
4.1.3
) and (
4.3.2
) for
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
and
C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
. Write
(g^{-1}_{p})^{+}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}c_{11}&c_{12}&c_{13}&\ast\\
c_{21}&c_{22}&c_{23}&\ast\\
c_{31}&c_{32}&c_{33}&\ast\\
&&&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,
(g^{-1}_{p})^{-}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d_{11}&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&\ast&\ast&\ast\end{smallmatrix}\right)
(which are elements in
\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
). The condition
g\notin C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})^{\flat}_{\mathrm{ord}}
implies that
c_{31}\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
c_{11}
or
c_{21}
belongs to
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
.

Suppose
c_{11}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
. The group
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mu}
is spanned by

(4.5.13)

c_{11}e^{+}_{1}+c_{21}e^{+}_{2}+c_{31}e^{+}_{3},\quad e^{-}_{1},

and
(\mathcal{G}_{\overline{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mathrm{f}}
is spanned by

(4.5.14)

e^{+}_{1},\quad e^{+}_{2},\quad e^{+}_{3},\quad e^{-}_{1},\quad(e^{-}_{2},e^{-}_{3},e^{-}_{4})\begin{pmatrix}-\iota^{c}_{p}(\zeta_{0})^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}c_{11}&0\\
0&c_{11}\\
-c_{21}&c_{31}\end{pmatrix}.

By the definition of
C^{\mathrm{f}}
in (
4.5.4
), a direct computation shows that
C^{\mathrm{f}}
is spanned by
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mu}
plus

(4.5.15)

\left((c_{11}u_{2}-c_{21}u_{1})e^{+}_{2}+c_{11}e^{+}_{3}\right)\cdot p^{n-1},\quad(e^{-}_{2},e^{-}_{3},e^{-}_{4})\begin{pmatrix}-\iota^{c}_{p}(\zeta_{0})^{-1}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}c_{11}&0\\
0&c_{11}\\
-c_{21}&c_{31}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}c_{11}&0\\
c_{11}u_{2}-c_{21}u_{1}&p\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,
C^{\mathrm{ab}}
is spanned by the image of (
4.5.15
) modulo
(\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\eta}}[p^{n}])^{\mu}
.
Let
u^{\prime}_{2}=c_{11}u_{2}-c_{21}u_{1}
. The trace in (
2.8.5
) corresponds to a sum with
u_{1},u^{\prime}_{2}
and the two
\ast
’s in
\gamma_{C,p}
varying in
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
. Since (
4.5.15
) depends only on
u^{\prime}_{2}
, we see that
C^{\mathrm{ab}}
, and hence
\mathcal{A}_{\bar{\eta}^{\prime}}
depends only on
u^{\prime}_{2}
. Its independence of the two
\ast
’s implies that the sum over the two
\ast
’s cancels the normalization factor in (
2.8.5
). Its independence of
u_{1}
implies that the sum over
u_{1}
contributes a factor
p
. Therefore, the evaluation of
\Phi_{g}\left(U^{+}_{p,2}f\right)
at
\bar{\eta}^{\prime}
is divisible by
p
. This shows the inclusion (
4.5.12
), hence the result in this case.
The case
c_{21}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
can be similarly treated.
∎

4.6.
The ordinary projection on
\mathpzc{V}
and the fundamental exact sequence

In this section, we use the exact sequence in Proposition
4.4.1
and the three propositions proved in §
4.5
to show that
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
converges for all
f\in\mathpzc{V}
, that
\mathpzc{V}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V},\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)
is a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module of finite rank, and deduce the fundamental exact sequence in part (4) of Theorem
2.9.1
.

Theorem 4.6.1

(Vertical Control Theorem)
.

Let
U_{p}=U^{+}_{p,2}U^{+}_{p,3}U^{-}_{p,1}
. Then for each
f\in\mathpzc{V}
,
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
converges, and we can define the semi-ordinary projector as
e_{\mathrm{so}}=\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}
. The
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module

\mathpzc{V}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{so}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V},\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)

is free of finite rank over
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
.

Proof.

Given
f\in V_{n,m}
, we define the following finiteness
property for
f
:

(F)

The submodule generated by
\left(U_{p}\right)^{r}f
,
r\geq 0
, is finitely generated over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}
.

It is easy to see that the convergence of
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
follows from the property (F) for
f
. By Proposition
4.5.3
, in order to show that all
f\in V_{n,m}
satisfy (F), it suffices to show that all
f\in V^{\flat}_{n,m}
satisfy (F). Given
f\in V^{\flat}_{n,m}
, it follows from Proposition
4.5.2
that
f^{\prime}=U^{N}_{p}f-f
belongs to
V^{0}_{n,m}
. Proposition
3.0.2
implies that there exists
M\geq 0
such that

U^{M+1}_{p}f^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{Z}}\left\{f^{\prime},U_{p}f^{\prime},U^{2}_{p}f^{\prime},\dots,U^{M}_{p}f^{\prime}\right\}.

Then

U^{M+N+1}_{p}f\in\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{Z}}\left\{f,U_{p}f,U^{2}_{p}f,\dots,U^{M+N}_{p}f\right\},

from which it follows that for all
r\geq 0
,

U^{r}_{p}f\in\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{Z}}\left\{f,U_{p}f,U^{2}_{p}f,\dots,U^{M+N}_{p}f\right\}.

Hence (F) holds for
f
. Therefore, we have proved that
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}f
converges for all
f\in\mathpzc{V}
, and we can define the semi-ordinary projector
e_{\mathrm{so}}
on
\mathpzc{V}
as

(4.6.1)

e_{\mathrm{so}}=\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow\infty}(U_{p})^{r!}.

By Proposition
4.5.3
, we have

(4.6.2)

e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}=e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}^{\flat}.

Applying
e_{\mathrm{so}}
to the Pontryagin dual of the exact sequence in Proposition
4.4.1
and using (
4.6.2
), we obtain the exact sequence

(4.6.3)

0\longrightarrow M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/U_{\mathcal{K},K^{p}_{f}}\rrbracket\longrightarrow\mathpzc{V}^{*}_{\mathrm{so}}\longrightarrow\mathpzc{V}^{0,*}_{\mathrm{so}}\longrightarrow 0.

We know that
M_{(0,0)}(K^{\prime}_{f,g};\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is a free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module of finite rank, so the leftmost term in (
4.6.3
) is a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module of finite rank. The rightmost term
\mathpzc{V}^{0,*}_{\mathrm{so}}
is also a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module of finite rank by Theorem
3.0.7
. Since
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}(M,N)
vanishes for free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-modules
M
, we deduce that
\mathpzc{V}^{*}_{\mathrm{so}}
, as a
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module, is isomorphic to the direct sum of the terms on the two ends of (
4.6.3
), and therefore is a free
\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}
-module of finite rank.
∎

Part (2) of Theorem
2.9.1
for non-cuspidal semi-ordianry families can be proved in the same way as the cuspidal semi-ordianry families. The freeness of
\mathpzc{V}^{0,*}_{\mathrm{so}}
implies that applying
\Hom_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}(\,\cdot\,,\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}})
preserves the exactness of (
4.6.3
), and part (4) of Theorem
2.9.1
follows.

4.7.
The Fourier–Jacobi expansion

We introduce the Fourier–Jacobi expansions of
p
-adic forms on
\GU(3,1)
, which will be used in §
7
for analyzing the Klingen Eisenstein family on
\GU(3,1)
constructed in §
5
.

In §
4.2
, for an ordinary cusp label
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
, we described
\mathfrak{X}_{g,n}
, the formal completion of
\mathscr{T}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{n}
along the boundary stratum
\mathscr{Z}_{g,n}
. From the description there, we see that

H^{0}\left(\mathfrak{X}_{g,n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{g,n}}\right)=\left(\prod_{\beta}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{g,n},\mathcal{L}(\beta)\right)\right)^{\Gamma_{g}},

where
\beta
runs over
{\bf S}^{\vee}_{\Phi^{(g)}_{K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n}}}\cap{\bf P}_{\Phi^{(g)}_{K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n}}}
, which can be identified with a subset of
\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})_{\geq 0}
, and
\mathcal{L}(\beta)
is the invertible sheaf over
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{g,n}
of
\beta
-homogeneous functions on
\Xi^{\mathrm{ord}}_{g,n}
. Therefore, given
g\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
and
\beta\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})_{\geq 0}
, the restriction to
\mathfrak{X}_{g,n}
induces a map

(4.7.1)

V_{n,m}\longrightarrow H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{g,n},\mathcal{L}(\beta)\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}\right).

We consider
g=\mathbf{1}_{4}
and
\beta\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})_{\geq 0}
. In this case, the
p
-component of level group for the Shimura variety
\mathscr{S}_{G^{\prime},K^{\prime}_{f,\mathbf{1}_{4},n}}
in the diagram (
4.2.2
) is

K^{\prime}_{p,\mathbf{1}_{4},n}=\left\{g\in\GU(2)(\mathbb{Z}_{p}):g^{+}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\mod p^{n}\right\},

(depending on
n
).
Let

(4.7.2)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{VJbeta}{{{}}V^{J,\beta}_{\GU(2)}}=\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n}H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathbf{1}_{4},n},\mathcal{L}(\beta)\otimes\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}\right).

The map gives the map of taking the
\beta
-th Fourier–Jacobi coefficient of
p
-adic forms on
\GU(3,1)
along the boundary stratum labeled by
\mathbf{1}_{4}
:

(4.7.3)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{FJ}{{{}}\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta}}:V_{\GU(3,1)}\longrightarrow V^{J,\beta}_{\GU(2)}.

5.
The construction of the Klingen family

5.1.
Some notation

Let
K

∞

be the maximal abelian pro-
p
extension of
\mathcal{K}
unramified outside
p
and
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{GammaK}{{{}}\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}=\Gal(\mathcal{K}_{\infty}/\mathcal{K})
. Then
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{p}
. Denote by
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Omega}{{{}}\Omega_{\infty}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
(resp.
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Omega}{{{}}\Omega_{p}}\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{ur},\times}_{p}
) the complex CM period (
p
-adic CM period) with respect to the embeddings in (
1.0.4
) (cf.
[
Hsi14a
, Section 2.8]
). We also fix an isomorphism
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\cong\mathbb{C}
compatible with the embeddings in (
1.0.4
). Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{L}{{{}}L}\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be a sufficiently large finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and denote by

^

O

ur

L

the ring of integers of the completion of the maximal unramified extension of
L
.

Four our later use of theta correspondence for unitary groups, we also fix a Hecke character

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{lambda}{{{}}\lambda}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times},

such that

\displaystyle\lambda|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}},

\displaystyle\lambda_{\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{|z\bar{z}|^{1/2}}.

5.2.
Our setup

We assume the following conditions on
π
, an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by a newform
f
of weight
2
:

•

for all finite places
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
,
\pi_{v}
is either unramified or Steinberg or Steinberg twisted by an unramified quadratic character of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}
,

•

\pi_{p}
is unramified,

•

there exists a prime
q
not split in
\mathcal{K}
such that
\pi
is ramified at
q
, and if
2
does not split in
\mathcal{K}
, then
\pi
is ramified at
2
,

•

\bar{\rho}_{\pi}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{K})}
is irreducible, (which is automatically true if
\pi
is not ordinary at
p
because in this case
\bar{\rho}_{\pi}|_{G_{\mathcal{K}},\mathfrak{p}}\cong\bar{\rho}_{\pi}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q},p}}
is irreducible by
[
Edi92
]
), where
\bar{\rho}_{\pi}
denotes the residual representation of the Galois representation
\rho_{\pi}
.

We also fix an algebraic Hecke character
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{xi}{{{}}\xi}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of
\infty
-type
\left(0,k_{0}\right)
with
k

0

an even integer.

5.3.
The weight space

For an algebraic Hecke character
\chi:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of
\infty
-type
(k_{1},k_{2})
, we denote by
\chi_{0}
its associated unitary Hecke character,
i.e.

\chi_{0}=\chi|\cdot|^{-\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}}},

and denote by
\chi_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
its associated
p
-adic character, sometimes called the
p
-adic avatar
of
\chi
, as

(5.3.1)

\displaystyle\chi_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},f}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p},

\displaystyle\chi_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}(x)=\chi(x)x^{k_{1}}_{\mathfrak{p}}x^{k_{2}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}.

Applying this convention to
\xi
, we get
ξ

0

and
ξ

p

-

adic

.

The weight space we will use for constructing the semi-ordinary Klingen Eisenstein family on
\GU(3,1)
is
\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}\big(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\big)
. By identifying
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
with a quotient of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},f}
, the arithmetic points in the weight space are
p
-adic avatars of the algebraic Hecke characters of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
whose
p
-adic avatars factor through
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
. We will use
τ
to denote such a Hecke character, and
τ

p

-

adic

to denote its
p
-adic avatar. By the above convention,
τ

0

denotes the associated unitary character of
\tau
. The interpolation points we will use are the
\tau
’s satisfying that
\xi\tau
has
\infty
-type
(0,k)
with
k
an even integer
\geq 6
. (One can consider more general
\infty
-types by using Masss–Shimura differential operators. For our purpose here, only considering the case of
\infty
-type
(0,k)
suffices.)

5.4.
The groups

By the assumption on
\pi
in §
5.2
, we can fix a prime
q
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{$q$ does not split in $\mathcal{K}$ and $\pi_{q}$ is ramified},&\text{if $2$ splits in $\mathcal{K}$},\\
q=2,&\text{if $2$ does not split in $\mathcal{K}$}.\end{array}\right.

Let
D
be the quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
ramified exactly at
q
and
\infty
, and let
\pi^{D}
be the Jacquet–Langlands transfer of
\pi
to
D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.

We can take a square free positive integer
s
, coprime to
pD_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}
and all the primes inert in
\mathcal{K}
where
D
splits, such that
\mathrm{inv}_{v}(D)=(-\mathfrak{s},D_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}})_{v}
, where
(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})_{v}
is the Hilbert symbol. Define
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{zeta}{{{}}\zeta_{0}}=\delta\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{s}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
with
\delta
the totally imaginary element in
\mathcal{K}
with
\delta^{2}
a
p
-adic unit as fixed in
Notation
. This
\zeta_{0}
is a skew–Hermitian matrix. Define the unitary group
\U(2)
(resp. similitude unitary group
\GU(2)
) over
\mathbb{Z}
as: for all
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra
R
,

(5.4.1)

\displaystyle\U(2)(R)

\displaystyle=\{g\in\GL_{2}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):g\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\overline{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\overline{g}=\zeta_{0}\}

(5.4.2)

\displaystyle\GU(2)(R)

\displaystyle=\{g\in\GL_{2}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):g\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\overline{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\overline{g}=\nu(g)\zeta_{0},\,\nu(g)\in R^{\times}\}.

We have

D=\{g\in M_{2}(\mathcal{K}):g\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\bar{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{g}=\det(g)\zeta_{0}\}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&-\mathfrak{s}\bar{b}\\
b&\overline{a}\end{pmatrix}:a,b\in\mathcal{K}\right\},

and we view both
D^{\times}
and
\GU(2)
as subgroups of
\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\GL_{2}(\mathcal{K})
. Then the homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\times}\times D^{\times}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q}),

\displaystyle(a,g)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow ag

induces an isomorphism

(5.4.3)

\GU(2)\simeq(\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\times D^{\times})/\{(a,a^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{2}):a\in\mathbb{G}_{m}\}.

Given an automorphic form on
\phi
on
D^{\times}
with central character
\chi_{1}
, by picking an extension
\chi
of
\chi_{1}
to
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
, one obtains an automorphic form
\phi^{\GU(2)}
on
\GU(2)
by (
5.4.3
).

We denote by
π

D

the Jacquet–Langlands transfer of
\pi
to
D^{\times}
, and by
f

D

the unique automorphic form on
D^{\times}
which is a newform for the action of
D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\simeq\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
for all
v\neq q,\infty
and takes value
1
at
\mathbf{1}_{2}
. By our assumption,
\pi^{D}
has trivial central character. We can extend
f^{D}
to a form
f

GU
(
2
)

via (
5.4.3
) and the trivial character. We denote this form by
f^{\GU(2)}
, and denote by
π

GU
(
2
)

the automorphic representation of
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by
f^{\GU(2)}
.

Define the unitary group
\U(3,1)
(resp. similitude unitary group
\GU(3,1)
) over
\mathbb{Z}
as

\displaystyle\U(3,1)(R)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in\GL_{4}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):g\begin{pmatrix}&&1\\
&\zeta_{0}\\
-1\end{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\!\overline{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\overline{g}=\begin{pmatrix}&&1\\
&\zeta_{0}\\
-1\end{pmatrix}\right\},

\displaystyle\GU(3,1)(R)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in\GL_{4}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):g\begin{pmatrix}&&1\\
&\zeta_{0}\\
-1\end{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\!\overline{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\overline{g}=\nu(g)\begin{pmatrix}&&1\\
&\zeta_{0}\\
-1\end{pmatrix},\,\nu(g)\in R^{\times}\right\},

Let

(5.4.4)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{PU}{{{}}P_{\GU(3,1)}}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}x&\ast&\ast\\
&g&\ast\\
&&\nu(g)\bar{x}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in\GU(3,1):g\in\GU(2),\,\,x\in\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\right\},

the (standard) Klingen parabolic subgroup of
\GU(3,1)
. Its Levi subgroup is

M_{P_{\GU{3,1)}}}\simeq\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\GU(2).

We consider the Klingen Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,1)
inducing
\pi^{\GU(2)}\boxtimes\xi_{0}\tau_{0}|\cdot|^{s}_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}}
from
P_{\GU(3,1)}
.

Define the unitary group
\U(1)
over as

\U(1)(R)=\left\{a\in(R\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}})^{\times}:a\bar{a}=1\right\}.

The projection
\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathcal{K}_{p}=\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}
induces maps

\displaystyle\U(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

\displaystyle\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

\displaystyle\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}

and they are all isomorphisms. We denote the inverse maps by
ϱ

-
1

p

:

(5.4.5)

\displaystyle\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\U(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

\displaystyle\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

\displaystyle\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

5.5.
The Klingen Eisenstein series and the doubling method

We briefly recall the definition of Klingen Eisenstein series and Garrett’s (generalized) doubling method formula which expresses a Klingen Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,1)
as an integral involving a Siegel Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,3)
.

5.5.1.
The Klingen Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,1)

Let
U_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}
be the unipotent subgroup of the Klingen parabolic subgroup
P_{\GU(3,1)}
in (
5.4.4
). For a unitary character
\xi_{0}\tau_{0}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
and a complex number
s
, define
I_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
as the space of smooth
K
-finite functions (where
K
is a maximal compact subgroup of
\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
)

F(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}):U_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})M_{P_{\GU{3,1)}}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

satisfying

(1)

F(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\left(\begin{pmatrix}x\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&\overline{x}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}g\right)=\xi_{0}\tau_{0}(x)|x\bar{x}|^{s+\frac{3}{2}}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}
for all
x\in\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
,
g\in\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
,

(2)

for all
g\in\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, the function
g_{1}\mapsto F(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\left(\begin{pmatrix}1\\
&g_{1}\\
&&\nu(g_{1})\end{pmatrix}g\right)
is a cuspidal automorphic form on
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.

The Klingen Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,1)
attached to
F(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\in I_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
is defined as

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{EklingF}{{{}}E^{\mathrm{Kling}}(g;F(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))}=\sum_{\gamma\in P_{\GU(3,1)}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{Q})}F(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})(\gamma g).

If the cuspidal automorphic form in (ii) belongs to
\pi^{\GU(2)}
, the Galois representation attached to
E^{\mathrm{Kling}}(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,;F(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}
(where we assume that
\xi\tau
has
\infty
-type
(0,k)
) is

\xi\tau\cdot\epsilon^{-2}_{\mathrm{cyc}}\circ\mathrm{Nm}\,\oplus\,(\xi\tau)^{-c}\cdot\epsilon_{\mathrm{cyc}}\circ\mathrm{Nm}\,\oplus\,\rho_{\pi}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{K})},

where the Hecke characters of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
are viewed as characters of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{K})
. The congruences between this Klingen Eisenstein series and cuspidal forms on
\GU(3,1)
can be used to construct elements in the Selmer group for
\rho_{\pi}(\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{cyc}})|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{K})}\otimes\xi^{-1}\tau^{-1}
.

5.5.2.
The Siegel Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,3)

Let
\GU(3,3)
be the similitude unitary group over
\mathbb{Z}
defined by

\GU(3,3)(R)=\left\{g\in\GL_{6}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):g\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{3}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{3}\end{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\!\bar{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{g}=\nu(g)\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{3}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{3}\end{pmatrix},\,\nu(g)\in R^{\times}\right\}.

The group
\GU(3,1)\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GU(2)=\{(g_{1},g_{2})\in\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2):\nu(g_{1})=\nu(g_{2})\}
embeds into
H
by

(5.5.1)

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{imath}{{{}}\imath}:\GU(3,1)\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GU(2)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\GU(3,3)

\displaystyle(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}\\
&g_{2}\end{pmatrix}\mathcal{S},

where
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cS}{{{}}\mathcal{S}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
&&1\\
&-\mathbf{1}_{2}&&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}
.

Let
Q

GU
(
3
,
3
)

be the (standard) Siegel parabolic subgroup of
\GU(3,3)
. For a
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra
R
,

Q_{\GU(3,3)}(R)=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
0&D\end{pmatrix}\in\GL_{6}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):D=\nu{\vphantom{\!\bar{A}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{A}^{-1},\nu\in R^{\times},\,A^{-1}B\in\Her_{3}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}})\right\}.

For a place
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
, a unitary character
\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v}
of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}_{v}
and a complex number
s
, the degenerate principal series
I_{Q_{\GU(3,3)},v}(s,\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v})
is defined as the space consisting of smooth
\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{Z}_{v})
-finite functions
f_{v}(s,\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v}):\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

satisfying

\displaystyle f_{v}(s,\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v})\left(\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
0&D\end{pmatrix}g\right)=\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v}(\det A)|\det AD^{-1}|^{s+\frac{3}{2}}_{v}f(g)

for all
g\in\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{Q}_{v}),\,\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
0&D\end{pmatrix}\in Q_{\GU(3,3)}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
.

Given a unitary character
\xi_{0}\tau_{0}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
and a section
f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v}f_{v}(s,\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v})
inside
\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v}I_{Q_{\GU(3,3)},v}(s,\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v})
, the Siegel Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,3)
attached to
f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
is defined as

E

Sieg

(g;f(s,ξ

0

τ

0

))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{\GU(3,3)}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{Q})}f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})(\gamma g).

5.5.3.
The (generalized) doubling method formula

Theorem 5.5.1

(
[
Gar89
]
)
.

Let
\xi_{0}\tau_{0}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a unitary character. For a section
f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\in I_{Q_{\GU(3,3)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
and a cuspidal automorphic form
\varphi
on
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\big(\imath\left(g,g_{1}t\right);f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)\varphi\left(g_{1}t\right)(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}(\det g_{1}t)\,dg_{1}=E^{\mathrm{Kling}}\big(g;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big),

where
t
is an element of
\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
with
\nu(t)=\nu(g)
(and it is easy to see that the left hand side does not depend on the choice of
t
), and
F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)
is the section in
I_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
defined by

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Fvarphi}{{{}}F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)}(g)

\displaystyle=\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}f(s,\tau_{0})(\imath(g,g_{1}t))\,\varphi(g_{1}t)(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}(\det g_{1}t)\,dg_{1}.

5.6.
The auxiliary data for the Klingen family

Let
Σ
be the set of finite places of
\mathbb{Q}
containing the prime
2
, the prime
q
, the finite places
v\neq p
where
\pi
or
\xi
or
\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}
is ramified and the primes dividing
\mathfrak{s}
. We denote by
Σ

s

the set of primes in
\Sigma
split in
\mathcal{K}
and
Σ

ns

the set of primes in
\Sigma
non-split in
\mathcal{K}
.

The Klingen Eisenstein family we will construct is not of the optimal level. It depends on some auxiliary data which are chosen such that we can prove the desired properties of its non-degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients. In this subsection, we fix these auxiliary data. We first fixes places
\ell,\ell^{\prime}
and positive integers
c_{v}
for
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
. Then we choose Hecke characters
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
satisfying a list of conditions. Then we fix positive integers
c_{v}
for
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
. The Siegel Eisenstein family on
\GU(3,3)
we use for constructing the Klingen Eisenstein family depends on the choice of
\ell,\ell,c_{v}
,
v\in\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
, the automorphic form on
\GU(2)
to pair with the restriction to
\GU(3,1)\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GU(2)
of the Seigel Eisenstein family is chosen from the space (
5.6.12
) defined below which depends on
\chi_{h,v}
,
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
. The characters
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
will show up in our analysis of non-degenerate Fourier coefficients.

We fix:

–

primes
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\neq 2,p
such that
\ell
splits in
\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}
,
\ell^{\prime}
is inert in
\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}
, and
\pi_{\ell},\pi_{\ell^{\prime}}
are unramified,

–

for each place
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
, a positive integer

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cv}{{{}}c_{v}}>\max\{\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\lambda_{v}),\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\pi_{v})),3)\}+1.

Before choosing the auxiliary Hecke characters
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
, we need to first introduce a Proposition on certain Schwartz functions in the Schrödinger model of Weil representations of
\U(2,1)
. Let
V,V^{-}
(resp.
W_{\beta}
) be the two dimensional skew-Hermitian spaces (resp. one dimensional Hermitian space) over
\mathcal{K}
, and
e_{1},e_{2}\in V\otimes W_{\beta}
,
e^{-}_{1},e^{-}_{2}\in V^{-}\otimes W_{\beta}
as in §
7.1
. We have
\U(V\oplus V^{-})\cong\U(2,2)
and
\U(W_{\beta})\cong\U(1)
.

Proposition 5.6.1
.

Let
v
be a finite place of
\mathbb{Q}
non-split in
\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}
. Denote by
\omega_{\beta,v}(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,,\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})
the Weil representation of
\U(2,2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\times\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
on the Schrödinger model
\mathpzc{S}\left(\mathcal{K}_{v}(e_{1}+e^{-}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}_{v}(e_{2}+e^{-}_{2})\right)
(with respect to
\lambda_{W_{\beta}}=\lambda
). Let

\mathscr{T}:\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{2}\big)\longrightarrow\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathcal{K}_{v}(e_{1}+e^{-}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}_{v}(e_{2}+e^{-}_{2})\big)

be the intertwining map between Schrödinger models.

For
\beta=1
, there exists a character
\chi_{v}:\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
with

\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\chi_{v}))>\max\{\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\chi_{v}|_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}},\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\lambda_{v})),\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\pi_{v})),3\},

a Schwartz function
\phi_{1,v}
on
\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2}
, and
u_{1},\dots,u_{t}\in\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
,
b_{1},\dots,b_{t}\in\mathbb{C}
such that the function

y\longmapsto\sum_{j=1}^{t}b_{j}\int_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2}}\mathscr{T}^{-1}\left(\omega_{\beta,v}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&q^{-c_{v}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
0&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix},u_{j}\right)\Phi_{0,v}\right)(x,y)\cdot\phi_{1,v}(x)\,dx

is nonzero and belongs to the
\chi_{v}\lambda^{2}_{v}|_{\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}
-eignespace for the action of
\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
, where
\Phi_{0,v}
is the characteristic function of
\mathcal{O}_{K,v}\frac{e_{1}+e^{-}_{1}}{2}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},v}\frac{e_{2}+e^{-}_{2}}{2}
.

Proof.

[
Wan20
, Lemma 6.26]
.
∎

Now we fix auxiliary Hecke characters

–

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{chi}{{{}}\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of
\infty
-type
(0,0)
with
\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\mathrm{triv}
satisfying the following properties:

(1)

\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
are unramified away from
\Sigma\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
and
\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}
is unramified at
q
.

(2)

\chi_{\theta,\mathfrak{p}},\chi_{h,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}
are unramified, and
\chi_{h,\mathfrak{p}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}=\chi^{-1}_{\theta,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}=\xi_{0,\mathfrak{p}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}
.

(3)

For
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
,

\displaystyle\chi_{\theta,v}|_{\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}=\text{a $\chi_{v}$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:nschi}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\chi_{h,v}\chi^{c}_{\theta,v})),\,\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}((\lambda^{2}_{v}\chi_{h,v}\chi_{\theta,v}))>\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\pi_{v})),\text{ if $v\neq q$},

(4)

For
v=\mathfrak{v}\overline{\mathfrak{v}}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
,
\chi_{\theta,\mathfrak{v}}
is unramified, and

\mathrm{ord}_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}(\mathrm{cond}(\chi_{\theta,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}))\geq 2\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\pi_{v}))+2,

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{ord}_{\mathfrak{v}}\left(\mathrm{cond}((\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta})_{\mathfrak{v}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{ord}_{\mathfrak{v}}\left(\mathrm{cond}(\chi_{\theta,{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}})\right)-\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\pi_{v})),&\pi_{v}\text{ ramified,}\\
\mathrm{ord}_{\mathfrak{v}}\left(\mathrm{cond}(\chi_{\theta,{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}})\right)-1,&\pi_{v}\text{ unramified}.\end{array}\right.

(5)

If
q=\mathfrak{q}^{2}
is ramified in
\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}
,

(\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta})_{q}(\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}})=\chi_{q}(q),

where
\chi_{q}
is the unramified quadratic character of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}_{q}
such that
\pi_{q}\cong\text{Steinberg}\otimes\chi_{q}
, and
\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a uniformizer of
\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

(6)

Furthermore, the value

(5.6.1)

\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{4}\pi^{-3}\cdot\gamma_{p}\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}\times\left(\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\right)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)^{-1}L\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\right)

is a
p
-adic unit, and

(5.6.2)

L^{q}\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\right)\neq 0.

For some algebraic Hecke character
\tau:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{k}(2\pi i)^{1-k}\Gamma(k-1)\cdot\gamma_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(\frac{k-2}{2},\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}^{c}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)^{-1}L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{k-2}{2},\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}^{c}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right),

(5.6.4)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{k-2}(2\pi i)^{2-k}\Gamma(k-2)\cdot L_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\frac{k-2}{2},\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\xi^{c}_{0}\tau^{c}_{0}\right)L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{k-2}{2},\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\xi^{c}_{0}\tau^{c}_{0}\right),

(5.6.5)

\displaystyle 1-(\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}^{c}\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{q}(q)q^{-\frac{k-2}{2}},

are all

-adic units.

5.8.
p
-adic measures and
p
-adic families of automorphic forms

For a
p
-adically complete
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-module
M
and a compact abelian group
Y
with totally disconnected topology, denote by
\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)
the space of
M
-valued
p
-adic measures on
Y
. Given
\mu\in\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)
and a continuous function
\eta:Y\rightarrow R
with
R
a
p
-adically complete
\mathcal{O}_{L}
algebra, we write

\mu(\eta)=\int_{Y}\eta(y)\,d\mu(y).

to denote the value of
\mu
at
\eta
, which is an element in
R\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}M
.

For each
y\in Y
, we have the delta measure
\delta_{y}\in\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)
defined by

(5.8.1)

\delta_{y}(\eta)=\eta(y).

Given
M_{1},M_{2}
and
\mu_{i}\in\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M_{i})
,
i=1,2
, the convolution
\mu_{1}\ast\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M_{1}\widehat{\otimes}M_{2})
is defined by

(5.8.2)

(\mu_{1}\ast\mu_{2})(\eta)=\int_{Y}\int_{Y}\eta(yz)\,d\mu_{1}(y)\,d\mu_{2}(z),

If
\chi
is a continuous character of
Y
valued in
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\bar{L}}
, then

(5.8.3)

(\mu_{1}\ast\mu_{2})(\chi)=\mu_{1}(\chi)\otimes\mu_{2}(\chi).

Let
A
be a group with a homomorphism
A\rightarrow Y
and an action on
M
. Then the group
A
acts on
\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)
in two ways:

–

A
acts on
\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)
via its action on
M
,

–

A
acts on
\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)
via the homomorphism
A\rightarrow Y
and the translation of
Y
on itself.

Define

(5.8.4)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{MeasYM}{{{}}\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)^{\natural}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{$p$-adic measures in $\mathcal{M}eas(Y,M)$ on which}\\
\text{the two actions of $A$ are compatible}\end{array}\right\}.

In our applications,
Y
will be taken to be
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
or
U_{\mathcal{K},p}=1+p\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},p}
,
A
will be taken to be
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
, which we identify with
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}
via
(\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}},\varrho_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}})
, and
M
will be taken to be either
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}
or spaces of
p
-adic forms on unitary groups. Next, we introduce the spaces of
p
-adic families of automorphic forms we will use.

5.8.1.
p
-adic families on
\GU(3,1)

Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{VGU31}{{{}}V_{\GU(3,1)}}=\varprojlim\limits_{m}\varinjlim\limits_{n}V_{n,m}
(defined in §
2.6
). In (
5.8.4
), put
Y=\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
,

with the homomorphism

(5.8.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\simeq\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}=Y,}
natural
embedding
natural
projection

and
M=V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}
, the component of
V_{\GU(3,1)}
on which the kernel of (
5.8.5
), acts through the character
\xi_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
. We make

act on

by the usual action of

\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&1\\
&&a_{1}\\
&&&a_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

on
V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}
multiplied by the scalar
\xi^{-1}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic},p}(a_{1},a_{2})
. (Here
\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the isomorphism in (
5.4.5
), and this action factors through the quotient of
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
by the kernel of (
5.8.5
).) Then we get

(5.8.6)

\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\right)^{\natural},

the
\xi_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
-component of the space of
p
-adic families on
\GU(3,1)
. The compatibility of the two actions of
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
implies that the value at
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
of an element in (
5.8.6
) is a
p
-adic form with
p
-adic nebentypus

\left(\mathrm{triv},\mathrm{triv},(\xi\tau)_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic},\mathfrak{p}},(\xi\tau)^{-1}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic},\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\right).

(By saying
p
-adic nebentypus, we refer to the usual action of
\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&a_{2}&\ast&\ast\\
&&a_{3}&\ast\\
&&&a_{4}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on
V_{\GU(3,1)}
.)

The space (
5.8.6
) contains the subspace of semi-ordinary families:

(5.8.7)

\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},e_{\mathrm{so}}V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)^{\natural},

which is naturally equipped with an
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
-module structure. The Klingen Eisenstein family we will construct belongs to this space. (It is tautological that an element in (
5.8.7
) gives rise to an element in
\xi_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
-component of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket=\Hom_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket}\left(\mathpzc{V}^{*}_{\mathrm{so}},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\right)
, see §
8.1
.)

5.9.
The
p
-adic family of Siegel Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,3)

We normalize the Siegel Eisenstein series
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}
(with
f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
chosen as in §
5.7
) as

(5.9.1)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Enorl}{{{}}E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}}=\left(\frac{2^{-6}(-2\pi i)^{3k}}{\pi^{3}\prod_{j=0}^{2}\Gamma(k-j)}\right)^{-1}d^{\,\Sigma\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{3}(s,\tau_{0})\cdot E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}},

where

(5.9.2)

\displaystyle d^{\,\Sigma\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{3}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})

\displaystyle=\prod_{v\notin\Sigma\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}d_{3,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{d3v}{{{}}d_{3,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{3}L_{v}\left(2s+j,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\eta^{3-j}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right).

Theorem 5.9.1
.

There exists a
p
-adic measure
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{bESieg}{{{}}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}}\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\GU(3,3)}\right)
such that

\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})=E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}

for all algebraic Hecke character
\tau:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
such that its
p
-adic avatar
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
factors through
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
and
\xi\tau
has
\infty
-type
\left(0,k\right)
with
k\geq 6
even.

Proof.

The measure is constructed by interpolating the
q
-expansions of the
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}
’s. First, we record the formulas for the Fourier coefficients in
[
Wan20
, §§6E-6H]
. Given
\beta\in\Her_{3}(\mathcal{K})
and
g\in\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, let

W_{\beta,v}\big(g_{v},f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)=\int_{\Her_{3}(\mathcal{K}_{v})}f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\left(\begin{pmatrix}&-\mathbf{1}_{3}\\
\mathbf{1}_{3}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{3}&\varsigma\\
0&\mathbf{1}_{3}\end{pmatrix}g_{v}\right)\mathbf{e}_{v}(-\mathrm{Tr}\,\beta\varsigma)\,d\varsigma

(where
\mathbf{e}_{v}
is the additive character in (
1.0.5
)), and

E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}\big(g;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)=\int_{\Her_{3}(\mathcal{K}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}})}E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{3}&\varsigma\\
0&\mathbf{1}_{3}\end{pmatrix}g;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\right)\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(-\mathrm{Tr}\,\beta\varsigma)\,d\varsigma.

Because the sections at
v\in\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
are chosen to be supported on the big cell, for
g\in Q_{\GU(3,3)}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, we have

E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}\big(g;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)=\prod_{v}W_{\beta,v}\big(g_{v},f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big).

For our choice of
f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
in §
5.7
, the formulas for
W_{\beta,v}\big(g_{v},f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)
are as follows.

The archimedean place.

\displaystyle\left.W_{\beta,\infty}\left(\begin{pmatrix}y&x{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}^{-1}\\
0&{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}^{-1}\end{pmatrix};f_{\infty}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\right)\right|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{2^{-6}(-2\pi i)^{3k}}{\pi^{3}\prod_{j=0}^{2}\Gamma(k-j)}(\det\bar{y})^{k}\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\mathrm{Tr}\,\beta(x+iy{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y})\right)\cdot(\det\beta)^{k-3},&\beta\in\Her_{3}(\mathcal{K})_{>0}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

v\notin\Sigma\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
. For
\det\beta\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\left.W_{\beta,v}\left(\mathrm{diag}(A,D);f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\right)\right|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}

\displaystyle=\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v}(\det D)|\det AD^{-1}|^{-\frac{k}{2}+3}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\cdot d_{3,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathds{1}_{\Her_{3}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},v})^{*}}(D^{-1}\beta A)\cdot h_{v,D^{-1}\beta A}\left(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0,v}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0,v}(q_{v})q_{v}^{-2s-3}\right),

where
h_{v,D^{-1}\beta A}\in\mathbb{Z}[X]
is a monic polynomial depending only on
v
and
D^{-1}\beta A
, and is the constant polynomial when
when
D^{-1}\beta A
belongs to
\GL_{3}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},v})
.

v\in\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
.

\displaystyle\left.W_{\beta,v}\left(\mathrm{diag}(A,D);f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\right)\right|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}

\displaystyle=|D_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}|^{3/2}_{v}\cdot\xi_{0,v}\tau_{0,v}(\det D)|\det AD^{-1}|^{-\frac{k}{2}+3}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathds{1}_{\Her_{3}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},v})^{*}}(D^{-1}\beta A)\,\mathbf{e}_{v}\left(\frac{(D^{-1}\beta A)_{22}+(D^{-1}\beta A)_{33}}{q^{c_{v}}_{v}}\right).

The place
p
.
An easy computation shows that

\displaystyle\left.W_{\beta,p}\left(\mathbf{1}_{6};f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}(-s,\xi^{-c}_{0}\tau^{-c}_{0})\right)\right|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\alpha_{\xi\tau,p})(\beta)=\alpha_{\xi\tau,p}(\beta).

The functional equation for
W_{\beta,p}
in
[
LR05
, (14)]
implies that

W_{\beta,p}\left(g;M_{p}\left(-s,\xi^{-c}_{0}\tau^{-c}_{0}\right)f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}(-s,\xi^{-1}_{0}\tau^{-1}_{0})\right)=c_{3,p}(-s,\xi^{-c}_{0}\tau^{-c}_{0},\beta)\cdot W_{\beta,p}\left(g;f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}(-s,\xi^{-c}_{0}\tau^{-c}_{0})\right)

with
c_{3,p}(-s,\xi^{-c}_{0}\tau^{-c}_{0},\beta)
defined as in (
5.7.5
). It follows that

\displaystyle\left.W_{\beta,p}\big(\mathbf{1};f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)\right|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}=c_{\beta,p}\cdot(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})_{p}(\det\beta)|\det\beta|^{2s}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\cdot\alpha_{\xi\tau,p}(\beta).

Denote by
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau,\beta}\big(\mathrm{diag}(A,D)\big)
the
\beta
-th coefficient in the (
p
-adic)
q
-expansion of
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}
at the (
0
-dimensional) cusp indexed by
\mathrm{diag}(A,D)\in\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathcal{K},f})
. From the above formulas for the local Fourier coefficients at the archimedean place, we see that
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau,\beta}\big(\mathrm{diag}(A,D)\big)
is nonzero only for
\beta
inside

\mathfrak{S}_{3}=\left\{\beta\in\Her_{3}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}})_{>0}:\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{21})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p},\,\begin{pmatrix}\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{21})&\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{22})\\
\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{31})&\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{32})\end{pmatrix}\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\right\},

and for
\beta\in\mathfrak{S}_{3}
we have

(5.9.3)

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau,\beta}\big(\mathrm{diag}(A,D)\big)

\displaystyle=\text{a constant independent of $\tau$}

\displaystyle\times(\xi\tau)_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}(\det D)\prod_{v\notin\Sigma\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}h_{v,{\vphantom{\!\bar{A}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{A}\beta A}\left((\xi\tau)^{\mathbb{Q}}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic},v}(q_{v})\right),\quad

\displaystyle\times(\xi\tau)_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic},\mathfrak{p}}\left(\det\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{21}&\beta_{22}\\
\beta_{31}&\beta_{32}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\right)(\xi\tau)^{\mathbb{Q}}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic},p}(\det\beta)

(Note that thanks to the condition that
\xi\tau
has
\infty
-type
(0,k)
, we have
(\xi\tau)_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic},\mathfrak{p}}=(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
(\xi\tau)_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}=\xi_{0}\tau_{0}|_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}}
.)

The factors on the right hand side of (
5.9.3
) are interpolated by
\delta
-measures in
\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\right)
. The convolution of those
\delta
-measures gives an element in
\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\mathcal{O}_{L}\right)
which interpolates the Fourier coefficient
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau,\beta}\big(\mathrm{diag}(A,D)\big)
. (See (
5.8.1
)(
5.8.2
)(
5.8.3
) for the definitions of
\delta
-measures and the convolution of
p
-adic measures.) We see that there exists a
p
-adic measure

\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\in\mathcal{M}eas\Big(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\bigoplus\limits_{\text{$p$-adic cusps}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\mathfrak{S}_{3}\rrbracket\Big)

that interpolates the
p
-adic
q
-expansions of
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}
. By the
q
-expansion principle and the fact that the
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}
’s are automorphic forms on
\GU(3,3)
, we deduce that
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}
belongs to
\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\GU(3,3)}\right)
. (Here we view the space of
p
-adic measures valued in
V_{\GU(3,3)}
as a subspace of the space of
p
-adic measures valued in copies of
\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\mathfrak{S}\rrbracket
via the embedding of
V_{\GU(3,3)}
into the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-module of
q
-expansions.)
∎

5.10.
The semi-ordinary family of Klingen Eisenstein series

Let
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}\big|_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}
be the automorphic form obtained by the composition of the restriction from
\GU(3,3)
to
\GU(3,1)\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GU(2)
via the embedding (
5.5.1
) and the extension by zero from
\GU(3,1)\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GU(2)
to
\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)
. Define the twsist
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}\big|^{\star}_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}
as

E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}\big|^{\star}_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})=E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}\big|_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\xi^{-1}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\tau^{-1}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}(\det g_{2}).

Then by our choice of the section
f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
, we see that

E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}\big|^{\star}_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}\in V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\otimes M_{\GU(2)}\left(K^{p}_{f}K_{p,0};\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)

with
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Kp0}{{{}}K_{p,0}}=\left\{g\in\GU(2)(\mathbb{Z}_{p}):\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(g)=\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
p\ast&\ast\end{pmatrix}\right\}
. (Note that
K_{p,0}
equals the
K^{\prime}_{p,g}
in (
4.4.1
). See (
5.6.9
) for the definition of
M_{\GU(2)}\left(K^{p}_{f}K_{p,0};\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)
.)

Therefore, by applying a twist of
(\xi\tau)^{-1}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\circ\,\det
to the family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\big|_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}
on the second factor, we obtain the
p
-adic family

(5.10.1)

\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\big|^{\star}_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\right)^{\natural}\widehat{\otimes}\,M_{\GU(2)}\left(K^{p}_{f}K_{p,0};\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)

interpolating
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\xi\tau}\big|^{\star}_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)}
when
\tau
varies. (To see that it belongs to the
♮
-subspace, one just needs to check the nebentypus.)

Attached to a
\varphi
in the space (
5.6.12
) is a linear functional

\displaystyle\left<\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}},\varphi\right>:M_{\GU(2)}\left(K^{p}_{f}K_{p,0};\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left<\varphi^{\prime},\varphi\right>=\int_{\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})}\varphi^{\prime}(g)\varphi(g)\,dg,

where the integral is understood as a sum over the finite set
\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})/K^{p}_{f}K_{p,0}
. Applying the linear functional
\left<\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}},\varphi\right>
to (
5.10.1
) gives a family

(5.10.2)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{bEKling}{{{}}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}}=\left<\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}|^{\star}_{\GU(3,1)\times\GU(2)},\,\varphi\right>\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)^{\natural}.

Proposition 5.10.1
.

For
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
satisfying the conditions in Theorem
5.9.1
, the evaluation of the family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
at
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
is a Klingen Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,1)
inducing
\xi_{0}\tau_{0}|\cdot|^{\frac{k-3}{2}}\boxtimes\pi^{\GU(2)}
, more precisely, the automorphic form

(5.10.3)

g\longmapsto\left.\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})/\U(2)(\mathbb{R})}E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\big(\imath(g,g_{2});f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)\varphi(g_{2})(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}(\det g_{2})\,dg_{2}\,\right|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}

normalized by the factor in (
5.9.1
).

Moreover,
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},e_{\mathrm{so}}V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)^{\natural}
, i.e. is a semi-ordinary family.

Proof.

From the construction of the family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
, it is easy to see that its evaluation
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
satisfying the conditions in Theorem
5.9.1
equals the form given in (
5.10.3
) normalized by the factor in (
5.9.1
). By the doubling method formula (Theorem
5.5.1
), we know that (
5.10.3
) is a Klingen Eisenstein series inducing
\xi_{0}\tau_{0}|\cdot|^{\frac{k-3}{2}}\boxtimes\pi^{\GU(2)}
.

In order to show that
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
is a semi-ordinary family, for all
\tau
satisfying the conditions in Theorem
5.9.1
and
(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}
ramified, we look at the action of
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators on the form (
5.10.3
), which has weight
(0,0,0;k)
. Given integers
m,m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
, by the formula for adelic
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators in (
2.8.7
) and our choice of the local section at
p
in §
5.7
