, the action of
(U^{+}_{p,2})^{m}(U^{+}_{p,3})^{m^{\prime}}(U^{-}_{p,1})^{m^{\prime\prime}}
on the form (
5.10.3
) can be computed by considering

(5.10.4)

p^{2m+km^{\prime\prime}}\int_{N(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}\left(\frac{3-k}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(gu\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{m+m^{\prime}}\\
&p^{m+m^{\prime}}\\
&&p^{m^{\prime}}\\
&&&p^{-m^{\prime\prime}}\\
&&&&1\\
&&&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\,du,

where we identify
\U(3,3)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
\GL_{6}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
via
\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathcal{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Writing
g=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}
in
3\times 3
blocks, we have

(5.10.5)

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Eso-1}=

\displaystyle p^{2m+km^{\prime\prime}}\cdot\left((\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}(p)p^{3}\right)^{2m+3m^{\prime}}\left((\xi\tau)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)p^{3}\right)^{m^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\times p^{-4m-3m^{\prime}-3m^{\prime\prime}}\sum_{u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+m^{\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}}}\\
v_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m^{\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\alpha_{\xi\tau,p})\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\\
&p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\\
&&p^{-m^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&-u_{1}\\
&1&-u_{2}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(C^{-1}D+\left(\begin{smallmatrix}v_{1}&0&0\\
v_{2}&0&0\\
v_{3}&0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-m^{\prime\prime}}\\
&1\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right).

A direct computation by using the formula (
5.7.2
) shows that when
(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}
is ramified

(5.10.6)

\displaystyle\sum_{u_{1},u_{2}}\sum_{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\alpha_{\xi\tau,p})\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\\
&p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\\
&&p^{-m^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&-u_{1}\\
&1&-u_{2}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(C^{-1}D+\left(\begin{smallmatrix}v_{1}&0&0\\
v_{2}&0&0\\
v_{3}&0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-m^{\prime\prime}}\\
&1\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p^{2m}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\alpha_{\xi\tau,p})\left(C^{-1}D\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\\
&&p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right).

Combining (
5.10.5
) and (
5.10.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Eso-1}=

\displaystyle\,p^{6m+6m^{\prime}}(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}(p)^{2m+3m^{\prime}}\left((\xi\tau)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)p^{k}\right)^{m^{\prime\prime}}\cdot\left(\xi\tau)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)p^{3}\right)^{-2m-2m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}\left(\frac{3-k}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{4}\\
&p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}(p)^{m^{\prime}}\left((\xi\tau)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)p^{k}\right)^{m^{\prime\prime}}\cdot(\xi\tau)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\xi\tau)^{-1}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)^{-2m-2m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}\left(\frac{3-k}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{4}\\
&p^{-m-m^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)

and the corresponding Siegel Eisenstein series paired with
\varphi\cdot(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}\circ\det
over
\U(2)
gives

\displaystyle(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}(p)^{m^{\prime}}\left((\xi\tau)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)p^{k}\right)^{m^{\prime\prime}}\cdot\text{the automorphic form given in \eqref{eq:Kling-int}}.

Here we use the condition that
\varphi
has trivial central character. Both
(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}(p)
and
(\xi\tau)_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(p)p^{k}
are
p
-adic units, so the form (
5.10.3
) is an eigenvector for the action of
(U^{+}_{p,2})^{m}(U^{+}_{p,3})^{m^{\prime}}(U^{-}_{p,1})^{m^{\prime\prime}}
with eigenvalue a
p
-adic unit. This shows that the evaluations of the Klingen Eisenstein family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
at all
\tau
satisfying the conditions in Theorem
5.9.1
with
(\xi\tau)_{\mathfrak{p}}
ramified are semi-ordinary. Hence,
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
is semi-ordinary.
∎

6.
The degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of the Klingen family

This section is devoted to proving Theorem
6.1.1
, which states that the degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
are divisible by the
p
-adic
L
-function attached to
\mathrm{BC}(\pi)
.

6.1.
The divisibility of the degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients by
p
-adic
L
-functions

Given a cusp label
g\in C(K^{p}_{f})
, we have the map

\Phi_{g}:\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},e_{\mathrm{so}}V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)^{\natural}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},M_{\GU(2)}\left(K^{p}_{f,g}K_{p,0};\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\right),

(which corresponds to the map
\Phi_{g}
in the fundamental exact sequence in part (4) of Theorem
2.9.1
). Here
K^{p}_{f,g}
is an open subgroup of
\GU(2)(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p})
depending on the cusp label
g\in C(K^{p}_{f})
. Applying it to our Klingen Eisenstein family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
, we get

\Phi_{g}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\right)\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},M_{\GU(2)}\left(K^{p}_{f,g}K_{p,0};\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\right)

Evaluating it at
g^{\prime}\in\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})
gives

\Phi_{g}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\right)(g^{\prime})\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\simeq\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket.

By the work of Kubota–Leopoldt on
p
-adic
L
-functions for Dirichlet characters, there exists a (unique)
p
-adic
L
-function
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Lxi}{{{}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}}\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
satisfying the interpolation property:

(6.1.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\Gamma(k-2)}{(2\pi i)^{k-2}}\cdot\gamma_{p}\left(3-k,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)\cdot L^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\left(k-2,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)

for all algebraic Hecke character
\tau:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
such that
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
factors through
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
and
\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\infty}=\mathrm{sgn}^{k}|\cdot|^{k}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

By
[
EW16
]
(and the archimedean computation in
[
EL
]
), there exists a (unique)
p
-adic
L
-function

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Lpipartial}{{{}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}}\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
satisfying the interpolation property: for all algebraic Hecke character
\tau:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
such that
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
factors through
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
and
\xi\tau
has
\infty
-type
\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)
with
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
k_{1}\leq 0
,
k_{2}\geq 2-k_{1}
,

(6.1.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\xi}\cdot\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{2(k_{2}-k_{1})}\frac{\Gamma(k_{2})\Gamma(k_{2}-1)}{(2\pi i)^{2k_{2}-1}}\cdot\gamma_{p}\left(\frac{3-(k_{1}+k_{2})}{2},\pi^{\vee}_{p}\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times L^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\left(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}-1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right),

with
C^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\xi}
a nonzero number independent of
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
.

Theorem 6.1.1
.

Suppose that
\varphi
belongs to the space (
5.6.12
). Then for all cusp labels
g\in C(K^{p}_{f})
and
g^{\prime}\in\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})
, we have

\left(\Phi_{g}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\right)(g^{\prime})\right)\subset\left(\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right)

in
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1.1
.

For
\tau
as in Theorem
5.9.1
, let
E^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\xi\tau}
be the classical Klingen Eisenstein series whose corresponding
p
-adic form is
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})
. The theorem is proved by computing
\Phi_{g}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\right)(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})
with

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=k_{\Sigma,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}=\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}k_{v},

\displaystyle k_{v}\in\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Z}_{v}),

which is essentially the
0
-th Fourier–Jacobi coefficient of the
E^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\xi\tau}
at

x=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&g^{\prime}\\
&&\nu(g^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)k_{\Sigma_{f},\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\,w_{3,p},

with
w_{3,p}\in\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
defined by

\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(w_{3,p})=\begin{pmatrix}&&1\\
1\\
&1\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}.

Note that
w_{3,p}\in\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is also our fixed element in
P^{\flat}_{\tt D}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for obtaining the isomorphism (
4.4.7
)).

By the description of
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})
in (
5.10.3
) and the doubling method formula (Theorem
5.5.1
), we have

E^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\xi\tau}(x)=\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{normalization factor}\\
\text{on the RHS of \eqref{eq:normalization}}\end{array}\cdot E^{\mathrm{Kling}}\big(x;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)\,\Big|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}

with
F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)
the section in
I_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
given by

\displaystyle F(f(s,\tau_{0}),\varphi)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})(\imath(x,g_{1}t))\varphi(g_{1}t)(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}(\det g_{1}t)\,dg_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\left(\imath\left(k_{\Sigma_{f},\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\,w_{3,p},g_{1}\right)\right)\varphi(g^{\prime}g_{1})(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}(\det g_{1})\,dg_{1},

where
t
is an element in
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
with
\nu(t)=\nu(x)
. What we need to compute is

(6.1.3)

\int_{U_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(\mathbb{Q})\big\backslash U_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}E^{\mathrm{Kling}}\big(ux;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)\,du.

It follows from
[
MgW94
, II.1.7]
that

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:const1}=F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)(x)+M_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)(x).

The archimedean computation in
[
Wan15
, Corollary 5.11]
shows that for our choice of
f_{\infty}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
in §
5.7
and
k\geq 6
,

(6.1.4)

\left.M_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\right|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}=0.

We reduce to compute
F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)(x)
. Take arbitrary
\varphi^{\prime}\in\bar{\pi}^{\GU(2)}
spherical away from
\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
and factorizable with respect to an isomorphism
\bar{\pi}^{\GU(2)}\simeq\bigotimes_{v}\bar{\pi}^{\GU(2)}_{v}
. Put

\displaystyle\left<F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,k_{\Sigma_{f},\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\,w_{3,p}),\varphi^{\prime}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q})Z_{\GU(2)}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)(g^{\prime}\,k_{\Sigma,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\,w_{3,p})\,\varphi^{\prime}(g^{\prime})\,dg^{\prime}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\left<F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,k_{\Sigma,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\,w_{3,p}),\,\varphi^{\prime}\right>=

\displaystyle\,\prod_{v\not\in\Sigma\,\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}Z_{v}\left(f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\mathbf{1}_{4},\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}Z_{v}\left(f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),k_{v},\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right)\cdot Z_{p}\left(f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),w_{3,p},\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right),

where for
g_{v}\in\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
,

(6.1.5)

\displaystyle Z_{v}\left(f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),g_{v},\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})(\imath(g_{v},g_{1}))(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{v}(\det g_{1})\left<\pi^{\GU(2)}_{v}(g_{1})\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right>\,dg_{1}.

The restriction of
f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,\imath(g_{v},\mathbf{1}_{2}))
to
\U(2,2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
is a section in the degenerate principal series of
\U(2,2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
inducing
\xi_{0}\tau_{0}|\cdot|^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}}}
. Hence the integral (
6.1.5
) is essentially a doubling zeta integral. We have the following formulas.

The archimedean place.

\displaystyle Z_{\infty}\left(f_{\infty}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\mathbf{1}_{4},\varphi_{\infty},\varphi^{\prime}_{\infty}\right)=\left<\varphi_{\infty},\varphi^{\prime}_{\infty}\right>,

because
f_{\infty}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})(\imath(\mathbf{1}_{4},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})
,
\varphi_{\infty}
,
\varphi^{\prime}_{\infty}
are all invariant under
\U(2)(\mathbb{R})
.

v\notin\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime},p\}
.

\displaystyle Z_{v}\left(f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\mathbf{1}_{4},\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right)=d_{2,v}\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)^{-1}\cdot L_{v}\left(s+1,\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)

with

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{d2v}{{{}}d_{2,v}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)}=\prod_{j=1}^{2}L_{v}(2s+1+j,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\eta^{2-j}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}).

This follows from the standard formula for unramified local doubling zeta integrals
[
LR05
, Proposition 3, Remark 3]
.

v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
. By
[
Wan20
, Lemma 6.20]
or an easy direct computation, one can see that for
\varphi
in the space (
5.6.12
),

Z_{v}\left(f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),k_{v},\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right)=0,\quad\text{if }k_{v}\notin P_{\U(3,1)}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})\begin{pmatrix}&&1\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
-1\end{pmatrix}P_{\U(3,1)}(\mathbb{Z}_{v}),

and for
k_{v}
such that
Z_{v}\left(f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),k_{v},\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right)\neq 0
,

\displaystyle Z_{v}\left(f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),k_{v},\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right)=\mathrm{vol}(\mathfrak{Y}_{c_{v}})\cdot\left((\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})_{v}(q_{v})|q_{v}|^{s+\frac{3}{2}}_{v}\right)^{-c_{v}}\left<\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\right>,

where
\mathfrak{Y}_{c_{v}}
is an open subgroup of
\{k^{\prime}_{v}\in\U(2)(\mathbb{Z}_{v}):k^{\prime}_{v}\equiv\mathbf{1}_{2}\mod q^{c_{v}}_{v}\}
, and
c_{v}
is the positive integer in the definition of
f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
in (
5.7.1
).

The place
p
.

(6.1.6)

\displaystyle Z_{p}\left(f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),w_{3,p},\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{p}\left(-2s,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-s,\pi_{p}\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)\left<\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right>

See §
6.2
for the computation. Note that we only need the value at
w_{3,p}\in\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
rather than a general
k_{p}\in\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. This significantly simplifies the computation. The value at a general
k_{p}\in\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
can be difficult to compute since intertwining operators are not easy to compute completely.

Combing the local formulas, since
\varphi^{\prime}
is arbitrary, we see that for
\tau
as in Theorem
5.9.1
,
F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,k_{\Sigma,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\,w_{3,p})\big|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}
is either
0
or equals

\displaystyle d^{\,\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{3}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}\cdot C_{\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\cdot\gamma_{p}\left(-2s,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)L^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\left(2s+1,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\times\gamma_{p}\left(-s,\pi_{p}\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)L^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,\ell,\ell^{\prime},p\}}\left(s+1,\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)\Big|_{s=\frac{k-3}{2}}\cdot\varphi

for some
C_{\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
independent of
\tau
. Combining this formula with the normalization (
5.9.1
), we see that

\Phi_{g}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\right)(g^{\prime})
, for all
g^{\prime}\in\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})
, is divisible by an element in
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
whose value equals that of
\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\xi,\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}
at all
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
as in Theorem
5.9.1
. Evaluations at those
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
’s already uniquely determine elements in
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
. Therefore, it follows that
\Phi_{g}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\right)(g^{\prime})
is divisible by
\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
.

∎

6.2.
The computation of local zeta integrals at
p

The ramification conditions in
[
Wan20
, Definition 6.30]
on
\xi_{0,p}\tau_{0,p}
and the characters from which
\pi_{p}
is induced are not satisfied in our case here because our
\pi_{p}
is unramified. Those conditions are used
loc.cit
to simplify the computation involving the intertwining operators. Here, we use the functional equation for doubling zeta integrals to handle the intertwining operator and compute
Z_{p}\left(f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),w_{3,p},\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right)
.

Proof of (
6.1.6
).

Inside the degenerated principal series on
\GU(2,2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
inducing the character
(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}_{p}|\cdot|^{-s}
, we define the section
f_{p}^{\U(2,2)}(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c})
as

\displaystyle f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c})\left(g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}\right)=|\det C{\vphantom{\!\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{C}|^{s-1}_{p}(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{p}\left(\det C\right)\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}\alpha_{\xi\tau,p}\begin{pmatrix}0&C^{-1}D\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},

where the Schwartz function
\alpha_{\xi\tau,p}
is defined in (
5.7.2
). Then we have

(6.2.1)

\displaystyle\int_{y_{1}\in\mathbb{Q}_{p},y_{2}\in\mathcal{K}^{2}_{p}}f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c})\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&&-1\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
1\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-y_{2}&y_{1}&\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&\\
&&{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\jmath(g_{2})\Upsilon^{-1}_{p}\imath(w_{3,p},\mathbf{1}_{2})\Upsilon_{p}\right)\,dy_{1}dy_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)(g_{2}),

where
\jmath:\U(2,2)\rightarrow\U(3,3)
is defined as

\jmath\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
&A&&B\\
&&1\\
&C&&D\end{pmatrix}.

To see (
6.2.1
), writing
g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}
, we have

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&&-1\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
1\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-y_{2}&y_{1}&\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&\\
&&{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\jmath(g_{2})\Upsilon^{-1}_{p}\imath(w_{3,p},\mathbf{1}_{2})\Upsilon_{p}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&-1&0\\
0&A&0&B\\
1&-y_{2}A&y_{1}&-y_{2}B\\
0&C&{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&D\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&1\\
&&&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}=\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&-1&0\\
A&0&0&B\\
-y_{2}A&1&y_{1}&-y_{2}B\\
C&0&{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&D\end{pmatrix}

and

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}-y_{2}A&1\\
C&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{}^{-1}y_{1}&-y_{2}B\\
{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&D\end{pmatrix}\right)=\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}\begin{pmatrix}C^{-1}{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&C^{-1}D\\
y_{1}+y_{2}AC^{-1}{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&y_{2}{\vphantom{\!\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{C}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

It follows that the integrand of the left hand side of (
6.2.1
) equals

\displaystyle f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s+\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)(g_{2})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(y_{1})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{2}_{\mathcal{K},p}}(y_{2}{\vphantom{\!\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{C}^{-1}),

so

\text{LHS of \eqref{eq:fU22}}=f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s+\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)(g_{2})\cdot\det|C{\vphantom{\!\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{C}|_{p}=f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)(g_{2}).

Put
\mathcal{S}_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}1&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-1&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}
and
\Upsilon_{0,p}
to be the element in
\U(2,2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(\Upsilon_{0,p})=\mathcal{S}^{-1}_{0}
. Set

(6.2.2)

\displaystyle\imath_{0}:\U(2)\times\U(2)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\U(2,2),

\displaystyle\imath_{0}(g_{1},g^{\prime}_{1})=\mathcal{S}^{-1}_{0}\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}\\
&g^{\prime}_{1}\end{pmatrix}.

By the definition of the intertwining operator
M_{p}(-s,\xi\tau^{-1}_{0}(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c})
(in (
5.7.4
)) and (
6.2.1
),

\displaystyle M_{p}(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c})f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c})\left(\imath(w_{3,p},g_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y_{1}&y_{2}\\
{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&y_{4}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Her_{3}(\mathcal{K}_{p})}f^{\text{big-cell}}_{p}(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c})\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&&-1\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
1\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-y_{2}&y_{1}&\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&\\
&&{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 120.00018pt\left.\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&&&-\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&1\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&&y_{4}\\
&&1\\
&&&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\imath(w_{3,p},g_{1})\Upsilon_{p}\right)\,dudy_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,M^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}(\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(\imath_{0}(1,g_{1})\Upsilon_{0,p}\right),

and

\displaystyle f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\left(\imath(w_{3,p},g_{1})\right)=

\displaystyle\,\gamma_{p}\left(-2s,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-2s-1,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-2s-2,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times M^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}(\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(\imath_{0}(1,g_{1})\Upsilon_{0,p}\right).

By the functional equation of the local doubling zeta integral
[
LR05
, (19)(25)]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}M^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(\imath_{0}(1,g_{1})\Upsilon_{0,p}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{c}_{p}(\det g_{1})\left<\pi^{\GU(2)}_{p}(g_{1})\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right>\,dg_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,c_{2,p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-s,\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(\imath_{0}(1,g_{1})\Upsilon_{0,p}\right)(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{c}_{p}(\det g_{1})\left<\pi^{\GU(2)}_{p}(g_{1})\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right>\,dg_{1},

with the factor

c_{2,p}\left(-s-\frac{-1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\gamma_{p}\left(-2s-1,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)^{-1}\gamma_{p}\left(-2s-2,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)^{-1}

as defined in (
5.7.5
). Therefore,

(6.2.3)

\displaystyle Z_{p}\left(f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),w_{3,p},\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right)=

\displaystyle\gamma_{p}\left(-2s,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-s,\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(\imath_{0}(1,g_{1})\Upsilon_{0,p}\right)(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{c}_{p}(\det g_{1})\left<\pi^{\GU(2)}_{p}(g_{1})\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right>\,dg_{1}.

By noting that

f^{\U(2,2)}_{p}\left(-s-\frac{1}{2},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\left(\imath_{0}(1,g_{1})\Upsilon_{0,p}\right)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\alpha_{\xi\tau,p}\begin{pmatrix}0&g_{1}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

and using the formula (
5.7.2
) for
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\alpha_{\xi\tau,p}
and that
\varphi_{p}
is spherical, an easy computation shows that

(6.2.4)

\text{the integral on the RHS of \eqref{eq:intU22}}=p^{-2ts-2t}\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{2}(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}(p)^{2t}\cdot\left<\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right>,

where
p^{t}
,
t\geq 1
, is the conductor of
(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Since
\pi_{p}
is unramified, we have

(6.2.5)

p^{-2ts-2t}\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{2}(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}(p)^{2t}=\gamma_{p}\left(s+1,\pi_{p}\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)

Combining (
6.2.3
), (
6.2.4
) and (
6.2.5
), we get

(
6.2.3
)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{p}\left(-2s,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-s,\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-c}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(s+1,\pi_{p}\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\cdot\left<\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right>

\displaystyle=\gamma_{p}\left(-2s,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-s,\pi_{p}\times(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)\cdot\left<\varphi_{p},\varphi^{\prime}_{p}\right>.

∎

7.
The non-degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of the Klingen family

The map (
4.7.3
) of taking the
\beta
-th Fourier–jacobi coefficient along the boundary stratum indexed by the cusp label
\mathbf{1}_{4}\in C(K^{p}_{f}K^{1}_{p,n})_{\mathrm{ord}}
induces

\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta}:\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\GU(3,1)}\right)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V^{J,\beta}_{\GU(2)}\right).

For
u\in\bigotimes_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}}\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
, let

(7.0.1)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Ebeta}{{{}}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}}=\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&u\end{smallmatrix}\right)\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}\right)\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V^{J,\beta}_{\GU(2)}\widehat{\otimes}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)

(The group
\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
acts on the Igusa tower and acts on
V_{\GU(3,1)}
, and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&u\end{smallmatrix}\right)\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
denotes the action of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&u\end{smallmatrix}\right)
on
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
.) The goal of this section is to prove Proposition
7.11.3
on the nonvanishing properties of the
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}
’s.

In §
7.1
-§
7.2
, we briefly recall some basics we will use about Weil representations, theta series, Jacobi forms and
p
-adic Petersson inner products. In §
7.4
and §
7.5
, we unfold the Siegel Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,3)
to compute the non-degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of the Klingen Eisenstein series on
\GU(3,1)
, and sketch our strategy for relating them to certain
L
-values, for which we can apply mod
p
nonvanishing results. Following that strategy, we need to choose an auxiliary Jacobi form on
\U(2)
and two CM families on
\U(2)
. §
7.6
-§
7.10
are about constructing the auxiliary Jacobi form and CM families and some other technical preparations. The desired nonvanishing property is proved in §
7.11
.

7.1.
The Schrödinger model of Weil representation and the intertwining maps for different polarizations

The non-degenerate terms in the Fourier–Jacobi expansion of an automorphic form on
\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A})
are Jacobi forms on the Jacobi group associated
\GU(2)
.

Let
V
(resp.
V^{-}
) be the two dimensional skew-Hermitian space
(\mathcal{K}^{2},\zeta_{0})
(resp.
(\mathcal{K}^{2},-\zeta_{0})
). We write elements in
V,V^{-}
as row vectors and fix the basis
v_{1}=(1,0),v_{2}=(0,1)
for
V
(resp.
v^{-}_{1}=(1,0),v^{-}_{2}=(0,1)
for
V^{-}
). With respect to the fixed basis, the unitary groups
\U(V)
and
\U(V^{-})
are naturally identified with our
\U(2)
(defined in (
5.4.1
)).

We fix the basis
v_{1},v_{2},v^{-}_{1},v^{-}_{2}
for
V\oplus V^{-}
and identify
\U(V\oplus V^{-})
with the unitary group whose
R
-points are

\left\{g\in\GL_{4}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):{\vphantom{\!\overline{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\overline{g}\begin{pmatrix}\zeta_{0}\\
&-\zeta_{0}\end{pmatrix}g=\begin{pmatrix}\zeta_{0}\\
&-\zeta_{0}\end{pmatrix}\right\}.

Define the unitary group
\U(2,2)
as

\displaystyle\U(2,2)(R)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in\GL_{4}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):{\vphantom{\!\overline{g}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\overline{g}\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}g=\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\}.

We fix the isomorphism

\displaystyle\U(V\oplus V^{-})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\U(2,2),

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\longmapsto\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}g\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix},

which induces the embedding

\displaystyle\U(2)\times\U(2)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\U(2,2),

\displaystyle(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}\\
&g_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}.

For a nonzero
\beta\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})
, denote by
W_{\beta}
the one dimensional Hermitian space
(\mathcal{K},\beta)
. Fix a basis
w_{\beta}=1
for
W_{\beta}
, and we can identify
\U(W_{\beta})
with the group
\U(1)
defined by

\U(1)(R)=\{g\in\GL_{1}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}):\bar{g}g=1\}.

We consider the Weil representations for the dual pairs
\U(2)\times\U(1)
and
\U(2,2)\times\U(1)
. The space
V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{\beta}
is a four dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space equipped with a non-degenerate symplectic pairing induced from the skew-Hermitian form on
V
and the Hermitian form on
W_{\beta}
. The group
\U(V)\times\U(W_{\beta})
embeds into
\Sp(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{\beta})
and forms a reductive dual pair. Let
\widetilde{\Sp}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{\beta})
denote the metaplectic group. Given a pair of Hecke characters
\lambda_{V},\lambda_{W_{\beta}}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
such that
\lambda_{V}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}^{\dim_{\mathcal{K}}V}
and
\lambda_{W_{\beta}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\eta^{\dim_{\mathcal{K}}W_{\beta}}_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}
, there is a splitting

(7.1.1)

\U(V_{v})\times\U(W_{\beta,v})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\widetilde{\Sp}(V_{v}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{\beta,v})

for all places
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
. The Weil representation of
\widetilde{\Sp}(V_{v}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{\beta,v})
(with respect to our fixed additive character
\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}:\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
in (
1.0.5
)) induces the Weil representation of
\U(V_{v})\times\U(W_{\beta,v})
. Similarly, with respect to
\lambda_{V^{-}},\lambda_{V\oplus V^{-}}:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
satisfying
\lambda_{V^{-}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}^{\dim_{\mathcal{K}}V^{-}}
,
\lambda_{V\oplus V^{-}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}^{\dim_{\mathcal{K}}V+\dim_{\mathcal{K}}V^{-}}
, we have the Weil representations of
\U(V^{-}_{v})\times U(W_{\beta,v})
and
\U(V\oplus V^{-}_{v})\times U(W_{\beta,v})
.

We choose our
\lambda_{V},\lambda_{V^{-}},\lambda_{W_{\beta}}
as

(7.1.2)

\displaystyle\lambda_{V}=\lambda_{V^{-}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2},

\displaystyle\lambda_{W_{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\lambda,

where
\lambda
is the Hecke character fixed in §
5.1
.

Remark 7.1.1
.

In some literature, instead of
\lambda_{V}=\lambda_{V^{-}}=\lambda^{2}
,
\lambda_{V}=\lambda_{V^{-}}=\mathrm{triv}
is used.

Put

\displaystyle e_{1}

\displaystyle=v_{1}\otimes w_{\beta},

\displaystyle e_{2}

\displaystyle=v_{2}\otimes w_{\beta},

\displaystyle e^{-}_{1}

\displaystyle=v^{-}_{1}\otimes w_{\beta},

\displaystyle e^{-}_{2}

\displaystyle=v^{-}_{2}\otimes w_{\beta}.

Then
\mathbb{Q}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}e_{2}
is a maximal isotropic subspace of the symplectic space
V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{\beta}
.
We have the polarizations

(7.1.3)

\displaystyle V\otimes W_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{Q}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}e_{2}\big)\oplus\big(\mathbb{Q}\delta e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}\delta e_{2}\big),

(Where
\delta
is our fixed totally imaginary element in
\mathcal{K}
.) The Schrödinger model of the Weil representation of
\U(V_{v})\times\U(W_{\beta,v})
is an action of
\U(V_{v})\times\U(W_{\beta,v})
on
\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2})
, the space of Schwartz functions on
\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2}
. We write this action as

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta,v}(g,u):\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2}),

\displaystyle g\in\U(V_{v}),\,u\in\U(W_{\beta,v}).

Similarly, we have the polarization

(7.1.4)

\displaystyle V^{-}\otimes W_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{Q}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}e^{-}_{2}\big)\oplus\big(\mathbb{Q}\delta e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}\delta e^{-}_{2}\big).

and the Schrödinger model

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta,v}(g,u):\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{2})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{2}),

\displaystyle g\in\U(V^{-}_{v}),\,u\in\U(W_{\beta,v}).

For a place
v=\mathfrak{v}\bar{\mathfrak{v}}
split in
\mathcal{K}
, we have
\mathcal{K}_{v}\simeq\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}\times\mathcal{K}_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}
with
\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}\simeq\mathcal{K}_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}\simeq\mathbb{Q}_{v}
. In addition to the above polarizations, we also consider the polarizations

\displaystyle V_{v}\otimes W_{\beta,v}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e_{1}\oplus\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e_{2}\big)\oplus\big(\mathcal{K}_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}e_{1}\oplus\mathcal{K}_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}e_{2}\big),

\displaystyle V^{-}_{v}\otimes W_{\beta,v}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e^{-}_{2}\big)\oplus\big(\mathcal{K}_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{K}_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}e^{-}_{2}\big),

and the intertwining maps

(7.1.5)

\displaystyle\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e_{1}\oplus\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}+\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2}\big),

(7.1.6)

\displaystyle\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{v}}e^{-}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{1}+\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{2}\big).

For
V\oplus V^{-}
, we have the polarization:

(V\oplus V^{-})\otimes W_{\beta}=\big(\mathcal{K}(e_{1}+e^{-}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}(e_{2}+e^{-}_{2})\big)\oplus\big(\mathcal{K}(e_{1}-e^{-}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}(e_{2}-e^{-}_{2})\big).

Writing an element in
\mathcal{K}_{v}(e_{1}+e^{-}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}_{v}(e_{2}+e^{-}_{2})
as
X\in M_{1,2}(\mathcal{K}_{v})
, for the Schrödinger model

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta,v}(g,u):\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathcal{K}(e_{1}+e^{-}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}(e_{2}+e^{-}_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathpzc{S}\big(\mathcal{K}(e_{1}+e^{-}_{1})\oplus\mathcal{K}(e_{2}+e^{-}_{2})\big),

\displaystyle g\in\U(2,2),\,u\in\U(W_{\beta,v}),

we have the following formulas:

(7.1.7)

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta,v}(\mathbf{1}_{4},u)\,\Phi(X)

\displaystyle=\Phi(u^{-1}X),

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta,v}\left(\begin{pmatrix}A\\
&{\vphantom{\!\bar{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{A}\end{pmatrix},1\right)\Phi(X)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{v}(\det A)|\det A|^{1/2}_{\mathcal{K}_{v}}\cdot\Phi(XA),

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta,v}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&B\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix},1\right)\Phi(X)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{v}\left(\beta\cdot XB{\vphantom{\!\bar{X}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{X}\right)\cdot\Phi(X),

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta,v}\left(\begin{pmatrix}&-\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix},1\right)\Phi(X)

\displaystyle=|\beta|_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\int\Phi(Y)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{v}\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal{K}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}(\beta\cdot Y{\vphantom{\!\bar{X}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{X})\right)\,dY.

7.2.
The Heisenberg group and Jacobi forms

Jacobi forms on
\U(2)
and
\U(2,2)
show up in our computation of the non-degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of the evaluations at classical points of the Klingen family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}
.

First, we introduce Jacobi forms on
\U(2)
. Recall that
V
is the skew Hermitian space
(\mathcal{K}^{2},\zeta_{0})
. Denote by
H(V)
be the Heisenberg group associated to
V
. For a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R

H(V)(R)=(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R)\ltimes R,

and the multiplication is

(x_{1},\sigma_{1})(x_{2},\sigma_{2})=\left(x_{1}+x_{2},\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\frac{x_{1}\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}_{2}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}_{2}+\overline{x_{1}\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}_{2}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}_{2}}}{2}\right).

It is easy to see that
H(V)
is isomorphic to
U_{P_{\GU(3,1)}}
via

(7.2.1)

(x,\sigma)\longmapsto u(x,\sigma)=\begin{pmatrix}1&x&\sigma+\frac{1}{2}x\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}.

The Jacobi group associated to
V
is the semi-direct product
H(V)\rtimes\U(V)
, which we identify with
a subgroup of
P_{\GU(3,1)}
by

(7.2.2)

\displaystyle\big((x,\sigma),g_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto u(x,\sigma)\,m(g_{1}),

\displaystyle(x,\sigma)\in H(W_{0}),\,g_{1}\in\U(V),

where for
g_{1}\in\U(V)
,

m(g_{1})=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
&g_{1}\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}.

A Jacobi form on
H(V)\rtimes\U(V)
or a Jacobi form on
\U(V)
of index
\beta
is a smooth function on
H(V)(\mathbb{Q})\rtimes\U(V)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(V)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\rtimes\U(V)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
such that

–

for all
\sigma\in\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, the left translation by
\big((0,\sigma),\mathbf{1}_{2}\big)
equals the multiplication by
\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\beta\sigma)
,

–

the right translation of a maximal compact subgroup and the action of the center of the universal enveloping Lie algebra at
\infty
satisfy finiteness conditions.

It is easy to see that a Jacobi form of index
\beta
holomorphic at
\infty
of weight
(0,0)
corresponds a section in
H^{0}\left(\mathscr{C},\mathcal{L}(\beta)\right)
with
\mathscr{C}
a torsor over the Shimura variety of
\GU(2)
of an abelian scheme isogeneous to the universal abelian scheme, and
\mathcal{L}(\beta)
the invertible sheaf over
\mathscr{C}
of
\beta
-homogeneous functions.

We will also need Jacobi forms on
\U(2,2)
. Denote by
\mathbb{V}
the skew Hermitian space
\mathcal{K}^{4}
equipped with the skew Hermitian form
\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}
, and by
H(𝕍)
its associated Heisenberg group whose
R
-points for a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
are

H(\mathbb{V})(R)=(\mathbb{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R)\ltimes R.

We write an element in
H(\mathbb{V})(R)
as
(x,y,\sigma)
,
x,y\in R\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{K}^{2}
,
\sigma\in R
. The multiplication is given by

(x_{1},y_{1},\sigma_{1})(x_{2},y_{2},\sigma_{2})=\left(x_{1}+x_{2},y_{1}+y_{2},,\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\frac{x_{1}{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}_{2}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}_{2}+\bar{x}_{1}{\vphantom{\!y}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!y_{2}-{\vphantom{\!y_{1}\bar{x}_{2}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!y_{1}\bar{x}_{2}-\bar{y}_{1}{\vphantom{\!x_{2}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!x_{2}}{2}\right).

The Jacobi group associated to
\mathbb{V}
is the semi-direct product
H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(\mathbb{V})
.

Denote by
P_{\GU(3,3)}
the parabolic subgroup of
\GU(3,3)
consisting of elements whose entries in the first column are all
0
except the
(1,1)
entry, and denote by
U_{P_{\GU(3,3)}}
its unipotent subgroup. We identify
H(\mathbb{V})
with
U_{P_{\GU(3,3)}}
by

\displaystyle(x,y,\sigma)

\displaystyle\longmapsto u(x,y,\sigma)=\begin{pmatrix}1&x&\sigma+\frac{x{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}-y{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}}{2}&y\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}&\\
&&1\\
&&-{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix},

and identify
H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(2,2)
with a subgroup of
P_{\GU(3,3)}
by

(7.2.3)

\displaystyle\big((x,y,\sigma),g\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto u(x,y,\sigma)\,m(g),

\displaystyle(x,y,\sigma)\in H(\mathbb{W}_{0}),\,g\in\U(2,2).

where for
g=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}\in\U(2,2)
,

(7.2.4)

m(g)=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
&A&&B\\
&&1\\
&C&&D\end{pmatrix}.

Similarly as above, we define Jacobi forms on
\U(\mathbb{V})
of index
\beta
to be smooth functions on
H(\mathbb{V})(\mathbb{Q})\rtimes\U(\mathbb{V})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{V})(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\rtimes\U(\mathbb{V})(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
on which the left translation of
\big((0,0,\sigma),\mathbf{1}_{4}\big)
equal the multiplication of
\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\beta\sigma)
plus finiteness conditions for the right translation of a maximal compact subgroup and the center of the universal enveloping Lie algebra at
\infty
.

We have the following embedding of skew Hermitian spaces

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{V},

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left(x,-\frac{x\zeta_{0}}{2}\right).

It induces the embedding of unitary groups

\displaystyle\U(V)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\U(\mathbb{V})=\U(2,2),

\displaystyle g_{1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}

and induces an embedding of the Jacobi groups

(7.2.5)

\displaystyle H(V)\rtimes\U(V)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(\mathbb{V}),

\displaystyle\left((x,\sigma),\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left(\left(x,-\frac{x\zeta_{0}}{2},\sigma\right),\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}\right).

From this embedding, we also get the embedding

(7.2.6)

\displaystyle\big(H(V)\rtimes\U(V)\big)\times\U(V^{-})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(\mathbb{V}),

\displaystyle\big(((x,\sigma),g_{1}),g_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left(\left(x,-\frac{x\zeta_{0}}{2},\sigma\right),\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}\\
&g_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}\right).

The embeddings (
7.2.2
)(
7.2.3
)(
7.2.6
) are compatible with the embedding
\imath
in (
5.5.1
) in the sense that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\big(H(V)\rtimes\U(V)\big)\times\U(V^{-})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eqref{eq:H-to-U31}\times\mathrm{id}}
(
7.2.6
)
\textstyle{\GU(3,1)\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GU(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\imath}
\textstyle{H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(\mathbb{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
7.2.3
)
\textstyle{\GU(3,3).}

7.3.
Theta series

Given a Schwartz function
\phi\in\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v}\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e_{2})
(with
\phi_{v}=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{v}e_{1}+\mathbb{Z}_{v}e_{2}}
for all most all finite places
v
), we define the theta series
\theta(\phi,\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})
, which is an automorphic form on
\U(V)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, as

\theta(\phi,g)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Q}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}e_{2}}\omega_{\beta}(g)\Phi(x).

We can also define the Jacobi theta series
\theta^{J}(\phi,\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})
, which is a Jacobi form on
H(V)\rtimes\U(2)
, as

(7.3.1)

\theta^{J}(\phi,(y,\sigma),g)=\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\left(\beta(\sigma+\frac{1}{2}y\zeta_{0}{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x})\right)\sum_{x\in\mathbb{Q}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}e_{2}}\omega_{\beta}(g)\Phi(x+y).

Similarly, given
\phi\in\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v}\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{v}e^{-}_{2})
, we can define the theta series
\theta_{\beta}(\phi,\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})
on
\U(V^{-})(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.

7.4.
The unfolding

Given a holomorphic automorphic form
\mathcal{F}
on
\GU(3,1)(\mathbb{A})
and
g\in\GU(3,1)
and
\beta\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})_{>0}=\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, the
\beta
-th Fourier–Jacobi coefficient of
\mathcal{F}
at
g
is defined as

\displaystyle a_{\beta}(g;\mathcal{F})=\int_{\sigma\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})\backslash\Her_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}})}\mathcal{F}\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&&\sigma\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&1\end{pmatrix}g\right)\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}}(-\beta\sigma)\,d\sigma.

With
g\in\GU(3,1)
fixed, the function

\displaystyle H(V)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\rtimes\U(V)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

\displaystyle\big((x,\sigma),g_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto a_{\beta}\big(u(x,\sigma)m(g_{1});\mathcal{F}\big)

is a Jacobi form on
H(V)\ltimes\U(V)
of index
\beta
.

The Klingen Eisenstein family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
interpolates a normalization of the Klingen Eisenstein series
E^{\mathrm{Kling}}\big(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}};F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)
, which can be expressed as the integral in (
5.10.3
). We can compute its
\beta
-th Fourier–Jacobi coefficients by first computing

(7.4.1)

E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}(g;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))=\int_{\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})\backslash\Her_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}})}E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&&\sigma\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
&&1\\
&&&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}g;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\right)\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(-\beta\sigma)\,d\sigma,

and then pairing its restriction to
\GU(3,1)\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GU(2)
with
\varphi
on
\GU(2)
. By unfolding the Siegel Eisenstein series in the integrand on the right hand side of (
7.4.1
), it’s not difficult to show that

(7.4.2)

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}(h;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))=\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{\U(2,2)}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2,2)(\mathbb{Q})}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{K}^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Her_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}})}f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\left(\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{3}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{3}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&&\varsigma&x\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}\\
&&1\\
&&&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}m\left(\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\gamma\right)h\right)\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(-\beta\sigma)\,d\varsigma,

where
m(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})
is the embedding of
\U(2,2)
into
\U(3,3)
as defined in (
7.2.4
). (cf
[
Wan15
, Section 3.3.1]
)

Definition 7.4.1
.

For
g_{v}\in\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
,
x_{v}\in\mathcal{K}^{2}_{v}
and
f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\in I_{Q_{\GU(3,3)},v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
, define

FJ

β
,
v

(g

v

,x

v

;f

v

(s,ξ

0

τ

0

))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v})}f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\left(\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{3}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{3}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&&\varsigma&x_{v}\\
&\mathbf{1}_{2}&{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}_{v}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}_{v}\\
&&1\\
&&&\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}m\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}g_{v}\right)\mathbf{e}_{\mathbb{A}}(-\beta\sigma)\,d\sigma.

For
g\in\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
,
x\in\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\mathcal{K}}
and
f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})=\otimes_{v}f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
, let

\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta}\big(g,x;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)=\otimes_{v}\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\big(g_{v},x_{v};f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big).

The unfolding result (
7.4.2
) gives the following proposition.

Proposition 7.4.2
.

Let
\beta\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})_{>0}
and
f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\in I_{Q_{\GU(3,3)}}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
. The
\beta
-th Fourier–Jacobi coefficient of the Siegel Eisenstein series
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}\big(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)
at
g\in\U(3,3)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
equals

E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}(g;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))=\sum_{\gamma\in Q_{\U(2,2)}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2,2)(\mathbb{Q})}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{K}^{2}}\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta}\big(m(\gamma)g,x;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big),

with
\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta}
defined as in Definition
7.4.1
and
m(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}})
the embedding (
7.2.4
).

One can easily check that
\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\left(u(x,y,\sigma)g,x_{0};f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{v}\left(\beta(\sigma+\frac{x{\vphantom{\!\bar{y}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{y}-y{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x}}{2})\right)\,\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\big(g,x_{0}+x;f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\left(m\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
0&{\vphantom{\!\bar{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{A}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}g,x_{0};f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\right)

\displaystyle=|\det A{\vphantom{\!\bar{A}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{A}|^{s+1}_{v}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}(\det A)\cdot|\det A{\vphantom{\!\bar{A}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{A}|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{v}\mathbf{e}_{v}(\beta xB{\vphantom{\!\bar{A}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{A}{\vphantom{\!\bar{x}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{x})

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\big(g,x_{0}A;f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big).

Comparing them with the formulas in (
7.1.7
) for the Schrödinger model of Weil representations, we see that for a fixed
g_{v}\in\U(3,3)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
,
\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\big(u(x_{v},y_{v},\sigma_{v})\,m(g_{1,v})g_{v},x_{0};f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)
is essentially (a finite sum of) the product

f_{2,v}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)(g_{1,v})\cdot\omega_{\beta,v}\big(u(x_{v},y_{v},\sigma_{v})\,m(g_{1,v})\big)\Phi_{v}(x_{0}),

with
f_{2,v}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)
a section in the degenerate principal series
I_{Q_{\U(2,2),v}}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)
,
\Phi_{v}
a Schwartz function on
\mathcal{K}^{2}_{v}
and
\omega_{\beta,v}
the Weil representation of
\U(2,2)
briefly recalled in §
7.1
. Both
f_{2,v}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)
and
\Phi_{v}
are determined by
f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\in I_{Q_{\GU(3,3)},v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
and
g_{v}\in\GU(3,3)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
. The computation at an unramified place
v
gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\big(u(x,y,\sigma)m(g_{1,v}),x_{0};f^{\mathrm{sph}}_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,L_{v}\left(2s+3,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)^{-1}\cdot f^{\mathrm{sph}}_{2,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})(g_{1,v})\cdot\omega_{\beta,v}\big(u(x,y,\sigma)m(g_{1,v})\big)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{2}_{\mathcal{K},v}}(x_{0}),

Therefore, the Jacobi form on
H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(2,2)
given by

\displaystyle\big((x,y,\sigma),g\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}(u(x,y,\sigma)m(g);f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))

is essentially (a finite sum of) the product

L\left(2s+3,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)^{-1}\cdot E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)\cdot\Theta^{J},

with
E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)
a Siegel Eisenstein series on
\U(2,2)
and
\Theta^{J}
a Jacobi theta function on the
H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(2,2)
. The restriction of
\Theta^{J}
from
H(\mathbb{V})\rtimes\U(2,2)
to
\big(H(V)\rtimes\U(2)\big)\times\U(2)
via (
7.2.3
) is essentially (a finite sum of)
\theta^{J}_{3}\boxtimes\theta_{2}
, with
\theta^{J}_{3}
(resp.
\theta_{2}
) a Jacobi theta function (resp. theta function) on
H(V)\rtimes\U(2)
(resp.
\U(2)
) attached to a Schwartz function
\phi_{3}
(resp.
\phi_{2}
) on
\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}}
. Therefore,

(7.4.3)

\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}};f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))\right|_{\big(H(V)\rtimes\U(2)\big)\times\U(2)}\approx L\left(2s+3,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)^{-1}\cdot\theta^{J}_{3}\boxtimes\theta_{2}\cdot\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)\right|_{\U(2)\times\U(2)}.

Here
\approx
means equal up to normalizations and more precise formulas for local sections are needed to be an actual identity. We will also use the notation
\approx
several times in the next section. All the identities with
\approx
are only for the purpose of illustrating the idea of relating the non-degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients with
L
-values, and will not be used for our rigorous analysis of
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}
starting from §
7.6
.

7.5.
Our strategy of analyzing the non-degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of the Klingen Eisenstein family

Before we move on to the involved computations, we give a brief explanation of how we choose the auxiliary data to study the pairing of (
7.4.3
) with
\varphi
. The description of this section should also explain why the choices of the auxiliary data in §
5.6
are made for constructing
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
and guaranteeing that its non-degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients satisfy the nonvanishing properties needed for applications.

7.5.1.
Choosing the auxiliary
\theta^{J}_{1}

The first step to analyze (
7.4.3
) is to pick a suitable Schwartz function
\phi_{1}
on
\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}}
, let
θ

J

1

be the associated Jacobi theta function on
H(V)\rtimes\U(2)
(defined as in (
7.3.1
)), and define the linear functional

\displaystyle l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}:\{\text{Jacobi forms on $H(V)\rtimes\U(2)$}\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\{\text{automorphic forms on $\U(2)$}\}

by

(7.5.1)

l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}(\varphi^{J})(g_{1})=\int_{H(V)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(V)(\mathbb{A})}\theta^{J}_{1}\big((x,\sigma),g_{1}\big)\varphi^{J}\big((x,\sigma),g_{1}\big)\,dxd\sigma.

Applying
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}
to (
7.4.3
), we obtain an automorphic form on
\U(2)\times\U(2)
whose value at
(g_{1},g_{2})
equals

(7.5.2)

\int_{H(V)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(V)(\mathbb{A})}\theta^{J}_{1}\big((w,\sigma),g_{1}\big)\cdot E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}\big(\imath(u(x,\sigma)m(g_{1}),g_{2});f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)\,dxd\sigma.

It is a standard fact that for two Schwartz functions
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}}
,

\int_{H(V)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(V)(\mathbb{A})}\theta^{J}_{\phi}\big((x,\sigma),g_{1}\big)\overline{\theta^{J}_{\phi^{\prime}}\big((x,\sigma),g_{1}\big)}\,dxd\sigma=\left<\phi,\overline{\phi^{\prime}}\right>=\int_{\mathcal{K}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}}}\phi(x)\overline{\phi^{\prime}(x)}\,dx,

independent of
g_{1}\in\U(2)
. Thanks to this fact, from (
7.5.2
) we obtain

(7.5.3)

\displaystyle l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{\beta}(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,;f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}))\right|_{\big(H(V)\rtimes\U(2)\big)\times\U(2)}\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\left<\phi_{1},\phi_{3}\right>}{L\left(2s+3,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)}\cdot(1\boxtimes\theta_{2})\cdot\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)\right|_{\U(2)\times\U(2)}.

Here
1\boxtimes\theta_{2}
denotes the automorphic form on
\U(2)\times\U(2)
which is the constant function
1
on the first factor and
\theta_{2}
on the second factor.

For an automorphic form on
{\tt F}
on
\U(2)
and a Hecke character
\chi:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, we use the notation
{\tt F}^{\chi}
to denote the form

(7.5.4)

{\tt F}^{\chi}(g)={\tt F}(g)\cdot\chi(\det g),\quad g\in\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}).

Our Klingen Eisenstein series
E^{\mathrm{Kling}}\big(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)
is obtained by pairing the restriction of the Siegel Eisenstein series with
\varphi^{\overline{\xi_{0}\tau}_{0}}
, so we have

(7.5.5)

\displaystyle l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(E^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\beta}\big(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,\,;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\left<\phi_{1},\phi_{3}\right>}{L\left(2s+3,\xi_{0}^{\mathbb{Q}}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)}\cdot\left<\theta_{2}\varphi^{\overline{\xi_{0}\tau}_{0}},\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)\right|_{\U(2)\times\U(2)}\right>_{1\times\U(2)},

where
\left<\cdot,\cdot\right>_{1\times\U(2)}
means integration over the second copy of
\U(2)
.

The standard doubling method formula
[
LR05
]
implies

\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)\right|_{\U(2)\times\U(2)}\approx\sum_{h}\frac{L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h})\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)}{L\left(2s+1,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)\,L\left(2s+2,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)}\frac{\overline{h}\boxtimes h^{\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\overline{\lambda}}}{\left<\overline{h},h\right>},

where
h
runs over a certain orthonormal basis of automorphic forms on
\U(2)
of certain level. By picking an
h
, we have

(7.5.6)

\displaystyle\left<l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(E^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\beta}\big(-;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)\right),h\right>

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{1},\phi_{3}\right>\frac{L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h})\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)}{d_{3}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})}\left<\theta_{2}\varphi^{\overline{\xi_{0}\tau_{0}}},h^{\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\overline{\lambda}}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{1},\phi_{3}\right>\frac{L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h})\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)}{d_{3}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})}\left<\theta_{2}^{\overline{\lambda}}\varphi,h\right>,

where
d_{3}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})
is as in (
5.9.2
). The Petersson inner product
\left<\theta_{2}^{\overline{\lambda}}\varphi,h\right>
is related to the central value of a triple product
L
-function by Ichino’s formula.

7.5.2.
Choosing auxiliary
h
and
\theta
and relating to
L
-values

We will choose suitable CM forms
h
and
\theta
on
\U(2)
such that we can apply the available mod
p
nonvanishing results to study
L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h})\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)
and
\left<\theta_{2}^{\overline{\lambda}}\varphi,h\right>
in (
7.5.6
).

Let
\chi_{h}
and
\chi_{\theta}
be two unitary Hecke characters of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
such that
\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\mathrm{triv}
(as chosen in §
5.6
). Let
h_{0}
be the theta lift of
\chi^{-1}_{h}|_{\U(1)}
to
\U(2)
, and
h
be the automorphic form on
\U(2)
obtained from
h_{0}
by

(7.5.7)

\displaystyle h(g)

\displaystyle=\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}(a)\cdot h_{0}(g),

\displaystyle g\in\U(2),\,a\in\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}},\,\det g=a\bar{a}^{-1}.

Thanks to the condition
\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\mathrm{triv}
, the definition of
h(g)
does not depend on the choice of
a\in\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
.

The form
\theta_{2}
does not generate an irreducible representation of
\U(2)
. Let
\theta
be the projection of
\theta_{2}
to its
\chi_{\theta}\lambda^{2}|_{\U(1)}
-eigenspace for the action of the center of
\U(2)
. Then
\theta
is a theta lift of
\chi_{\theta}\lambda^{2}|_{\U(1)}
. Since our
\varphi
is assumed to have the trivial central character and
h
has the central character
\chi^{-1}_{\theta}|_{\U(1)}
, we have

(7.5.8)

\left<\theta_{2}^{\overline{\lambda}}\varphi,h\right>=\left<\theta^{\overline{\lambda}}\varphi,h\right>.

By
[
GI14
, Theorem C.5]
, with the choice of the splitting characters for the theta correspondence between
\U(2)
and
\U(1)
as in (
7.1.2
), we have

(7.5.9)

\displaystyle\mathrm{BC}\left(\pi_{h}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{BC}\left(\pi_{h_{0}}\right)\otimes\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}=\left(\mathrm{BC}\left(\chi^{-1}_{h}|_{\U(1)}\right)\lambda^{-1}\oplus\lambda\right)\otimes\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}

\displaystyle=\left(\chi^{-1}_{h}\chi^{c}_{h}\lambda^{-1}\oplus\lambda\right)\otimes\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}=\chi^{c}_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\lambda^{-1}\oplus\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\lambda,

and

(7.5.10)

\mathrm{BC}\big(\pi_{\theta^{\overline{\lambda}}}\big)=\big(\mathrm{BC}\left(\chi_{\theta}\lambda^{2}|_{\U(1)}\big)\lambda^{-1}\oplus\lambda\right)\bar{\lambda}\bar{\lambda}^{-c}=\left(\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\lambda^{3}\oplus\lambda\right)\lambda^{-2}=\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\lambda\oplus\lambda^{-1}.

Thus, we have

(7.5.11)

\displaystyle L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h})\times\lambda^{-1}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\lambda^{-2}\chi^{c}_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)\,L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\xi^{c}_{0}\tau^{c}_{0}\right)\,L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right).

By the triple product formula

(7.5.12)

\displaystyle\left<\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}\varphi,h\right>\left<\tilde{\theta}^{\lambda}_{3}\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{h}_{3}\right>

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\big<\varphi,\tilde{\varphi}\big>}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})}\,\frac{\big<h,\tilde{h}_{3}\big>}{\zeta_{\mathcal{K}}(1)\,L(1,\chi_{h}\chi^{-1}_{h}\lambda^{2})}\,\frac{\big<\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{3}\big>}{\zeta_{\mathcal{K}}(1)\,L(1,\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\lambda^{2})}

\displaystyle\times L\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\lambda^{2}\right)L\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\right),

where
\tilde{\varphi}
(resp.
\tilde{h}_{3}
,
\tilde{\theta}_{3}
) are suitable forms taken from the dual representation of
\pi^{\U(2)}=\pi_{\varphi}
(resp.
\pi_{h}
,
\pi_{\theta}
). (In our case here, the triple product
L
-function for
\pi^{D}\times\pi^{D}_{\theta}\times\pi^{D}_{h}
factorizes as the product of the two
L
-functions in (
7.5.12
). Also, note that Ichino’s triple product formula is for automorphic forms on
D^{\times}
, we actually need to relate the integral on
\U(2)
for
\left<\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}\varphi,h\right>\left<\tilde{\theta}^{\lambda}_{3}\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{h}_{3}\right>
to an integral on
D^{\times}
. In §
7.10
, we discuss extending automorphic forms on
\U(2)
to
\GU(2)
. Thanks to (
5.4.3
), the integral over
\GU(2)
is the same as the integral over
D^{\times}
.)

Combining (
7.5.6
)(
7.5.8
)(
7.5.11
)(
7.5.12
), we get

(7.5.13)

\displaystyle d_{3}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\cdot\left<l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(E^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\beta}\big(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)\right),h\right>\left<\tilde{\theta}^{\lambda}_{3}\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{h}_{3}\right>

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{1},\phi_{3}\right>\frac{\big<\varphi,\tilde{\varphi}\big>}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})}\cdot\frac{\big<h,\tilde{h}_{3}\big>}{\zeta_{\mathcal{K}}(1)\,L(1,\chi_{h}\chi^{-1}_{h}\lambda^{2})}\,\frac{\big<\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{3}\big>}{\zeta_{\mathcal{K}}(1)\,L(1,\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\lambda^{2})}

\displaystyle\times L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\xi^{c}_{0}\tau^{c}_{0}\right)\,L\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\right)\cdot L\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\lambda^{2}\right)L\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\right).

The relation between
E^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\beta}\big(-;F(f(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}),\varphi)\big)
and
L
-values illustrated in (
7.5.13
) explains the nonvanishing conditions on the
L
-values in (
5.6.1
)(
5.6.2
)(
5.6.3
)(
5.6.4
) in our choice of the auxiliary Hecke characters
\chi_{\theta}
and
\chi_{h}
in §
5.6
.

In the following, guided by the strategy described in this section, we carry out the necessary local computations and the construction of the auxiliary objects to prove the the desired property of
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}
with
\beta=1
. The computation is place by place, and we have the following cases:

–

For the archimedean place, we only need to consider the cases for which there are standard choices of local sections and computations are easy.

–

For unramified places, we have the standard formulas for spherical sections.

–

For the place
p
and the places in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
, we compute precise formulas.

–

For the places in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
, we do not attempt to compute precise formulas. At these places, the local data actually do not change when
\tau
varies in a
p
-adic family, and we only make sure that local integrals contribute a nonzero scalar.

7.6.
The auxiliary Jacobi form
\theta^{J}_{1}

Given a Schwartz function
\phi
on
\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\phi_{\infty}
is the standard Gaussian function, let
\theta^{J}
be the associated Jacobi form on
\U(2)
(as defined in (
7.3.1
)). The map attached to this
\theta^{J}
defined in (
7.5.1
) extends to
V^{J,\beta}_{\GU(2)}
and induces

(7.6.1)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{lthetaJ}{{{}}l_{\theta^{J}}}:\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V^{J,\beta}_{\GU(2)}\right)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\U(2)}\right).

Apply it to
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}
(defined in (
7.0.1
)), we get

l_{\theta^{J}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\U(2),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)^{\natural}.

Proposition 7.6.1
.

Let
\beta=1\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})
. There exists a Schwartz function
\phi_{1}
on
\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\phi_{1,\infty}
the standard Gaussian function, elements
u_{1},\dots,u_{r}\in\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\U(1)\left(\mathbb{Q}_{v}\right)
, and constants
b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
, (all independent of
\tau
), such that

l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\U(2),\,\xi}\right)^{\natural},

the image of
\sum\limits_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}
under the map (
7.6.1
) attached to the Jacobi form
θ

J

1

corresponding to
\phi_{1}
, satisfies the following interpolation properties: for all
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
as in Theorem
5.9.1
,

(7.6.2)

\displaystyle l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum\nolimits_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})=

\displaystyle d^{\Sigma\,\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left<\theta(\phi_{2,\tau})\left(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1\end{pmatrix}\right)\varphi^{\overline{\xi_{0}\tau}_{0}},\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(-\,;f_{2}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})\right)\right|_{\U(2)\times\U(2)}\right>_{1\times\U(2)},

with
\phi_{2,\xi\tau}\in\mathpzc{S}(\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\mathbb{Q}})
(independent of
\tau
except
v=p
) and
f_{2}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})\in\bigotimes_{v}I_{Q_{\U(2,2)},v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})
described as follows:

–
v=\infty
.

\displaystyle\phi_{2,\xi\tau,\infty}(y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathfrak{s}^{1/4}\mathrm{Nm}(\delta)^{1/4}}{2}e^{-2\pi\sqrt{\mathrm{Nm}(\delta)}\,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y_{1}&y_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\zeta_{0}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y_{1}\\
y_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)},

\displaystyle f_{2,\infty}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})\left(g^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-2\pi i)^{k}}{\Gamma(k)}\cdot\det\left(C\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}+D\right)^{-k}\left|C\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}+D\right|^{-s+1+\frac{k}{2}}_{\mathbb{C}}

–
v\notin\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime},p\}
.

\displaystyle\phi_{2,\xi\tau,v}

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{v}}\times\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{v}},

\displaystyle f_{2,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})

\displaystyle=L_{v}\left(2s+3,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\right)^{-1}\cdot f^{\mathrm{sph}}_{2,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})

–
v=\mathfrak{v}\overline{\mathfrak{v}}\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
.

\displaystyle\phi_{2,\xi\tau,v}=\text{Image of \eqref{eq:int-S} of }\Big((y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{v}}}(y_{1})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}}(y_{2})\chi_{\theta,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}\lambda_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}}(y_{2})\Big)

(7.6.3)

\displaystyle f_{2,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})\left(g^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}\right)=

\displaystyle|\det C{\vphantom{\!\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{C}|^{-s-1}(\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})_{v}\left(\det\bar{C}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathds{1}_{\Her_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},v})}\left(C^{-1}D+q^{-c_{v}}_{v}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{2}\right)

–
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
.

\displaystyle\phi_{2,\xi\tau,v}=

a nonzero Schwartz function on
\mathbb{Q}^{2}_{v}
inside the
\chi_{\theta}\lambda^{2}|_{\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}
-eigenspace

for the action of
\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
, invariant under
K_{v}
, independent of
\tau
,

\displaystyle f_{2,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})=

\displaystyle\,\text{same as \eqref{eq:f2-ramv}}.

– The place
p
.

(7.6.4)

\displaystyle\phi_{2,\xi\tau,p}=

\displaystyle\text{Image of \eqref{eq:int-S} of }

\displaystyle\Big((y_{1},y_{2})\longmapsto p^{-2t}\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}(p^{t}y_{1})(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}(-p^{t}y_{1})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(p^{t}y_{2})\Big),

\displaystyle f_{2,p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})=

\displaystyle\,\gamma_{p}\left(-2s,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)\gamma_{p}\left(-2s-1,(\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times M^{\prime}_{p}\left(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})^{-c}\right)f^{\textnormal{big-cell}}_{2,p}\left(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda)^{-c}\right)(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\,\Upsilon^{\prime}_{p}),

where

•

M^{\prime}_{p}\left(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda)^{-c}\right):I_{Q_{\U(2,2)},p}\left(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda)^{-c}\right)\rightarrow I_{Q_{\U(2,2)},p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})
is the intertwining operator,

•

\Upsilon^{\prime}_{p}
is the element in
\U(2,2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(\Upsilon^{\prime}_{p})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{1}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}&-\frac{\zeta_{0}}{2}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}
,

•

\displaystyle f^{\textnormal{big-cell}}_{2,p}\left(-s,(\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda)^{-c}\right)\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}=

\displaystyle\,|\det C{\vphantom{\!\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\bar{C}|^{s-1}_{p}(\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})_{p}(\det C)\cdot\alpha^{\prime}_{\xi\tau,p}(C^{-1}D).

with
\alpha^{\prime}_{\xi\tau,p}
the Schwartz function on
\Her_{2}(\mathcal{K}_{p})
defined as

\alpha^{\prime}_{\xi\tau,p}(x)=\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}^{3}_{p}}(x_{11},x_{21},x_{22})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}(p^{t}x_{12})(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}(p^{t}x_{12}),\quad\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}&x_{12}\\
x_{21}&x_{22}\end{pmatrix},

Proof.

This proposition is proved in
[
Wan20
, sections 6.E-6.H]
by computing
\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\big(g,x;f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)
place by place. The place
\infty
is done in Lemmas 6.9 and 6.11 and the unramified places are done in Lemma 6.18. (Note that at the unramified places, we have
d_{2,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})=\frac{d_{3,v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})}{L_{v}(2s+3,\xi^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0}\tau^{\mathbb{Q}}_{0})}
.) The places in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
are done in Lemmas 6.22 and 6.24, and the places in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
are done in Lemmas 6.22 and 6.26. Note that Lemma 6.26
loc.cit
is Proposition
5.6.1
and in §
5.6
we choose
\chi_{\theta}
such that at
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
we have nonzero
\phi_{2,\xi\tau,v}
described in this proposition. The place
p
