is done from Lemmas 6.35 to the end of the section 6.H. The Schwartz function
\phi_{2,\tau_{0},p}
is the
\phi^{\prime}_{2,p}
given below Definition 6.38. Note that our
\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,v}\big(g,x;f_{v}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0})\big)
defined in Definition 4.5.1. equals to
\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta}(f_{sieg,v};z,x,g\eta^{-1},1)
in
[
Wan20
]
with
z=s
and
\eta=\begin{pmatrix}&\mathbf{1}_{2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}
, so the results in
loc.cit
all contain the extra
\eta
but the formulas in this proposition do not.

At the place
p
, in fact the pairing of
\phi_{1,p}(x)
and
\mathrm{FJ}_{\beta,p}\big(g,(x,y)_{p};f_{p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\big)
along
x
does not directly equal
\phi_{2,\tau,p}(y)\cdot f_{2,p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})(g)
, but equals the average

(7.6.5)

\int_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\omega_{\beta,p}\left(\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
n&1\end{pmatrix},1\right)\phi_{2,\tau,p}(y)\cdot f_{2,p}(s,\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})\left(g\Upsilon^{\prime}_{p}\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
&1\\
&&1&\\
&&n&1\end{pmatrix}\right)\,dn.

Meanwhile,
\varphi
is spherical at
p
, in particular invariant under the right translation by
\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
\mathbb{Z}_{p}&1\end{pmatrix}
, so by replacing (
7.6.5
)
\phi_{2,\tau,p}(y)\cdot f_{2,p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})(g)
does not change the resulting Petersson inner product with
\varphi
over
\U(V^{-})=\U(2)
.

The Schwartz function (
7.6.4
) is the the
(v_{3},v_{4})
-part of
\Phi_{\mathcal{D},p}
where
\Phi_{\mathcal{D},p}
is the Schwartz function on
\mathbb{Q}^{4}_{p}
defined and appearing in
[
Wan20
, Lemma 6.37, Definition 6.36]
. As mentioned above, our formula in the Proposition corresponds to replacing
g
by
g\eta^{-1}
in the formula in
loc.cit
, so there is a translation by
\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1\end{pmatrix}
in the theta series in (
7.6.2
).
∎

7.7.
The construction of the auxiliary CM families
h,\tilde{h}_{3},\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{3}

Besides
\theta^{J}_{1}
, our strategy described in §
7.5
also includes auxiliary CM forms. We want to construct a CM family
\bm{h}
on
\U(V)=\U(2)
and pair it with
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)
. The splitting characters for the theta correspondence between
\U(2)
and
\U(1)
are chosen as in (
7.1.2
).

7.7.1.
The auxiliary group
U_{\mathcal{K},p}

First, we note that for
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
as in Theorem
5.9.1
, the nebentypus of
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)
at
p
is
\left(\mathrm{triv},(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}\right)
(
i.e.

\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
0&a_{2}\end{pmatrix}\in\U(2)(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
acts by
(\xi_{0}\tau_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}(a_{2})
). In order to get a CM family
\bm{h}
with the correct nebentypus to pair with
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)
, we want Hecke characters of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
unramified away from
p\infty
with restriction
\left(\tau_{0,\mathfrak{p}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}},\mathrm{triv}\right)
on
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}
. However, in general there is no canonical way to extend local characters
\left(\tau_{0,\mathfrak{p}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}},\mathrm{triv}\right)
of
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}
to global Hecke characters. Hence, we need to consider an auxiliary group
U_{\mathcal{K},p}
and the non-standard type of
p
-adic measures defined in (
5.8.9
).

Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{UKp}{{{}}U_{\mathcal{K},p}}=1+p\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},p}
and
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{UKp}{{{}}U_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}=1+p\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}
,
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{UKp}{{{}}U_{\mathcal{K},\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}}=1+p\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K},\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Then the natural map

U_{\mathcal{K},p}\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},f}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}

is an injection. We can pick
N
, a non-negative power of
p
, such that raising to the
{\tt N}
-th power maps
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
into
U_{\mathcal{K},p}
. Define

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{PN}{{{}}\mathpzc{P}_{{\tt N}}}:\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\mathcal{K},p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}\simeq 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}.}
\scriptstyle{\tt N}
-th power
natural proj.

Then given a (local)
p
-adic character
\epsilon:U_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
, the composition
\epsilon\circ\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}
is a (global)
p
-adic character of
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
whose restriction back to
U_{\mathcal{K},p}=U_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}\times U_{\mathcal{K},\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}
is
\left(\epsilon^{{\tt N}},\mathrm{triv}\right)
.

In particular, for an algebraic Hecke character
\tau:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of
\infty
-type
\left(0,k\right)
whose
p
-adic avatar
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
factors through
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
, we can define

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{tauP}{{{}}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}}=\left.\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\right|_{U_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}\circ\mathpzc{P}_{N}.

Then
\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}
is a
p
-adic Hecke character of
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
with

\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}|_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}}=\left(\tau^{{\tt N}}_{\mathfrak{p}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}},\mathrm{triv}\right)=\left(\tau^{\tt N}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}|_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}},\mathrm{triv}\right).

Because
\tau
has
\infty
-type
(0,k)
, the local character
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}
is of finite order, and it follows that the (global) character
\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}:\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}
is also of finite order. Hence, it takes values in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
and is also an algebraic Hecke character of
\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}
of
\infty
-type
\left(0,0\right)
. (From the definition in (
5.3.1
), we can see that the
p
-adic avatar of a Hecke character of
\infty
-type
(0,0)
is itself.)

The map
\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}
induces a map

(7.7.1)

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{PNast}{{{}}\mathpzc{P}_{{\tt N},\ast}}:\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\U(2)}\right)^{\natural}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}eas\left(U_{\mathcal{K},p},V_{\U(2)}\right)^{\natural}.

(See §
5.8.3
for the definition of these spaces of
p
-adic measures.) We have

\mathpzc{P}_{{\tt N},\ast}\left(l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum\nolimits_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)\right)\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},_{p}}}\right)=l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum\nolimits_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)(\tau^{\tt N}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})

for every
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\in\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}\right)
. In order to show the nonvanishing property of
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)
in Proposition
7.11.3
, it suffices to show the nonvanishing for
\mathpzc{P}_{{\tt N},\ast}\left(l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)\right)
.

Next, we construct auxiliary CM families
\bm{h}
,
\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}
,
\bm{\theta}
,
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{3}
on
\U(2)
as
p
-adic measures on
U_{\mathcal{K},p}
.

The auxiliary CM families
\bm{h}
and
\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}

.

∎

\displaystyle\phi_{\theta,\tau,v}

\displaystyle=\phi_{2,\xi\tau^{\tt N},v}\text{in Proposition~\ref{prop:phi2f2}},

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{\theta,\tau,v}

\displaystyle=\text{a Schwartz function on $\mathbb{Q}^{2}_{v}$ invariant under $K_{v}$, independent of $\tau$,}

belonging to the
\chi^{-1}_{\theta}\lambda^{-2}|_{\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}
-eigenspace for the action of
\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
,

\displaystyle\quad\text{and }\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{2}_{v}}\phi_{\theta,\tau,v}(y)\phi^{\prime}_{\theta,\tau,v}(y)\,dy\neq 0

–

v=p
. We have
(\chi_{\theta}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}})_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}=(\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Let
t=\mathrm{ord}_{p}\left(\mathrm{cond}\left((\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\right)
.

\displaystyle\phi_{\theta,\tau,p}

\displaystyle=\text{Image of \eqref{eq:int-S} of }

\displaystyle\quad\Big((y_{1},y_{2})\longmapsto p^{-t}\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau^{{\tt N}}_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\cdot\mathds{1}_{p^{-t}\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}(y_{1})(\xi_{0}\tau^{{\tt N}}_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}(-p^{t}y_{1})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(y_{2})\Big),

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{t}\mathbb{Z}}\omega_{\beta,p}\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
n&1\end{pmatrix},1\right)\phi_{2,\tau^{\tt N},p},

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{\theta,\tau,p}

\displaystyle=\text{Image of \eqref{eq:int-S} of }\Big((y_{1},y_{2})\longmapsto\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(y_{1})\cdot\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}}(y_{2})(\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})_{\mathfrak{p}}(y_{2})\cdot\Big),

where
\phi_{2,\tau^{\tt N},p}
is as in Proposition
7.6.1
.

(2) The specialization
\bm{\theta}\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}}\right)
equals the projection of
\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{t}\mathbb{Z}}\theta(\phi_{2,\tau^{\tt N}})\left(g\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
n&1\end{pmatrix}\right)
to the
\left.\lambda^{2}\chi_{\theta}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right|_{\U(1)}
-eigenspace for the action of the center of
\U(2)
. (Note that the projection of a theta series to the
\left.\lambda^{2}\chi_{\theta}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right|_{\U(1)}
-eigenspace for the action of the center is a theta lift of
\left.\lambda^{2}\chi_{\theta}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right|_{\U(1)}
.)

Proof.

See
[
Wan20
, §8B]
. The idea is the same as the construction of
\bm{h}_{0}
and
\bm{h}^{\prime}_{0}
, and (2) follows immediately from (1).
∎

7.8.
p
-adic Petersson inner product on
\U(2)

We need
p
-adic Petersson inner product for families on
\U(2)
. Let
V_{\U(2)}
,
e_{\mathrm{ord}}V^{\prime}_{\U(2)}
be the space of
p
-adic forms on
\U(2)
defined in §
5.8.3
.

Following
[
Hsi19
, p.33]
, we can define a
p
-adic Petersson inner product

(7.8.1)

\left<\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}:V^{\prime}_{\U(2),\mathrm{ord}}\times V_{\U(2)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L}

such that for all
\phi\in V_{\U(2)}
(resp.
\phi^{\prime}\in e_{\mathrm{ord}}V^{\prime}_{\U(2)}
) of with
K_{p,0}(p^{n})
-nebentypus
(\mathrm{triv},\chi)
(resp.
(\chi^{\prime},\mathrm{triv})
)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Petersson}{{{}}\left<\phi^{\prime},\phi\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\limits_{g}\,(U^{-n}_{p}\phi^{\prime})\left(g\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
-p^{n}\end{pmatrix}\right)\phi\left(g\right),&\chi\chi^{\prime}=\mathrm{triv},\\[15.00002pt]
0,&\chi\chi^{\prime}\neq\mathrm{triv},\end{array}\right.

where
K_{p,0}(p^{n})=\left\{g\in\U(2)(\mathbb{Z}_{p}):\varrho_{\mathfrak{p}}(g)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
0&\ast\end{pmatrix}\mod p^{n}\right\}
, and in the sum,
g
runs over the finite set
\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{\mathbb{Q},f})/K^{p}_{f}K_{p,0}(p^{n})
.

A useful property of this
p
-adic Petersson inner product is that

\left<\phi^{\prime},U_{p}\phi\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}=\omega_{\phi^{\prime},p}(\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}(p))^{-1}\left<U_{p}\phi,\phi^{\prime}\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}.

if
\phi^{\prime}
has central character
\omega_{\phi^{\prime}}
. Here
\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the isomorphism from
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}
to
\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

The
p
-adic Petersson inner product in (
7.8.1
) induces the
p
-adic Petersson inner product for
p
-adic families on
\U(2)
:

(7.8.2)

\left<\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}:\mathcal{M}eas\big(U_{\mathcal{K},p},e_{\mathrm{ord}}V^{\prime}_{\U(2),\,\xi^{-1}}\big)^{\natural}\times\mathcal{M}eas\left(U_{\mathcal{K},p},V_{\U(2),\,\xi}\right)^{\natural}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}eas\big(U_{\mathcal{K},p},\mathcal{O}_{L}\big)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket U_{\mathcal{K},p}\rrbracket.

7.9.
The Rallis inner product formulas for
\big<\boldmath\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
and
\big<\boldmath h,\tilde{h}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
.

From the description of our strategy in §
7.5
, in particular (
7.5.13
), we see that we need a formula relating
\big<\bm{\theta},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{2}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
and
\big<\bm{h},\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
to certain
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{L3}{{{}}\mathcal{L}_{3},\mathcal{L}_{4}}\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(U_{\mathcal{K},p},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\simeq\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket U_{\mathcal{K},p}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
be the
L
-function interpolating the special value at
s=1
of
L\left(s,\pi^{\GL_{2}}_{\theta},\mathrm{Ad}\right)
,

, which factorize as

\displaystyle L\left(s,\pi^{\GL_{2}}_{\theta},\mathrm{Ad}\right)

\displaystyle=L\left(s,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)L\left(s,\lambda^{2}\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right),

\displaystyle L\left(s,\pi^{\GL_{2}}_{h},\mathrm{Ad}\right)

\displaystyle=L\left(s,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)L\left(s,\lambda^{-2}\chi^{-1}_{h}\chi^{c}_{h}\tau^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right)

\displaystyle=L\left(s,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)L\left(s,\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi^{-c}_{h}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right).

More precisely, for
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
as in Theorem
5.9.1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi^{-1}\cdot L^{\infty}\left(1,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{2}\pi^{-2}\cdot\gamma_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(0,\lambda^{2}\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right)^{-1}\cdot L^{p\infty}\left(1,\lambda^{2}\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{4}\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi^{-1}\cdot L^{\infty}\left(1,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{2}\pi^{-2}\cdot\gamma_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(0,\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi^{-c}_{h}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right)^{-1}\cdot L^{p\infty}\left(1,\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi^{-c}_{h}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right).

The existence of
\mathcal{L}_{3},\mathcal{L}_{4}
follows from Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Proposition 7.9.1
.

There exists
C_{\theta},C_{h}\in L^{\times}
such that

\displaystyle\big<\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{3},\bm{\theta}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}

\displaystyle=C_{\theta}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{3},

\displaystyle\big<\bm{h},\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}

\displaystyle=C_{h}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{4}.

(See (
7.8.2
) for the definition of the
p
-adic Petersson inner product for families.)

Proof.

The proof is the same as
[

, Proposition 8.9]
, which computes the specializations of the left hand side by using the Rallis inner product formula.
∎

7.10.
Extending CM forms on
\U(2)
to
\GU(2)

Let
H=\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q})Z_{\GU(2)}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then
H
is a subgroup of
\GU(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
of index
2
consisting of
g\in\GU(2)(\mathbb{A})
with
\nu(g)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\mathrm{Nm}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}})
. Suppose that
\phi
is an automorphic form on
\U(2)
and generates an irreducible automorphic representation
\pi_{\phi}
of
\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. Let
\omega:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a Hecke character extending the central character of
\pi_{\phi}
. We can first extend
\phi
to a function on
H
by

\displaystyle\gamma ag_{1}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\omega(a)\phi(g_{1}),

\displaystyle\gamma\in\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q}),\,a\in Z_{\GU(2)}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}),\,g_{1}\in\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}),

and then extend this function by zero from
H
to
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. We denote this extension of
\phi
to
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
by
\breve{\phi}
. This
\breve{\phi}
is an automorphic form on
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.

Proposition 7.10.1
.

Suppose that
\pi_{\phi}
is of CM type, i.e. if
\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{\phi})\simeq\mathrm{BC}(\chi_{1})\lambda\oplus\mathrm{BC}(\chi_{2})\lambda
for some characters
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
of
\U(1)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(1)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then
\breve{\phi}
generates an irreducible automorphic representation
\pi_{\breve{\phi}}
of
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A})
and
\pi_{\breve{\phi}}|_{\U(2)}\simeq\pi_{\phi}\oplus\pi^{\prime}_{\phi}
, where
\pi^{\prime}_{\phi}
is isomorphic to the conjugation of
\pi_{\phi}
by an element in
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
outside
H
. Moreover, if
\phi\in\pi_{\phi}
is a pure tensor in
\pi_{\phi}\simeq\bigotimes_{v}\pi_{\phi,v}
, then
\breve{\phi}
is also a pure tensor in
\pi_{\breve{\phi}}\simeq\bigotimes_{v}\pi_{\breve{\phi},v}
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma
be the automorphic representation of
\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by
\breve{\phi}
. Then either
\sigma
is irreducible and
\sigma\simeq\sigma\otimes\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\circ\nu
or
\sigma\simeq\sigma_{1}\oplus\sigma_{2}
with
\sigma_{1}\not\simeq\sigma_{2}
and
\sigma_{2}\simeq\sigma_{1}\otimes\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\circ\nu
. If
\pi_{\phi}
is of CM type and
\sigma_{1}
is a direct summand of
\sigma
, we know that
\sigma_{1}\simeq\sigma_{1}\otimes\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\circ\nu
. Hence,
\sigma
must be irreducible. (See also
[
Wan20
, Lemma 8.1]
.) We have
\pi_{\breve{\phi}}\simeq\pi_{\phi}
. Since
\breve{\phi}
is obtained by extension by zero, it belongs to the direct summand
\pi_{\phi}
in
\pi_{\breve{\phi}}|_{\U(2)}\simeq\pi_{\phi}\oplus\pi^{\prime}_{\phi}
. Hence,
\phi
being a pure tensor in
\pi_{\phi}
implies that
\breve{\phi}
is a pure tensor in
\pi_{\breve{\phi}}
.
∎

The above extension from
\U(2)
to
\GU(2)
also works for
p
-adic automorphic forms. We denote by

˘

h

the extension of
\bm{h}
whose specialization at
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
is the extension by zero of
\bm{h}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})
to
\GU(2)
with respect to the character
\chi^{-1}_{\theta}\tau^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}
. Similarly, We denote by

˘

~

h

3

(resp.
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{thetaext}{{{}}\breve{\bm{\theta}}},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{thetaext}{{{}}\breve{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}_{3}}
) the extension of
\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}
whose specialization at
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
is the extension by zero of
\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})
to
\GU(2)
with respect to the character
\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}
(resp.
\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}},\chi^{-1}_{\theta}\tau^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}
).

7.11.
The nonvanishing property of the degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of the Klingen Eisenstein family

Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{L5}{{{}}\mathcal{L}_{5},\mathcal{L}_{6}}\in\mathcal{M}eas\left(U_{\mathcal{K},p},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\simeq\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket U_{\mathcal{K},p}\rrbracket
be the Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions interpolating special values of
L\left(s,\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\right)
,
L\left(s,\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\,\tau^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}(\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{c}\right)
. In particular, for a Hecke character
\tau:\mathcal{K}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
unramified away from
p\infty
of
\infty
-type
\left(0,k\right)
with
k\geq 6
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{5}\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{{\tt N}k}\frac{\Gamma({\tt N}k-1)}{(2\pi i)^{{\tt N}k-1}}\cdot\gamma_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(\frac{4-{\tt N}k}{2},(\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau^{{\tt N}}_{0})^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{{\tt N}k-2}{2},\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{6}\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{{\tt N}k-2}\frac{\Gamma({\tt N}k-2)}{(2\pi i)^{{\tt N}k-2}}\cdot L_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\frac{{\tt N}k-2}{2},\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}(\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{c}\right),

\displaystyle\quad\times L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{{\tt N}k-2}{2},\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}(\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{c}\right).

The product of
\mathcal{L}_{5}\mathcal{L}_{6}
interpolates the special values of
L(s,\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h})\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})
where
\pi_{h}
is the automorphic representation associated to specializations of the CM family
\bm{h}
. Note that the restriction of
\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}(\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{c}
to
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}
is
\left(\xi_{0,\mathfrak{p}}\xi_{0,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau^{\tt N}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}\tau^{\tt N}_{0,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathrm{triv}\right)
, so
\mathcal{L}_{6}
is essentially of one-variable. In fact
\mathcal{L}_{6}
is the so-called “improved”
p
-adic
L
-function, for which the local factor at
p
in the interpolation formula is a partial local
L
-factor instead of a partial local
\gamma
-factor.

Proposition 7.11.1
.

For
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\in\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[\GU(2)\big(\mathbb{Q}_{v}\big)\right]
such that
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\bm{h}
is still invariant under the tame level group
K^{p}_{f}
(defined (
5.6.8
)),
\varphi
inside the space (
5.6.12
), and
u_{1},\dots,u_{r}\in\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
,
b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
as in Proposition
7.6.1
,

(7.11.1)

\left<\left(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{\bm{h}}\right)|_{\U(2)},\mathpzc{P}_{N,\ast}\left(l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum\nolimits_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)\right)\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}=\mathcal{C}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{5}\mathcal{L}_{6}\cdot\left<\left(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{\bm{h}}\right)|_{\U(2)},\bm{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}\varphi\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}},

where
\bm{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}=\bm{\theta}\cdot(\bar{\lambda}\circ\det)
and
\mathcal{C}_{1}\in\left(\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket U_{\mathcal{K},p}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\right)^{\times}
.

Proof.

The identity is proved by comparing the evaluations of both sides at
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
as in Theorem
5.9.1
. For such a
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
, put
\theta_{\tau}=\bm{\theta}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}})
and
h_{\tau}=\bm{h}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}})
, which are classical ordinary CM forms. The description of
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}b_{i}\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u_{i}}\right)
in Proposition
7.6.1
and the definition of the
p
-adic Petersson inner product in §
7.8
imply that

(7.11.2)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\right)^{-2{\tt N}k+2}\cdot\text{LHS of \eqref{eq:FJ-h} evaluated at $\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}$}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{\Sigma\,\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\theta_{\tau}\left(g_{2}\varrho^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}\right)\varphi^{\bar{\xi}_{0}\bar{\tau}^{\tt N}_{0}}(g_{2})\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}

\displaystyle\left.E^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2}\left(\imath_{0}(g_{1},g_{2});f_{2}(s,\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)\xi^{-1}_{0}\tau^{-{\tt N}}_{0}\lambda(\det g_{1})\cdot(U^{-m}_{p}\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{h}_{\tau})\left(g_{1}\begin{pmatrix}&1&\\
-p^{m}\end{pmatrix}_{p}\right)\,dg_{1}dg_{2}\,\right|_{s=\frac{{\tt N}k-3}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}\left(g_{2}\right)\varphi(g_{2})\,h^{\prime}_{\tau}(g_{2})\,dg_{2}

with
\imath_{0}
defined in (
6.2.2
) and

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{\tau}(g_{2})

\displaystyle=d^{\Sigma\,\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{2}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\left.\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}f_{2,v}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)\left(\imath_{0}\left(\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}_{p}g,1\right)\right)\cdot(U^{-m}_{p}\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{h}_{\tau})\left(g_{2}g\begin{pmatrix}&1&\\
-p^{m}\end{pmatrix}_{p}\right)\,dg\,\right|_{s=\frac{{\tt N}k-3}{2}},

where
m
is any sufficiently large integer. The integral over
g
is essentially doubling local zeta integral.

By using the same computation results in the proof of Theorem
6.1.1
for all
v\neq p
, we obtain

(7.11.3)

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{\tau}(g_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{1}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})\cdot\frac{\Gamma({\tt N}k-1)\Gamma({\tt N}k-2)}{(2\pi i)^{2{\tt N}k-3}}\cdot L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{{\tt N}k-2}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h_{\tau}})\times\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left.\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}f_{2,p}\left(s,\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)\left(\imath_{0}\left(\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}_{p}g,1\right)\right)(U^{-m}_{p}\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{h}_{\tau})\left(g_{2}g\begin{pmatrix}&1&\\
-p^{m}\end{pmatrix}_{p}\right)\,dg\,\right|_{s=\frac{{\tt N}k-3}{2}},

where
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is the product of

•

the local doubling zeta integrals at
v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
, which is a nonzero constant in
L
by our choice, and

•

the element in
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket U_{\mathcal{K},p}\rrbracket
interpolating
L_{q}\left(\frac{{\tt N}k-2}{2},\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0}\right)^{-1}
, which is a unit by our assumption.

Our choice of
\chi_{h}
,
\chi_{\theta}
implies that
L_{v}\left(s,\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h_{\tau}})\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)=1
for
v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
. The computation at
p
is slightly different from that in the construction of
p
-adic
L
-functions because the section
f_{2,p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})
is slightly different from the one for
p
-adic
L
-functions. The Schwartz function
\alpha^{\prime}_{\xi\tau,p}
in the description of
f_{2,p}(s,\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})
in Proposition
7.6.1
only requires
x_{12}
to be supported on
p^{-t}\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
. Unlike the section for constructing
p
-adic
L
-functions,
\alpha_{\xi\tau,p}
does not require the whole
\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}&x_{12}\\
x_{21}&x_{22}\end{pmatrix}
to be supported on
p^{-t}\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. (In fact,
f_{2,p}(s,\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1})
is essentially of the same type as the section in
[
LR20
, Table 2 on p.210]
for constructing the “improved”
p
-adic
L
-function.) The computation at
p
is done in
[
Wan20
, 6I]
, and plugging it into (
7.11.3
), we get

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{\tau}(g_{2})=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{C}_{1}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})\cdot\frac{\Gamma({\tt N}k-1)\Gamma({\tt N}k-2)}{(2\pi i)^{2{\tt N}k-3}}\cdot L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{{\tt N}k-2}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi_{h_{\tau}})\times\xi_{0}\tau_{0}\lambda^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\gamma_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(\frac{4-{\tt N}k}{2},(\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\xi_{0}\tau^{{\tt N}}_{0})^{-1}\right)L_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\frac{{\tt N}k-2}{2},\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}(\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\times(U^{-m}_{p}\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{h}_{\tau})\left(g_{2}\begin{pmatrix}&1&\\
-p^{m}\end{pmatrix}_{p}\right).

Plugging this into (
7.11.2
), we see that the evaluations at
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
of the two sides of (
7.11.1
) are equal.

∎

We introduce some more

-adic

-functions. Let

,

\displaystyle\quad\times L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right).

. Let

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\lambda^{2}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\chi_{h,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\chi_{\theta,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau^{-{\tt N}}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(p)p^{1/2}\right)^{-2t}

\displaystyle=p^{-t}\cdot\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\left(\lambda_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{2}\chi_{\theta,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\chi^{-1}_{\theta,\mathfrak{p}}\tau^{-{\tt N}}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(p)\right)^{-t}\cdot\mathfrak{g}\left((\xi_{0}\tau^{\tt N}_{0})^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\left(\lambda_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{2}\chi_{h,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\chi^{-1}_{h,\mathfrak{p}}\tau^{-{\tt N}}_{0,\mathfrak{p}}(p)\right)^{-t}

\displaystyle=p^{-t}\cdot\gamma_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(0,\lambda^{2}\chi_{\theta}\chi^{-c}_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right)^{-1}\cdot\gamma_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(0,\lambda^{2}\chi_{h}\chi^{-c}_{h}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\tau^{-c}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}\right)^{-1}.

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathcal{L}_{1}\mathcal{L}_{2}}{\mathcal{L}_{3}\mathcal{L}_{4}}\right)\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}}\right)=
