\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi\frac{L^{\infty}\left(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)}{\left<f,\bar{f}\right>}\cdot p^{-t}\,\frac{L_{p}\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\right)}{L_{p}\left(1,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)^{2}\cdot L_{p}\left(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{L^{p\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\pi^{\GL_{2}}_{h}\times\pi^{\GL_{2}}_{\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}}\right)}{L^{p\infty}\left(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)\cdot L^{p\infty}\left(1,\pi^{\GL_{2}}_{\theta},\mathrm{Ad}\right)\cdot L^{p\infty}\left(1,\pi^{\GL_{2}}_{h},\mathrm{Ad}\right)}.

Proposition 7.11.2
.

There exist operators
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}},\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{ns}}\in\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[\GU(2)\big(\mathbb{Q}_{v}\big)\right]
, and
\varphi
inside the space (
5.6.12
),
\varphi^{\prime}
inside the space (
5.6.11
) such that
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\bm{h}
is invariant under the tame level group
K^{p}_{f}
(defined in (
5.6.8
)) and

(7.11.6)

\displaystyle\left<\left(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{\bm{h}}\right)|_{\U(2)},\bm{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}\varphi\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\left<\Big(\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{\tilde{\bm{h}}}_{3}\Big)|_{\U(2)},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\lambda}_{3}\varphi^{\prime}\right>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}=C_{2}\cdot\big<\bm{\theta},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\big<\bm{h},\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{L}_{1}\mathcal{L}_{2}}{\mathcal{L}_{3}\mathcal{L}_{4}}

with
C_{2}\in L^{\times}
.

Proof.

For
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}},\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{ns}}\in\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[\GU(2)\big(\mathbb{Q}_{v}\big)\right]
and
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}
in (
5.6.12
) corresponding to pure tensors in
\bigotimes_{v}\pi^{\GU(2)}_{v}
, we consider evaluations at
\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
as in Theorem
5.9.1
with
\mathrm{cond}(\tau_{\mathfrak{p}})=p^{t}
. Put
\theta_{\tau}=\bm{\theta}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})
and
h_{\tau}=\bm{h}(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}})
.

(7.11.7)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{LHS of \eqref{eq:triple-L}}\\
\text{valued at $\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}$}\end{array}=

\displaystyle\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}(g)\,\varphi(g)\,\left(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{h}_{\tau}\right)\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\scriptscriptstyle p}\right)\,dg

\displaystyle\times\int_{\U(2)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\tilde{\theta}^{\lambda}_{\tau}(g)\,\varphi^{\prime}(g)\,\left(\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{\tilde{h}}_{\tau}\right)\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\scriptscriptstyle p}\right)\,dg.

We can replace
\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}
(resp.
\tilde{\theta}^{\lambda}_{\tau}
) in the integrand by
\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}
(resp.
\breve{\tilde{\theta}}^{\lambda}_{\tau}
), its extension by zero to
\GU(2)
with respect the character
\chi_{\theta}\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\tau_{\tt N}}
(resp.
\chi^{-1}_{\theta}\tau^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p},\tau_{\tt N}}
) (as described at the beginning of §
7.10
), and replace the domain of integration by
\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q})Z_{\GU(2)}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. Thanks to (
5.4.3
), we can further replace
\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q})Z_{\GU(2)}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\backslash\GU(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
by
D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q})Z_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. Then for
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}},\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{ns}}\in\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]\subset\bigotimes\limits_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]
, we can rewrite (
7.11.7
)

(7.11.8)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{LHS of \eqref{eq:triple-L}}\\
\text{valued at $\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}$}\end{array}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}(g)\,\varphi(g)\,(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{h}_{\tau})\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\scriptscriptstyle p}\right)\,dg

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\breve{\tilde{\theta}}^{\lambda}_{\tau}(g)\,\varphi^{\prime}(g)\,(\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{\tilde{h}}_{\tau})\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{\scriptscriptstyle p}\right)\,dg.

This is the triple product integral.

Let

\displaystyle F_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\left(\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}\otimes\varphi\otimes\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{h}_{\tau}\right)|_{D^{\times}(\mathbb{A})},

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\left(\breve{\tilde{\theta}}^{\lambda}_{3,\tau}\otimes\varphi^{\prime}\otimes\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{ns}}\breve{\tilde{h}}_{3,\tau}\right)|_{D^{\times}(\mathbb{A})}.

Then
F_{\tau}
generates
\Pi_{\tau}=\pi^{D}_{\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}}\otimes\pi^{D}_{h_{\tau}}\otimes\pi^{D}
, where
\pi^{D}_{\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}}
(resp.
\pi^{D}_{h_{\tau}}
,
\pi^{D}
) is the automorphic representation of
D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by the restriction of
\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}
(resp.
\breve{h}_{\tau}
,
\varphi
) to
D^{\times}
, and
F^{\prime}_{\tau}
generates
\tilde{\Pi}_{\tau}
, the contragredient representation of
\Pi_{\tau}
.
By the isomorphism (
5.4.3
) and Proposition
7.10.1
, we know that
\pi^{D}_{\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}}
,
\pi^{D}_{h_{\tau}}
are irreducible, and
\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau}
,
\breve{h}_{\tau}
are pure tensors. By the definition of
\varphi
, we know that
\pi^{D}_{\varphi}=\pi^{D}
. Hence,
\Pi_{\tau}
(resp.
\tilde{\Pi}_{\tau}
) is irreducible, and the image of
F_{\tau}
(resp.
F^{\prime}_{\tau}
) under
\Pi_{\tau}\simeq\bigotimes_{v}\Pi_{\tau,v}
(
\tilde{\Pi}_{\tau}\simeq\bigotimes_{v}\tilde{\Pi}_{\tau,v}
) is a pure tensor
\bigotimes_{v}F_{\tau,v}
(resp.
\bigotimes_{v}F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}
). By Ichino’s triple product formula
[
Ich08
]
, (
7.11.8
) becomes

(7.11.9)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{LHS of \eqref{eq:triple-L}}\\
\text{valued at $\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}$}\end{array}=

\displaystyle\prod_{v}\int_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})}\big<\Pi_{v}(g_{v})F_{\tau,v},F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}\big>_{v}\,dg_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big<f^{D},\bar{f}^{D}\big>\big<\theta_{\tau},\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\big<h_{\tau},\tilde{h}_{3,\tau}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\cdot\frac{\zeta^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}(2)^{2}\cdot L^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\left(\frac{1}{2},\Pi\right)}{L^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\left(1,\Pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\big<\Pi_{v}(g)F_{\tau,v},F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}\big>_{v}\,dg}{\big<f^{D}_{v},\bar{f}^{D}_{v}\big>_{v}\big<\theta_{\tau,v},\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{v}\big<h_{\tau,v},\tilde{h}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{v}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\big<\Pi_{p}(g)F_{\tau,p},F^{\prime}_{\tau,p}\big>_{p}\,dg}{\big<f^{D}_{p},\bar{f}^{D}_{p}\big>_{p}\big<\theta_{\tau,p},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{p}\big<h_{\tau,p},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\tilde{h}_{3,\tau,p}\big>_{p}}

We have the following results for the local integrals at
v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\infty,p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
:

–

v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\infty,\ell\}
.

\frac{\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\big<\Pi_{v}(g)\phi_{v},\phi^{\prime}_{v}\big>_{v}\,dg}{\big<f^{D}_{v},\bar{f}^{D}_{v}\big>_{v}\big<\theta_{\tau,v},\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{v}\big<h_{\tau,v},\tilde{h}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{v}}=\text{a nonzero constant independent of $\tau$}

For
v=\infty
, the integral is
1
because
\Pi_{\infty}
is the trivial representation. For
v=\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
, this is
[
Wan20
, Lemmas 8.13-8.17]
. Note that the twist at
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\ell\}
in (
5.6.10
) guarantees that the local integrals are nonzero constants.

–

v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
. There exist
\mathscr{T}_{v},\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{v}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]\subset\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]
and
\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\in\pi^{\GU(2)}_{v}
such that
\varphi_{v}
and
\mathscr{T}_{v}\bm{h}
are invariant under the level group
K_{v}
(defined in (
5.6.7
)) and

\frac{\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\big<\Pi_{v}(g)F_{\tau,v},F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}\big>_{v}\,dg}{\big<f^{D}_{v},\bar{f}^{D}_{v}\big>_{v}\big<\theta_{\tau,v},\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{v}\big<h_{\tau,v},\tilde{h}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{v}}=\text{a nonzero constant independent of $\tau$}

This is proved in the following §
7.12
.

–

v=p
.

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\big<\Pi_{p}(g)F_{\tau,p},F^{\prime}_{\tau,p}\big>_{p}\,dg}{\big<f^{D}_{p},\bar{f}^{D}_{p}\big>_{p}\big<\theta_{\tau,p},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau,v}\big>_{p}\big<h_{\tau,p},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{t}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\tilde{h}_{3,\tau,p}\big>_{p}}=\,p^{-t}\,\frac{\zeta_{p}(2)^{2}\cdot L_{p}\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\right)}{L_{p}\left(1,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)^{2}\cdot L_{p}\left(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)}.

This is a direct consequence of
[
Hsi19
, Proposition 6.3]
. We also use the formulas for local norms of the chosen vectors in the proof of Lemma 6.4 of
loc.cit.
.

Plug these results on local integrals into (
7.11.9
) and compare with (
7.11.5
), we see that

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{LHS of \eqref{eq:triple-L} }\text{valued at $\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}$}\end{array}=C_{2}\cdot\big<\theta_{\tau},\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\big<h_{\tau},\tilde{h}_{3,\tau}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\cdot\left(\frac{\mathcal{L}_{1}\mathcal{L}_{2}}{\mathcal{L}_{3}\mathcal{L}_{4}}\right)\left(\tau_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}}\right)

with
C_{2}\in L^{\times}
the product of

•

the local triple product integrals divided by the norms at
v\in\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
, which are nonzero constants,

•

\prod\limits_{v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\Big(\frac{\zeta_{v}(2)^{2}L_{v}\left(\frac{1}{2},\Pi\right)}{L_{v}\left(1,\Pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)}\Big)^{-1}
, which, by our choice of the Hecke characters
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
, equals
L_{q}\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\chi_{h}\chi^{c}_{\theta}\right)^{-1}\prod\limits_{v\in\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}\zeta_{v}(2)^{-2}L_{v}\left(1,\eta_{\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}}\right)^{2}L_{v}\left(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)
, a nonzero constant,

•

\left(\pi\frac{L^{\infty}\left(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad}\right)}{\left<f,\bar{f}\right>}\cdot\big<f^{D},\bar{f}^{D}\big>\right)^{-1}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
.

∎

Given
g\in\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f})
, evaluating a
p
-adic form at
g
induces a map

\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},V_{\U(2)}\widehat{\otimes}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)^{\natural}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\simeq\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket.

Denote by
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)(g)\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
the image of
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)
under this map.

Proposition 7.11.3
.

Let
\beta=1\in\Her_{1}(\mathcal{K})
,
\theta^{J}_{1}
be the Fourier–Jacobi form on
\U(2)
as in Proposition
7.6.1
, and
\varphi
be an automorphic form on
\U(2)
as in Proposition
7.11.2
. Let
J\subseteq\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
be the ideal generated by

\displaystyle l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)(g),

\displaystyle g\in\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f}),\quad u\in\hskip-5.0pt\bigotimes_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v}).

Then
J=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
J\neq\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
. Then
J
is contained in a maximal ideal of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, so is contained in the maximal ideal associated to a
p
-adic character
\kappa:\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}
. Let
\kappa^{\prime}:\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}
be a
p
-adic character whose
{\tt N}
-th power is
\kappa
. Then

\mathpzc{P}_{{\tt N},*}\left(l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)\right)(\kappa^{\prime}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}})=l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)(\kappa)=0

for all
u\in\bigotimes_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
. Then it follows from Propositions
7.11.1
and
7.11.2
that

\left(\big<\bm{\theta},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\big<\bm{h},\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}\big>_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{L}_{1}\mathcal{L}_{2}\mathcal{L}_{5}\mathcal{L}_{6}}{\mathcal{L}_{3}\mathcal{L}_{4}}\right)(\kappa^{\prime}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}})=0,

which, combining with Proposition
7.9.1
, implies that

\left(\mathcal{L}_{1}\mathcal{L}_{2}\mathcal{L}_{5}\mathcal{L}_{6}\right)(\kappa^{\prime}|_{U_{\mathcal{K},p}})=0.

However, the condition (3) in our choice of
\chi_{h},\chi_{\theta}
in §
5.6
implies that
\mathcal{L}_{1}\mathcal{L}_{2}\mathcal{L}_{5}\mathcal{L}_{6}
is a unit in
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, so is nonzero at all characters in
\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(U_{\mathcal{K},p},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{p}\right)
. We get a contradiction. Therefore,
J=\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.
∎

7.12.
The local triple product integrals at non-split places

Proposition 7.12.1
.

For
v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
, there exist
\mathscr{T}_{v},\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{v}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]\subset\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]
and
\varphi_{v},\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\in\pi^{D}_{v}
such that
\varphi_{v}
and
\mathscr{T}_{v}\bm{h}
are invariant under the level group
K_{v}
(defined in (
5.6.7
)), and

(7.12.1)

\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\big<\Pi_{v}(g)F_{\tau,v},F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}\big>_{v}\,dg=\text{a nonzero constant independent of $\tau$},

where
F_{\tau,v}=\left(\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau,v}\otimes\varphi_{v}\otimes\mathscr{T}_{v}\breve{h}_{\tau,v}\right)|_{D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}
,
F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}=\left(\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{v}\breve{\tilde{\theta}}^{\lambda}_{3,\tau,v}\otimes\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\otimes\mathscr{T}^{\prime\prime}_{v}\breve{\tilde{h}}_{3,\tau,v}\right)|_{D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}
.

Proof.

Let
v
be a place in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}
. Then
\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\subset\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},v}
and
\tau_{\mathfrak{p},\mathpzc{P}_{\tt N}}|_{\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}
is trivial. From the construction of
\bm{h},\tilde{\bm{h}}_{3}
in §
7.7.2
and
\bm{\theta},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{3}
in §
7.7.3
, we see that
\pi_{h_{\tau},v}
,
\pi_{\theta_{\tau},v}
and
h_{\tau,v},\tilde{h}_{3,\tau,v},\theta_{\tau,v},\tilde{\theta}_{3,\tau,v}
do not change when
\tau
varies. It follows that the left hand side of (
7.12.1
) is independent of
\tau
.

Next we show the non-vanishing. By our choice of
\chi_{h},\chi_{\theta}
, we have
\Hom_{D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\left(\Pi_{v},\mathbb{C}\right)\neq 0
. By
[
Pra90
, Theorem 1.4]
, we can pick
\mathscr{T}^{\prime}_{v}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]
and
\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\in\pi^{D}_{v}
such that the linear functional

(7.12.2)

\mathscr{L}_{F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}}:\Pi_{v}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C},\quad\quad\mathscr{L}_{F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}}(F_{v})=\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}\backslash D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\big<\Pi_{v}(g)F_{v},F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}\big>_{v}\,dg

is nonzero and spans the one-dimensional space
\Hom_{D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\left(\Pi_{v},\mathbb{C}\right)
. What we need to show is that there exists
\mathscr{T}_{v}\breve{h}_{\tau,v}\in\pi^{D}_{h_{\tau},v}
and
\varphi_{v}\in\pi^{D}_{v}
invariant under the level group
K_{v}
defined in (
5.6.7
) such that
\mathscr{L}_{F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}}\left(\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau,v}\otimes\varphi_{v}\otimes\mathscr{T}_{v}\breve{h}_{\tau,v}\right)\neq 0
. Since
\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau,v}
is invariant under
K_{v}
, it suffices to show that there exists
\mathscr{T}_{v}\breve{h}_{\tau,v}\in\pi^{D}_{h_{\tau},v}
invariant under
K_{v}
such that the linear functional

(7.12.3)

\mathscr{L}_{F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}}\left(\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau,v}\otimes\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\otimes\mathscr{T}_{v}\breve{h}_{\tau,v}\right):\pi_{v}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

is nonzero. There are two cases.

Case 1
:
v\neq q
,
D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\cong\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
. Let
\varphi_{v}
denote the prime number corresponding to
v
. Recall that at the beginning of §
5.4
, we have fixed the positive integer
\mathfrak{s}
and totally imaginary element
\delta
in
\mathcal{K}
, and
\U(2)
is the unitary group for the skew-Hermitian form
\zeta_{0}=\delta\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{s}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
. By our assumption on
\mathfrak{s}
, there exists
\mathfrak{r}\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},v}
such that
\mathrm{Nm}_{\mathcal{K}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}(\mathfrak{r})=-\mathfrak{s}
. We fix the following isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}:\GU(2)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\GU(1,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v}),

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\longmapsto\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\delta}{2}&\frac{\delta\mathfrak{r}}{2}\\
1&-\mathfrak{r}\end{pmatrix}g\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\delta}{2}&\frac{\delta\mathfrak{r}}{2}\\
1&-\mathfrak{r}\end{pmatrix}^{-1},

where
\GU(1,1)
denotes the unitary group attached to the skew-Hermitian form
\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}.
Let
K^{\GL_{2}}_{v}=\mathfrak{i}(K_{v})\cap\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})
. By our assumptions in §
5.2
,
v\neq 2
. One can check that

K^{\GL_{2}}_{v}=\begin{pmatrix}1+\varpi^{c_{v}}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}&\varpi^{c_{v}+\epsilon_{v}}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}\\
\varpi^{c_{v}}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}&1+\varpi^{c_{v}}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}\end{pmatrix}\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{v}).

where
\epsilon_{v}=\begin{cases}0,&v\text{ inert}\\
1,&v\text{ ramified}\end{cases}
.

Given a character
\chi_{v}:\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, we denote also by
\chi_{v}
the character
B(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
,
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\chi_{v}(ad^{-1})
, and
\Ind_{B}^{\GL_{2}}(\chi_{v})
the (normalized) induction of
\chi_{v}
. By our assumption on
\pi
,
\pi_{v}
is either isomorphic to
\Ind_{B}^{\GL(2)}(\chi_{v})
for an unramified character
\chi_{v}\neq|\cdot|^{\pm 1/2}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
or is isomorphic to the irreducible sub-representation of
\Ind_{B}^{\GL(2)}(\chi_{v})
with
\chi_{v}=|\cdot|^{-1/2}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
. Let
\psi_{1}=\mathbf{e}_{v}
, the additive character of
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
defined in (
1.0.5
),
\psi_{2}=\bar{\mathbf{e}}_{v}
, and
W_{1}
(resp.
W_{2}
) be the Kirillov model of
\pi^{D}_{\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau},v}
(resp.
\pi^{D}_{h_{\tau},v}
) with respect to
\psi_{1}
(resp.
\psi_{2}
). We have

(7.12.4)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\left(W_{1}\otimes W_{2},\tilde{\pi}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\left(W_{1}\otimes W_{2},\Ind^{\GL_{2}}_{B}(\chi^{-1}_{v})\right)

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{B(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}\left(W_{1}\otimes W_{2},\chi^{-1}_{v}|\cdot|^{1/2}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\right),

where the second isomorphism is deduced from the Frobenius reciprocity, and when
\chi_{v}=|\cdot|^{-1/2}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
, the first isomorphism is because by our choice of
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
,
W_{1}\not\cong\tilde{W}_{2}
. Our choice of
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
also implies that
W_{1},W_{2}
are supercuspidal, so they consist of Schwartz functions on
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}
on which the action
B(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
has the formula

(7.12.5)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}\cdot w_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=\omega_{\pi_{j}}(d)\,\psi_{j}(bd^{-1}x)\,w_{j}(ad^{-1}x),

\displaystyle w_{j}\in W_{j},

where
\omega_{\pi_{j}}
is the central character of
\pi_{j}
. By the way we extend
\bm{h}
and
\bm{\theta}
in §
7.10
, we know that
\omega_{\pi_{1}}\omega_{\pi_{2}}=\mathrm{triv}
. It is easy to see that the map

(7.12.6)

\mathscr{L}(w_{1},w_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}}w_{1}(x)w_{2}(x)\cdot\chi_{v}(x)|x|^{-1/2}_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\,d^{\times}x,\quad\quad w_{1}\in W_{1},\,w_{2}\in W_{2},

is a nonzero element in (
7.12.4
), which is one dimensional, so it equals the image of
\mathscr{L}_{F^{\prime}_{\tau,v}}\left(\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau,v}\otimes\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\otimes\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\displaystyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\textstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\,\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,\,$}}}}}\right)
in (
7.12.4
) up to a nonzero scalar. Hence, showing the existence of
\breve{h}_{\tau,v}\in\pi^{D}_{h_{\tau},v}
invariant under
K_{v}
such that (
7.12.3
) is nonzero reduces to showing the existence of
\mathpzc{y}_{2}\in W_{2}
invariant under
K^{\GL_{2}}_{v}
such that
\mathscr{L}(\mathpzc{y}_{1},\mathpzc{y}_{2})\neq 0
, where
\mathpzc{y}_{1}\in W_{1}
is the vector invariant under
K^{\GL_{2}}_{v}
corresponding to
\breve{\theta}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau,v}\in\pi^{D}_{\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau},v}
.

For a character
\vartheta:\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, put

\mathpzc{y}_{1,\vartheta}=\int_{\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v}}\vartheta^{-1}(a)\mathpzc{y}_{1}(ax)\,dx,

the projection of
\mathpzc{y}_{1}
to the
\vartheta
-eigenspace for the action of
\mathrm{diag}(\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v},1)
. Let
\vartheta^{*}
be the extension of
\vartheta
to
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}
taking value
1
at
\varpi_{v}
. For each integer
m
, put define
w^{m}_{j,\vartheta}\in W_{j}
,
j=1,2
, as

w^{m}_{j,\vartheta}(x)=\mathds{1}_{\varpi^{m}_{v}\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v}}(x)\,\vartheta^{*}(x).

Then we have
\mathpzc{y}_{1,\vartheta}=\sum_{m}c_{m}w^{m}_{1,\vartheta}
with
c_{m}\in\mathbb{C}
finitely many nonzero, and
\mathscr{L}(\mathpzc{y}_{1},w^{m}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}})=\mathscr{L}(\mathpzc{y}_{1,\vartheta},w^{m}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}})
. We can take a
\vartheta
of conductor
\leq\varpi^{c_{v}}_{v}
such that
\mathpzc{y}_{1,\vartheta}\neq 0
. This
\mathpzc{y}_{1,\vartheta}
is still invariant under
K^{\GL_{2}}_{v}
. By the formula (
7.12.5
) and (1) of Lemma
7.12.2
proved below, we deduce that

\mathpzc{y}_{1,\vartheta}=\sum_{m=-(c_{v}+\epsilon_{v})}^{c_{v}-n(w^{0}_{1,\vartheta})}c_{m}w^{m}_{1,\vartheta},\quad\quad c_{m}\in\mathbb{C},

with
n(w^{0}_{1,\vartheta}
defined as in (
7.12.8
). Take
-(c_{v}+\epsilon_{v})\leq m_{0}\leq c_{v}-n(w^{0}_{1,\vartheta})
such that
c_{m_{0}}\neq 0
. Then

\mathscr{L}(\mathpzc{y}_{1},w^{m_{0}}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}})=c_{m_{0}}\mathscr{L}(w^{m_{0}}_{1,\vartheta},w^{m_{0}}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}})\neq 0,

and by the formula (
7.12.5
), we know that
w^{m_{0}}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}}
is invariant under
\begin{pmatrix}1+\varpi^{c_{v}}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}&\varpi^{c_{v}+\epsilon_{v}}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}\\
0&1+\varpi^{c_{v}}_{v}\end{pmatrix}
, and by (2) of Lemma
7.12.2
, we know that

n(w^{m_{0}}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}})=m_{0}+c(\pi_{2}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1})\leq c_{v}-n(w^{0}_{1,\vartheta})+c(\pi_{2}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1})=c_{v}-c(\pi_{1}\otimes\vartheta^{*})+c(\pi_{2}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1}).

By our choice of
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
and the formula (
7.12.7
), we have
c(\pi_{1}\otimes\vartheta^{*})\geq c(\pi_{2}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1})
. Hence,
n(w^{m_{0}}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}})\geq c_{v}
, and
w^{m_{0}}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}}
is invariant under
\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\varpi^{c_{v}}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}&1\end{pmatrix}
. Therefore,
\mathpzc{y}_{2}=w^{m_{0}}_{2,\vartheta^{-1}}
is the desired vector in
W_{2}
invariant under
K^{\GL_{2}}_{v}
such that
\mathscr{L}(\mathpzc{y}_{1},\mathpzc{y}_{2})\neq 0
.

Case 2
:
v=q
,
D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
modulo its center is compact and
\pi^{D}_{v}
is one-dimensional isomorphic to
\chi_{q}\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{D}
, where
\chi_{q}
is the unramified quadratic character in condition (5) in the choice of
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
at the beginning of §
5.6
, and
\mathrm{Nm}_{D}
denotes the norm of
D
. By our choice of
\chi_{\theta},\chi_{h}
, we have

\pi^{D}_{\theta^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\tau},q}\otimes\chi_{q}\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{D}\cong\tilde{\pi}^{D}_{h_{\tau},q}.

It follows that there exists
\mathscr{T}_{q}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}\left[D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\right]
such that (
7.12.3
) is nonzero. Since
K_{v}
is a normal subgroup of
D^{\times}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
,
\mathscr{T}_{q}\breve{h}_{\tau,q}
is still invariant under
K_{v}
.
∎

Lemma 7.12.2
.

Let
v
be a place of
\mathbb{Q}
non-split in
\mathcal{K}
and
\sigma:\mathcal{K}^{\times}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}
be a character nontrivial on the
\ker\mathrm{Nm}_{\mathcal{K}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
. Let
\pi_{\sigma}
be the dihedral supercuspidal representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
obtained by the theta lift of
\sigma
. (See
[
Sch02
, §1.2]
for the precise definition.) Then the conductor of
\pi_{\sigma}
equals

(7.12.7)

c(\pi_{\sigma})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2\mathrm{cond}(\sigma),&v\text{ inert,}\\
\mathrm{cond}(\sigma)+1,&v\text{ ramified}.\end{array}\right.

by
[
Sch02
, Theorem in §2.3.2]
. Let
W
be the Kirillov model of
\pi_{\sigma}
with respect to the additive character
\mathbf{e}_{v}^{\pm 1}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{v}
. For
w\in W
, define

(7.12.8)

n(w)=\min\left\{n\in\mathbb{Z}:\text{$w$ is fixed by $\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\varpi^{n}_{v}\mathbb{Z}_{v}&1\end{pmatrix}$}\right\}

Let
\vartheta
be a character of
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v}
and
\vartheta^{*}
be its extension to
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}
taking value
1
at
\varpi_{v}
. For each integer
m
, define
w^{m}_{\vartheta}\in W
as

w^{m}_{\vartheta}(x)=\mathds{1}_{\varpi^{m}_{v}\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v}}(x)\,\vartheta^{*}(x).

Suppose that
\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\sigma))>\mathrm{ord}_{v}(\mathrm{cond}(\sigma|_{\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{v}}))
.

(1)

Let
w=\sum\limits_{m=l_{1}}^{l_{2}}c_{m}w^{m}_{\vartheta}
with
c_{l_{2}}\neq 0
. Then
n(w)=n(w^{l_{2}}_{\vartheta})=n(w^{0}_{\vartheta})+l_{2}
.

(2)

We have

\displaystyle n(w^{m}_{\vartheta})

\displaystyle=m+c(\pi_{\sigma}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}m+2\mathrm{cond}(\sigma\cdot\vartheta^{*-1}\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{\mathcal{K}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}),&\text{$v$ inert},\\
m+\mathrm{cond}(\sigma\cdot\vartheta^{*-1}\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{\mathcal{K}_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}})+1,&\text{$v$ ramified}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

(1) It is easy to see that
\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{l}_{v}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\cdot w^{m}_{\vartheta}=w^{m-l}_{\vartheta}
. Thus,
n(w^{m}_{\vartheta})=n(w^{0}_{\vartheta})+m
. It follows that
n(w^{m}_{\vartheta})\leq n(w^{l_{2}}_{\vartheta})
for all
m\leq l_{2}
, so
n(w)\leq n(w^{l_{2}}_{\vartheta})
. On the other hand, if
n(w)\leq n(w^{l_{2}}_{\vartheta})-1
, then
n(w^{l_{2}}_{\vartheta})=n\Big(c^{-1}_{l_{2}}(w-\sum\limits_{l_{1}}^{l_{2}-1}c_{m}w^{m}_{\vartheta})\Big)\leq n(w^{l_{2}}_{\vartheta})-1
. This is a contradiction. Hence,
n(w)=n(w^{l_{2}}_{\vartheta})
.

(2) Let
W^{\prime}
the representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
whose underlying
\mathbb{C}
-vector space equals that of
W
with the action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
defined as the action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
on
W
twisted by
\vartheta\circ\det
,
i.e.
for
g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
and
w\in W^{\prime}
, the action of
g
on
w
gives
\vartheta^{-1}(\det g)(g\cdot w)
, where
g\cdot w
denotes the action of
g
on
w
viewed as an element in
W
. Then
W^{\prime}\cong\pi_{\sigma}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1}
. Suppose that
w^{\prime}\in W^{\prime}
is the new vector, then
\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v}&\mathbb{Z}_{v}\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}
,
d\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{v}
, acts on it by the scalar
\sigma\vartheta^{-2}(d)
, and we have
w^{\prime}=\sum\limits_{m=l_{1}}^{l_{2}}c_{m}w^{m}_{\vartheta}
with
c_{l_{1}}\neq 0
for some
l_{1}\geq 0
. Then

(7.12.9)

n(w^{0}_{\vartheta})\geq c(\pi_{\sigma}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1})=n(w^{\prime})=n(w^{0}_{\vartheta})+l_{2},

where the first inequality and the middle equality follows from the definition of new vectors and conductors of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
-representations, and the right equality follows from (1). (
7.12.9
) implies that
l_{1}=l_{2}=0
,
w^{0}_{\vartheta}
is the new vector in
W^{\prime}
and
n(w^{0}_{\vartheta})=c(\pi_{\sigma}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1})
. Since
n(w^{m}_{\vartheta})=n(w^{0}_{\vartheta})+m
, we see that
n(w^{m}_{\vartheta})=m+c(\pi_{\sigma}\otimes\vartheta^{*-1})
.
∎

8.
Proof of Greenberg–Iwasawa Main Conjecture

8.1.
The Klingen Eisenstein ideal

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so}}=\Hom_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}}\left(\mathpzc{V}^{*}_{\mathrm{so}},\Lambda_{\mathrm{so}}\right)
be the
\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module of semi-ordinary families on
\GU(3,1)
of tame level
K^{p}_{f}
(defined in (
5.6.8
)) as in Theorem
2.9.1
. Identifying
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}\simeq\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}
, we have the map

(8.1.1)

T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathcal{K},p}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},

with kernel
\mu_{p}\times\mu_{p}
. Denote by
V

so
,

ξ

(resp.
M

so
,

ξ

) the sub-
\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
-module of
e_{\mathrm{so}}\mathpzc{V}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so}}
) on which
\mu_{p}\times\mu_{p}
acts through
\xi_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
. We make
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
act on
\mathpzc{V}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}
through its usual action multiplied by the character
\xi^{-1}_{p\text{-}\mathrm{adic}}
. Then this action factors through the image of
T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
in
\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
under the map (
8.1.1
). Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{pVxi}{{{}}\mathpzc{V}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}}\subset\mathpzc{V}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}
,
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Mxi}{{{}}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}
be the cuspidal part. We consider the
\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
-modules

M

so
,

ξ
,
Γ

K

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket,

M

0

so
,

ξ
,
Γ

K

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket.

The Klingen Eisenstein family
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
constructed in §
5.10
belongs to
\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},e_{\mathrm{so}}V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)
. By the natural pairing between
V_{\GU(3,1),\mathrm{so}}
and the space
\mathpzc{V}^{*}_{\mathrm{so}}
in Theorem
2.9.1
, we see that there is a natural map

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},e_{\mathrm{so}}V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\times(\mathpzc{V}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi})^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\simeq\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket,

and it induces a
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
-linear Hecke-equivariant map

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}eas\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}},e_{\mathrm{so}}V_{\GU(3,1),\,\xi}\widehat{\otimes}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{so}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket}\left((\mathpzc{V}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi}^{*},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\right)=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}.

Therefore, we can view
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
as an element in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
. Let
\mathbb{T}
be the abstract algebra generated by the unramified Hecke algebras away from
\Sigma\cup\{p,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
and
\mathbb{U}_{p}
-operators at
p
. Then
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
is an eigen-family for the action of
\mathbb{T}
, and we denote by
\lambda_{\mathrm{Eis},\pi,\xi}:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
the corresponding eigen-system.

Let
𝕋

0

so
,

ξ
,
Γ

K

be the reduced Hecke algebra generated by
\mathbb{T}
acting on
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
. Let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{IEis}{{{}}\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Eis},\pi,\xi}}\subset\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
be the ideal generated by the images of
T-\lambda_{\mathrm{Eis},\pi,\xi}(T)
in
\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
,
T\in\mathbb{T}
. We define the Klingen Eisenstein ideal as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cEpi}{{{}}\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}}=\ker\Big(\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}/\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Eis},\pi,\xi}\Big).}
structure
map

It measures the congruences between the Hecke eigen-systems in
\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
and the Hecke eigen-system
\lambda_{\mathrm{Eis},\pi,\xi}
attached to
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
.

Theorem 8.1.1
.

Let
\varphi
be as in Proposition
7.11.2
. Let
P
be a height one prime ideal of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
such that
P\cap\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}=(0)
. Then

\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}\right)\leq\mathrm{ord}_{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}),

(where
\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\in\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
are the
p
-adic
L
-function satisfying the interpolation property in (
6.1.1
)(
6.1.2
)).

Proof.

By Theorem
2.9.1
, we have the fundamental exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{g\in C(K^{p}_{f})}M_{\GU(2)}\left(K^{p}_{f,g}K_{p,0},\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\longrightarrow 0.

Combining it with Theorem
6.1.1
on the degenerate Fourier–Jacobi coefficients of
\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}
, we deduce that there exists
\bm{E}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
such that

\bm{F}=\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi}-\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\cdot\bm{E}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}.

Let
P
be a height one prime ideal of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
such that

P\cap\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}=(0),\quad\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right)=m\geq 1.

By Proposition
7.11.3
, for
\beta=1
, there exists
g\in\U(2)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{K}})
and
u\in\bigotimes_{v\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{ns}}\cup\{\ell^{\prime}\}}\U(1)(\mathbb{Q}_{v})
such that
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)(g)\notin P
. Then
l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{F}_{\beta,u}\right)(g)\notin P
, and we have the
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}
-linear map

(8.1.2)

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\right)_{P}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}/P^{m}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P},

which sends the image of
T\in\mathbb{T}
in
\left(\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\right)_{P}
to

\frac{l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left((T\cdot\bm{F})_{\beta,u}\right)(g)}{l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{F}_{\beta,u}\right)(g)}\equiv\frac{l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left((T\cdot\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi})_{\beta,u}\right)(g)}{l_{\theta^{J}_{1}}\left(\bm{E}^{\mathrm{Kling}}_{\varphi,\beta,u}\right)(g)}\equiv\lambda_{\mathrm{Eis},\pi,\xi}(T)\mod P^{m}.

We see that (
8.1.2
) factors through
\left(\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\right)_{P}/\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{Eis},\pi,\xi}\left(\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\right)_{P}
. It follows that the composition

(8.1.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\,\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\right)_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}/P^{m}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}.}
structure
map
(
8.1.2
)

factors through
\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}/\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}
. By the definition of (
8.1.2
), we also know that the composition (
8.1.3
) is the natural projection. Hence, we deduce that
\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}\subset P^{m}\hat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{K}}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}
, and

\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}\right)\leq m=\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right).

∎

8.2.
The main theorem

We prove the following partial results towards Conjecture
1.0.3
.

Theorem 8.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{K}
be an imaginary quadratic extension of
\mathbb{Q}
and
p\geq 3
be a prime split in
\mathcal{K}
. Let
\pi
and
\xi
be as in §
5.2
.

(1)

Let
\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\subset\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}
be the rank one
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module on which the complex conjugation acts by
+1
. As fractional ideals of
\mathpzc{R}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket}\mathrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\right)
,

(8.2.1)

\big(\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\big)\supset\mathrm{char}{R}\big(X_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket}\mathpzc{R}\big).

See §
1
for the definition of the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\in\mathrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\right)
, the
\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
-module
X_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
, and the characteristic ideals.

(2)

If
\xi
satisfies
\xi_{\mathfrak{p}}\not\equiv\xi_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}
modulo the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, then

\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket

and if it further satisfies:

–

\xi|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\omega^{2}
with
\omega:\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
the Teichmüller character, and
k_{0}\equiv 0\mod 2(p-1)
,

–

for every finite place of
\mathbb{Q}
non-split in
\mathcal{K}
,
\epsilon_{v}\left(\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\xi_{0}\right)=1
,

–

the conductor of
\xi
is only divisible by primes split in
\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}
,

then

\big(\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\big)\supset\mathrm{char}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}\big(X_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\big)

Proof.

(1) First, relaxing the conditions at
v\in\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
in the definition of
\mathrm{Sel}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
in (
1.0.1
) and
X_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
in (
1.0.1
), we define the
\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
-primitive Selmer group:

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Selpartial}{{{}}\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}}=\ker\left\{H^{1}\big(\mathcal{K},T_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\big)\longrightarrow\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{v}\neq\mathfrak{p}\\
\mathfrak{v}\,\nmid\text{ places in $\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}$}\end{subarray}}H^{1}\big(I_{\mathfrak{v}},T_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket^{*}\big)\right\}.

and

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Xpartial}{{{}}X^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}}:=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi},\,\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right).

We can assume that the module
X_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
is
\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
-torsion (since otherwise its characteristic ideal is defined to be
(0)
and the inclusion (
8.2.1
) automatically holds). By the same argument as in the proof of
[
JSW17
, Theorem 6.1.6]
, which uses the fact that the sizes of the unramified cohomology groups at primes
outside
p
are controlled by the local Euler factors of the
p
-adic
L
-functions (
[
GV00
, Proposition 2.4]
), it suffices to prove the inclusion

\left(\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right)\supset\mathrm{char}{R}\left(X^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket}\mathpzc{R}\right)

as fractional ideals of
\mathpzc{R}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket}\mathrm{Frac}\left(\mathcal{O}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\right)
.

We reduce to show that given a height one prime ideal
P\subset\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
such that
P\cap\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket=(0)
, we have the inequality

(8.2.2)

\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right)\leq\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}}\left(\left(X^{\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right)_{P}\right),

which, by Theorem
8.1.1
, is implied by

(8.2.3)

\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}\right)\leq\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket_{P}}\left(\left(X^{\Sigma\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right)_{P}\right).

We have the following setup.

•

A_{0}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
and
A=\hat{A}_{0,P}
.

•

R_{0}=\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}
, a prime ideal
Q\subset R
such that
Q\cap A_{0}=P
, and
R=\hat{R}_{0,Q}
.

•

G=\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and
H=\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{K})
.

The following data gives us the setup (1)-(5) on
[
Wan20
, page 478]
.

(1)

Let
\nu:H\rightarrow A^{\times}_{0}
be the trivial character.

(2)

\chi=\xi\Psi_{\mathcal{K}}:G\rightarrow A^{\times}_{0}
, with
\chi\not\equiv\chi^{-c}
modulo the maximal ideal of
A_{0}
by the choice of
\xi
,

(3)

\rho:G\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}_{A}(V)
, a Galois representation obtained from the two-dimensional Galois representation
\rho_{\pi}:H\rightarrow\GL(V_{\pi})
with
V=T_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}A
and
T_{\pi}\subset V_{\pi}
a
G
-stable lattice.

(4)

\sigma:G\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}_{R\otimes_{A}F}(M)
, a Galois representation on
M=(R\otimes_{A}\mathrm{Frac}(A))^{4}
obtained as the pseudo-representation associated to
R_{0}
as in
[
SU14
, Proposition 7.2.1]

(5)

I=\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}A_{P}
and
J=\ker(R\rightarrow A/I)
where the map
R\rightarrow A
is defined as the map (
8.1.2
) and is surjective, and
A/I=R/J
.

Moreover, the data satisfies the properties (6)-(9) on
[
Wan20
, page 478]
. Properties (6)-(8) are checked in the same way as
loc.cit
.

Property (9) is about the irreducibility of
\sigma
. It requires that for each
\mathrm{Frac}(A)
-algebra homomorphism
\lambda:R\otimes_{A}\mathrm{Frac(A)}\rightarrow K
,
K
a finite extension of
\mathrm{Frac}(A)
, the representation
\sigma_{\lambda}:G\rightarrow\GL_{4}(K)
obtained from
\sigma
via
\lambda
is either absolutely irreducible or contains an absolutely irreducible two-dimensional sub-representation whose trace is congruent to
\chi+\chi^{-c}
mod
I
. It can be checked as follows. Let
\mathpzc{T}:G\rightarrow A
be the pseudo representation giving rise to
\sigma
. Suppose that
\sigma
does not satisfy (9). Then
\mathpzc{T}=\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}+\delta_{3}
with
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
one-dimensional pseudo-character and
\delta_{3}
a two-dimensional pseudo-representation with irreducible residual representation is
\mathrm{Tr}\bar{\rho}_{\pi}
. Take an arithmetic point
x:\mathbb{T}^{0}_{\mathrm{so},\xi,\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
corresponding to an automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\U(3,1)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by a classical semi-ordinary cuspidal automorphic form fixed by
K^{0}_{p,1}
of weight
(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})
,
0\gg t^{+}\gg-t^{-}
. We can associate a
2
-dimensional (irreducible) Galois representation
\rho_{3,x}:G\rightarrow\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
to the specialization of
\delta_{3}
at
x
. As in
[
SU14
, Theorem 7.5]
, a twist of
\rho_{3,x}
descends to
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
, which we denote by
\rho^{\prime}_{3,x}:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.

If
\pi
is ordinary at
p
, then
\Pi
is ordinary at
p
. By the argument in
[
SU14
, Theorem 7.5]
, we know that
\rho^{\prime}_{3,x}
is modular and
\Pi
is CAP, but the condition on the weights
(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})
excludes this possibility (
[
Har84
, Theorem 2.5.6]
).

If
\pi
is not ordinary at
p
, then
\bar{\rho}_{\pi}|_{G_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}\cong\bar{\rho}_{\pi}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q},p}}
is irreducible by
[
Edi92
]
. Since
\bar{\rho}_{\Pi}\cong\bar{\rho}_{\pi}|_{G}\oplus\text{two characters}
, we deduce that the semi-simplification of
\rho_{\Pi}|_{G_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}
is not a direct sum of four characters. Identify
\U(3,1)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and let
Q\subset\GL_{4}
be a parabolic subgroup with its Levi subgroup isomorphic to
\GL_{2}\times\GL_{1}\times\GL_{1}
. As in the proof of Proposition
3.0.5
, it follows from the theory of Jacquet modules that
\Pi_{p}
is isomorphic to a subquotient of
\Ind^{\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{Q(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\sigma\boxtimes\chi\boxtimes\chi^{\prime}
with
\chi,\chi^{\prime}
characters of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}
and
\sigma
an irreducible admissible representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
containing a nonzero vector fixed by the Iwahori subgroup. Since the semi-simplification of
\rho_{\Pi}|_{G_{\mathcal{K},p}}
is not a direct sum of four characters,
\sigma
is not Steinberg or a twist of Steinberg, and must be unramified. Hence,
\Pi_{p}
is isomorphic to a subquotient of
\Ind^{\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{B(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\chi_{1}\boxtimes\chi_{2}\boxtimes\chi_{3}\boxtimes\chi_{4}
with
\chi_{1},\chi_{2},\chi_{3},\chi_{4}
unramified characters of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{p}
. Let
\alpha_{j}=\chi_{j}(p)
,
1\leq j\leq 4
. We can assume that
\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{1})\leq\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{2})\leq\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{3})\leq\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{4})
. The semi-ordinarity of
\Pi
at
p
implies that

(8.2.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{1})+\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{3})

\displaystyle=-t^{+}+\frac{3}{2},

\displaystyle\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{4})

\displaystyle=t^{-}-\frac{3}{2},

and the integrality of the operator
U^{+}_{p,1}
(defined in (
3.0.7
)) implies that

(8.2.5)

\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{1}),\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{2})\geq-\frac{1}{2}.

Thanks to the condition
0\gg t^{+}\gg-t^{-}
, (
8.2.4
) and (
8.2.5
) imply that
\alpha_{i}\alpha^{-1}_{j}\neq p^{\pm 1}
unless
\{i,j\}=\{1,2\}
and
\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{1})=-\frac{1}{2}
,
\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{2})=\frac{1}{2}
. However,
\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{1})=-\frac{1}{2}
,
\mathrm{val}_{p}(\alpha_{2})=\frac{1}{2}
imply that
\Pi
is ordinary at
p
and this contradicts that
\rho_{\Pi}|_{G_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}
is not a direct sum of four characters. Therefore, we have
\alpha_{i}\alpha^{-1}_{j}\neq p^{\pm 1}
for all
i,j
, so
\Ind^{\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{Q(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\sigma\boxtimes\chi\boxtimes\chi^{\prime}
is irreducible and
\Pi_{p}
is unramified. It follows that
\rho_{\Pi}|_{G_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}}}
is crystalline, so
\rho^{\prime}_{3,x}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q},p}}
is crystalline. Also,
\bar{\rho}^{\prime}_{3,x}\equiv\bar{\rho}_{\pi}
is not induced from a Galois character for
\mathbb{Q}\left(\sqrt{(-1)^{(p-1)/2}p}\right)
, because its restriction to
I_{p}
has semi-simplification
\omega^{i}_{2}\oplus\omega^{pi}_{2}
with
\omega_{2}
the fundamental character of level
2
and
i\equiv 1\mod(p-1)
(since
\rho_{\pi}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q},p}}
is crystalline of weight
(0,1)
), and if
\bar{\pi}
was induced from a Galois character for
\mathbb{Q}\left(\sqrt{(-1)^{(p-1)/2}p}\right)
,
i
must be a multiple of
\frac{p+1}{2}
, which is impossible when
p
is odd. Thus, we can apply
[
Kis09
]
, we deduce that
\rho^{\prime}_{3,x}
is modular. Then
\Pi
is CAP,
i.e.
it has the same system of Hecke eigenvalues as a Klingen-type
Eisenstein series). This is impossible thanks to the condition on the weights
(0,0,t^{+};t^{-})
by
[
Har84
, Theorem 2.5.6]
. Property (9) is verified.

Let

\displaystyle X^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi\circ\mathrm{Nm}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\ker\left\{H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket,\xi\Psi_{\mathcal{K}}\circ\mathrm{Nm}\right)\rightarrow H^{1}\left(G_{\mathcal{K},\mathfrak{p}},\xi\Psi_{\mathcal{K}}\circ\mathrm{Nm}\right)\right\},\,\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right).

With the input (1)-(5) satisfying the properties (6)-(9), applying the lattice construction
[
Wan20
, Proposition 9.2]
, one proves that if
P\subset\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
is a height one prime such that
\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}\right)>0
and
\left(X_{\xi\circ\mathrm{Nm}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\right)_{P}=0
, then the inequality (
8.2.3
) holds. It follows that for all height one prime ideals
P\subset\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
with
P\cap\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket=(0)
, the desired inequality (
8.2.2
) holds.

(The congruence ideal
\mathcal{E}_{\pi,\xi}
is bounded by the product
\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
, and is expected to give a bound for the product of the characteristic ideals of
X^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi\circ\mathrm{Nm}}
and
X^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
. Although, the characteristic ideals of
X^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi\circ\mathrm{Nm}}
is known to be generated by
\mathcal{L}^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\xi,\mathbb{Q}}
by the Iwasawa Main Conjecture proved in
[
MW84
]
, the lattice construction argument in
[
SU14
,
Wan20
]
only proves the desired bound for
\mathrm{ord}_{P}\left(\mathrm{char}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket}\left(X^{\Sigma\,\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\right)\right)
when
\left(X_{\xi\circ\mathrm{Nm}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\right)_{P}=0
. In
[
Wan20
]
, height one primes in
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
do not need to be excluded thanks to Lemma 9.3
loc.cit
and the fact that
\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
is not contained in any height one prime ideal in
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
by
[
Hsi14b
]
. However, in our case with
\pi
fixed instead of moving in a family, we cannot prove an analogue of Lemma 9.3
loc. cit
.)

(2) Let
\sigma_{\xi}
be the irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by a CM form associated to
\xi
, and
\rho_{\sigma_{\xi}}:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be the Galois representation associated to
\sigma_{\xi}
. Then
\rho_{\sigma_{\xi}}\cong\Ind^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathcal{K}}\rho_{\mathcal{K},\xi}
. The condition
\xi_{\mathfrak{p}}\not\equiv\xi_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}
modulo the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
implies that

(
irred
)

\rho_{\sigma_{\xi}}
is residually absolutely irreducible,

(
dist
)

the characters of
\rho_{\sigma_{\xi}}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q},p}}
on the diagonal are distinct.

Let
\bm{g}
be the ordinary
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
-adic CM family passing through the CM form associated to
\xi
. As in
[
Wan20
, §7E]
, one can construct a
p
-adic
L
-function
L

Hida

π
,
K
,
ξ

by taking the product of Hida’s Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions for the family
\bm{g}
and the newform
f\in\pi
and Katz’s
p
-adic
L
-function restricted to the line of interpolating special values at
1
. Note that although Hida’s construction assumes that
\bm{g}
and
\pi
are both ordinary, only the ordinarity of
\bm{g}
is used. By using
[
HT93
, Theorem 7.1, (8.8b)]
and
[
Hid91
, Lemma 5.3(vi)]
(which relates the Petersson norm with the adjoint
L
-value at
1
), one can check that
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{Hida}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
and the specializations of
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{Hida}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
and our
\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
agree at a Zariski dense subsets of the weight space. Hence,
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{Hida}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}=\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
. Then under the conditions (
dist
) and (
dist
), by using the Iwasawa Main conjecture proved in
[
HT93
,
HT94
,
Hid06
]
, one can check that
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{Hida}}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
. Hence,
\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
.

With further conditions that
\xi|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\omega^{2}
and
k_{0}\equiv 0\mod 2(p-1)
and conditions on the epsilon factors and the conductors of
\xi
, one can use the vanishing of the anticyclotomic
\mu
-invariant proved in
[
Hsi14b
]
to deduce that the
\mathcal{L}_{\pi,\mathcal{K},\xi}
is not divisible by any height one prime ideal in
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{L}\llbracket\Gamma^{+}_{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket
. Thus, the inclusion in (1) implies the inclusion in (2).
∎
